论中国传统民居村寨聚落的改造——中国建筑师神圣的历史使命
中文

论中国传统民居村寨聚落的改造——中国建筑师神圣的历史使命

单德启

（原载于《建筑学报》，1992.4）

中国传统民居聚落是传统建筑文化的历史遗存，又是当今众多中国人特别是农民和少数民族地区人民的居住现实，这是传统民居与宫殿、寺庙、园林建筑、祭坛等传统建筑类型最重要的区别；是"人与居住环境"学术研究面临的最重要的一个课题。

极富特色的各地传统民居聚落正在日益受到重视：艺术家们在那里创作了许多乡土田园景色的作品；民俗学者在那里采集了大量色彩斑斓的民风乡情；旅游部门在策划着乡土民俗特色旅游；文物系统在组织测绘记录希望保护，等等。当文化遗产和物质资源一样受到现代化进程重视的此刻，建筑师们却面临着莫大的难题和困惑。

现在保存下来的、形成和完善在数百年前农耕社会的传统民居聚落居住环境模式，和业已迈入社会主义现代化建设中乡民大众的交往、生产和生活需求，毕竟产生了极大的错位；也就是说，八亿农民传统的居住环境和建筑文化正受到现代化经济和文化建设的剧烈冲击。

中国人口的大量增长（其中农村人口增长率高于城市），发展经济与改善生活的迫切要求，给传统民居聚落居住环境带来巨大压力。农村18000万户、8亿多人，1978年统计住房面积为10.2平方米/人，10年后即1988年统计为19平方米/人，新建、扩建了62亿平方米。广大乡村产业结构正在变化，沿海地区、大中城市周边以及交通干道附近乡村工业、手工业和第三产业的发展，使传统民居聚落迅猛演变乃至解体。

国土生态产生了严重危机，森林覆盖率减至12%、耕地减少1000万亩；土地流失和盐碱化严重，水资源和农村传统燃料柴草也愈益匮乏；环境污染在广大农村蔓延。以江西省上饶地区为例：1987年全区人均产值仅250元，但发达的乡村却远不止这个数字，如果在人均350元以上，必然是乡村企业增收使然，乡村工业产值接近和超过传统农业产值，原有民居聚落的环

【传统民居聚落改造的必要性】

传统民居聚落的改造是必要的，原因在于：

1. 居住环境与现代生活需求的错位。传统民居聚落形成于数百年前的农耕社会，其居住环境模式已经无法满足现代乡民的交往、生产和生活需求。

2. 人口增长带来的压力。中国农村人口大量增长，住房需求急剧增加，传统民居聚落的容量已经无法满足。

3. 经济发展带来的变化。乡村产业结构正在变化，乡村工业、手工业和第三产业的发展，使传统民居聚落迅猛演变乃至解体。

4. 生态环境的危机。森林覆盖率减少、耕地减少、土地流失和盐碱化严重、水资源和传统燃料匮乏、环境污染蔓延，传统民居聚落的生态基础已经动摇。

5. 文化遗产保护的需要。传统民居聚落是传统建筑文化的历史遗存，需要在改造中得到保护和传承。

【传统民居聚落改造的原则】

传统民居聚落的改造应该遵循以下原则：

1. 因地制宜，顺应自然。改造应该尊重当地的自然环境和地形地貌，不能强行改造自然。

2. 尊重传统，传承文化。改造应该尊重当地的建筑传统和文化传统，保留具有历史文化价值的建筑和空间。

3. 群众参与，自力更生。改造应该充分调动乡民的积极性，依靠乡民自身的力量来改善居住环境。

4. 以旧更新，节约资源。改造应该充分利用旧料，降低造价，同时保留传统建筑的造型特征。

5. 循序渐进，逐步完善。改造是一个历史过程，不是一代两代人所能毕其功于一役的，应该循序渐进，逐步完善。

6. 整体思维，系统规划。改造应该从整体出发，系统规划，不能只关注单个建筑而忽视整个聚落的环境和文化。

【中国建筑师的历史使命】

传统民居聚落的改造是中国建筑师神圣的历史使命。建筑师应该：

1. 深入乡村，了解乡民的真实需求和生活习惯。

2. 研究传统民居聚落的历史文化价值和建筑艺术价值。

3. 探索适合当地条件的改造模式和方法。

4. 创造具有时代特征又有民族特色和地方特色的新民居。

5. 在改造中保护和传承传统建筑文化。

6. 关注生态环境，实现可持续发展。

启发甚至可以借鉴，但它最宝贵的还是它所体现的中国乡土建筑文化的思想方法和这种思想方法所必然导致的人与居住环境的价值观。这就是"人是唯一的这一价值观是片面的，环境的生态、形态或情态的某一方面有意义也是片面的，而人与自然是和谐的；人与居住环境的相互作用；居住环境的生态、形态和情态的有机统一这样的价值观才是完整的。"（《规划师》，1990年第4期）

引录这些话，是因为笔者认为它对传统民居村寨聚落的改造、改建工程也是重要的。所谓"中国乡土建筑文化的思想方法"，其实就是中国传统文化的精粹"整体思维"或称"系统思维"的意思。传统民居聚落是老祖宗们在族长的主持下，由使用者、风水先生、工匠、邻里乡亲帮忙共同营建的"没有建筑师的建筑"，也就是群体创作；它融合礼仪制度、观察风水、图腾禁忌、风俗习惯、材料结构、施工营建和生活需要于一体，说它体现了"整体思维"，显然是好理解的。

广西融水路家寨业已完成的改建实践，笔者看来是它朝向"整体思维"的方向迈了关键的一步。其一，也是最重要的它调动了广大乡民的积极性，中国农民最讲实际，事情干得成就有积极性；其二，它获得政府各部门和各界的支持，诸如颁布旧木材外运、批准砍伐少量承包林法令等等；其三，它有一个主持改建的企业实体，本想该是建筑工程公司，现在却是水泥总厂属下的公司等等。

从建筑师传统的职业眼光看来，路家寨改建并不很理想，因为它不仅改变了建筑材料和结构、不仅改变了空间模式，还改变了建筑形象、色彩和符号；尽管在聚落、整体景观上，由于在原有宅基地上改建，极少破坏地形地貌和植被，还展现了高低错落，点缀于大自然之间，但毕竟建筑"改得太多了"、"保留得太少了"，而且有些问题如牲畜棚、厨房厕所乃至杂物棚并没有完美解决，由乡民自行在屋前屋后搭建木棚。

可能在路家寨等融水各个改建点的"整体思维"中还缺少一些建筑师的神经细胞，因而，这种实践其实也在呼唤建筑师们加入到它的"整体思维"中来。

传统民居聚落，特别是一些经济和文化不甚发达地区，它的改造、改建必然也是一个历史过程，不是一代、两代人所能毕其功于一役的。新民居聚落必然也是一个由"下里巴人"逐步到"阳春白雪"的过程。初期的不完备、不成熟，理应受到宽容而不宜求全责备。这方面的学术研究必须走出"沙龙"迈向实践，把建筑师的专业训练，把学术研究中的成果和乡民的实践，和地方的民情乡情结合起来。通过实践，保留优秀的、有益或无害的传统乡土建筑文化遗产，创造新颖的有时代特征的又为广大人民乐于接受的新时代的传统建筑文化。即便是"洋"的东西，落地生根开花结果了，也是中国的、地方的乡土文化。

在笔者看来，路家寨民居改建的成果，总体上应该肯定，它毕竟解决了或在一定程度上解决了乡民的实际生活需要；另一方面，它并不十分成熟。试探讨分析如下：

（1）从乡村生态环境来说，一方面新民居依山就势、不占耕地就地改建，保

English

On the Reconstruction of Traditional Chinese dwelling Village Settlements — The Sacred Historical Mission of Chinese Architects

By Shan Deqi

(Originally published in Architectural Journal, 1992.4)

Traditional Chinese dwelling settlements are historical relics of traditional architectural culture, and also the living reality of many Chinese people today, especially farmers and people in ethnic minority regions.

This is the most important difference between traditional dwellings and other traditional architectural types such as palaces, temples, garden architecture, and altars; it is the most important subject faced by academic research on "humans and the living environment."

traditional dwelling settlements with rich local characteristics are increasingly receiving attention: artists have created many works of vernacular pastoral scenery there; folklore scholars have collected a great deal of colorful folk customs; tourism departments are planning vernacular folk-custom特色 tourism; cultural heritage systems are organizing surveying and mapping records hoping for protection, etc.

At this moment when cultural heritage, like material resources, is receiving attention in the modernization process, architects are facing great difficulties and confusion.

The traditional dwelling settlement living environment models preserved today, formed and perfected in agricultural society hundreds of years ago, have after all produced a great dislocation with the communication, production, and living needs of rural masses who have entered socialist modernization construction; that is to say, the traditional living environment and architectural culture of 800 million farmers are being violently impacted by modern economic and cultural construction.

The massive growth of China's population (with rural population growth rate higher than urban), and the urgent demands of economic development and improving living standards, bring enormous pressure to the living environment of traditional dwelling settlements. Rural areas have 180 million households and over 800 million people.

In 1978, the statistical housing area was 10.2 square meters per person; ten years later in 1988, it was 19 square meters per person, with 6.2 billion square meters newly built or expanded. The industrial structure in vast rural areas is changing.

The development of rural industry, handicrafts, and tertiary industry in coastal areas, around large and medium cities, and near major transportation arteries, causes traditional dwelling settlements to evolve rapidly and even disintegrate.

The national territory ecology has produced a serious crisis: forest coverage rate reduced to 12%, cultivated land reduced by 10 million mu; soil erosion and salinization are serious, water resources and traditional rural fuel (firewood) are increasingly scarce; environmental pollution is spreading in vast rural areas.

Taking Shangrao Prefecture in Jiangxi Province as an example: in 1987, the per capita output value of the whole prefecture was only 250 yuan, but developed villages far exceeded this figure; if per capita income was above 350 yuan, it must be due to increased income from rural enterprises, with rural industrial output value approaching and exceeding traditional agricultural output value, and the environment of original dwelling settlements

[Necessity of traditional dwelling Settlement Reconstruction]

The reconstruction of traditional dwelling settlements is necessary for the following reasons:

1. Dislocation between living environment and modern living needs. traditional dwelling settlements were formed in agricultural society hundreds of years ago, and their living environment models can no longer meet the communication, production, and living needs of modern rural people.

2. Pressure from population growth. China's rural population has grown massively, housing demand has increased sharply, and the capacity of traditional dwelling settlements can no longer be met.

3. Changes brought by economic development. The rural industrial structure is changing, and the development of rural industry, handicrafts, and tertiary industry causes traditional dwelling settlements to evolve rapidly and even disintegrate.

4. Crisis of ecological environment. Forest coverage reduction, cultivated land reduction, serious soil erosion and salinization, scarce water resources and traditional fuels, spreading environmental pollution — the ecological foundation of traditional dwelling settlements has been shaken.

5. Need for cultural heritage protection. traditional dwelling settlements are historical relics of traditional architectural culture and need to be protected and inherited in reconstruction.

[Principles of traditional dwelling Settlement Reconstruction]

The reconstruction of traditional dwelling settlements should follow the following principles:

1. Adapting to local conditions, conforming to nature. Reconstruction should respect the local natural environment and topography, and cannot forcibly transform nature.

2. Respecting tradition, inheriting culture. Reconstruction should respect local architectural and cultural traditions, preserving buildings and spaces with historical and cultural value.

3. Mass participation, self-reliance. Reconstruction should fully mobilize villagers' enthusiasm, relying on villagers' own strength to improve the living environment.

4. Renewing with the old, conserving resources. Reconstruction should make full use of old materials to reduce costs while retaining the formal characteristics of traditional architecture.

5. Progressive advancement, gradual improvement. Reconstruction is a historical process, not something that can be accomplished in one or two generations; it should be progressive and gradually improved.

6. Holistic thinking, systematic planning. Reconstruction should proceed from the whole, with systematic planning, and cannot only focus on individual buildings while ignoring the environment and culture of the entire settlement.

[The Historical Mission of Chinese Architects]

The reconstruction of traditional dwelling settlements is the sacred historical mission of Chinese architects. Architects should:

1. Go deep into the countryside to understand villagers' real needs and living habits.

2. Study the historical, cultural, and architectural artistic value of traditional dwelling settlements.

3. Explore reconstruction models and methods suitable for local conditions.

4. Create new dwellings with era characteristics as well as ethnic and local features.

5. Protect and inherit traditional architectural culture in reconstruction.

6. Pay attention to the ecological environment and achieve sustainable development.

inspiration or even reference, but its most precious value is the thought method of Chinese vernacular architectural culture it embodies and the values of humans and living environment inevitably led by this thought method.

This is that "the value view that humans are the only one is one-sided, and that only one aspect of environmental ecology, form, or sentiment has meaning is also one-sided, while humans and nature are harmonious; the interaction between humans and the living environment; the organic unity of the ecology, form, and sentiment of the living environment — such a value view is complete." (planner, No.

4, 1990)

I quote these words because I believe they are also important for the reconstruction and renovation projects of traditional dwelling villages.

The so-called "thought method of Chinese vernacular architectural culture" is actually the essence of traditional Chinese culture — "holistic thinking" or "systems thinking." traditional dwelling settlements were collectively built by ancestors under the leadership of clan chiefs, with users, fengshui masters, craftsmen, and neighbors and relatives helping — "architecture without architects," that is, group creation; it integrates ritual systems, fengshui observation, totem taboos, customs and habits, materials and structures, construction, and living needs into one body.

Saying it embodies "holistic thinking" is obviously easy to understand.

The completed reconstruction practice of Lujiazhai in Rongshui, Guangxi, in my view, has taken a key step toward "holistic thinking." First, and most importantly, it mobilized the enthusiasm of the vast number of villagers — Chinese farmers are most practical, and if things can be done, they have enthusiasm; second, it received support from various government departments and all sectors, such as promulgating laws on old timber transport out, approving limited logging of contracted forests, etc.; third, it has an enterprise entity presiding over the reconstruction — originally supposed to be a construction engineering company, but now it is a company under the cement general factory, etc.

From the traditional professional perspective of architects, the reconstruction of Lujiazhai is not very ideal, because it not only changed building materials and structures, not only changed spatial models, but also changed architectural images, colors, and symbols; although in terms of settlement and overall landscape, due to reconstruction on original homesteads, it rarely damaged topography and vegetation, and still shows high and low staggered arrangement, embellished in nature — after all, the architecture was "changed too much" and "preserved too little," and some problems such as livestock sheds, kitchens and toilets, and even utility sheds were not perfectly solved, with villagers自行 building wooden sheds in front of and behind houses.

Perhaps the "holistic thinking" at various reconstruction points in Rongshui such as Lujiazhai still lacks some architects' nerve cells, and therefore, this practice is actually calling for architects to join its "holistic thinking."

traditional dwelling settlements, especially in some economically and culturally less developed areas, their reconstruction and renovation are inevitably also a historical process, not something that can be accomplished in one or two generations.

New dwelling settlements will inevitably also be a process from "popular" to "refined." The incompleteness and immaturity of the early stage should be tolerated rather than criticized for perfection.

Academic research in this regard must walk out of the "salon" toward practice, combining architects' professional training, academic research results with villagers' practice and local customs.

Through practice, preserve excellent, beneficial or harmless traditional vernacular architectural cultural heritage, and create novel new-era traditional architectural culture with era characteristics that the broad masses are happy to accept.

Even "foreign" things, once they take root and bloom and bear fruit, become Chinese and local vernacular culture.

In my view, the results of the Lujiazhai dwelling reconstruction should generally be affirmed — after all, it solved or to some extent solved villagers' actual living needs; on the other hand, it is not very mature. Let me try to analyze as follows:

(1) From the perspective of rural ecological environment, on one hand, new dwellings follow the mountain terrain, do not occupy cultivated land, and are reconstructed in-situ, preserving

干栏的时空轨迹——广西融水苗寨木楼改建的思辨
中文

干栏的时空轨迹——广西融水苗寨木楼改建的思辨

单德启

（"中日传统民居学术研讨会"论文，1992.11）

【溯源——干栏的兴起】

由上古"构木为巢"演进而成的干栏式穿斗架木楼民居，曾经广泛流行于长江流域以南的中国大片国土上，它为考古学、民族学发掘研究的大量遗迹、出土文物和典籍记载，以及今天遗存下来的南方各地传统民居所印证。

最早见于文字的是三国时代吴人沈莹《临海水土记》，该文称"安家之民，悉依深山，架立屋舍于栈格上似楼状。"所谓"安家"即今浙南、闽北一带古越人。而最早区分中国南、北方不同民居模式的是晋人张华，他在《博物志》卷三中提出"南越巢居，北朔穴居，避寒暑也。"至于首记"干栏"名称，则推北齐人魏收的《魏书·僚传》："僚者、盖南蛮之别种，自汉中达于邛、笮、川洞之间，所在皆有，""依树积木，以居其上，名曰干栏。干栏大小，随其家口之数。

"唐、宋以后，记载益多，描述亦较详尽，如《旧唐书·南蛮西南蛮传》、《通典·边防三》、《文献通考·四裔考》、《桂海虞衡志》、《岭外代答·风土》、《太平寰宇记》等等。值得注意的是，明代晚期徐霞客亲自考察广西隆安一带的干栏民居，记曰："土人俱架屋为栏，下畜牛豕，上爨与卧处之所托焉，架高五六尺，以巨竹捶开，径尺余，架与壁俱用之。爨以方板三、四尺铺于竹架之中，置灰热火，以块石支锅，而炊锅之上三四尺，悬一竹筐，日炙稻而春。

"（《徐霞客游记·粤西游日记三》）徐公所记，非常相同于今天我们在桂、黔等地传承下来的干栏楼居的室内外状况了。纵观历代记载，"干栏"名称亦多，如"干阑"、"杆栏"、"麻栏"、"葛栏"、"阁栏"，以至"巢居"、"楼居"、"竹楼"、"栏房"、"棚屋"等等；其流行地域遍及两湖、苏皖、浙闽、两广、琼台以至西南的云贵川。中国民族史的研究，把这一地区先民统称为"百越"，细分亦有"于越"、"山越"、"闽越"、"骆越"等等，并以"干栏"式住屋文化以为百越重要特征。当然，先民中的羌、楚人亦有居于栏者。（图1）

考古的发现，提供了实物的论证。最重要的发现是位于今浙江余姚的河姆渡文化，其遗址发掘有成片大面积长方形干栏式建筑遗迹，大量带有卯榫的木构件，说明干栏穿斗木构架技艺的发达；它和出土的石锛、石斧、手制灰黑陶器以及稻作碳粒等，都表明这一地区早在新石器时代就有原始社会相当先进的文明。浙江吴兴钱山漾、江苏丹阳香草河、吴江梅堰、湖北圻春毛家嘴、云南剑川川门口等新石器时代遗址文化，都发现有干栏建筑遗迹或木柱遗物。

河姆渡文化及其邻近的稍晚的马家滨（浙江嘉兴）文化，说明至迟五六千年以前，江浙一带就完成了天然巢居向干栏楼居的发展。此外，云南晋宁石寨山出土的干栏小铜器、四川成都地区出土的商代干栏遗迹、广东广州市郊皇帝岗、广西贵县汶井岭、湖南长沙等地汉墓中的干栏陶屋明器，也都证明中国南部和西南部先后陆续完善了干栏楼居的居住模式。

尤其值得重视的是：建筑遗迹考古表明，一定模式的民居是与当时人类生产和生活等各个领域的文明相联系的，如稻作，如造舟，如棺葬，如生产工具和生活用具等。凡干栏木楼流行，其自然地理条件则是湿热多雨的山地、丘陵、河汉之地，其生态资源则是林木茂密，其生产方式则由采集狩猎发展到农耕稻作，其技术水平则已创造普及了相对先进的伐木、加工工具和技艺。

前面提及的各个干栏遗迹的地方，无不同时出土了大量的石器工具，如铸、斧、犁等；领先发达的吴越地区，至迟在春秋时就普遍采用青铜器铲、锄、镰；闽越、南越地区秦汉之际就有大量青铜器铸、矛、斧、田、凿等，两广汉墓甚至已有铁斧和铁凿，古代建筑，尤其是民居实物遗存极少，文字记载亦若雾中花，这些生产工具为我们考证干栏木楼流行的时空提供了有力的佐证。

穿斗木构架与卯榫技艺的发展（图2），首先是出于当时人们生产和生活需要和适应自然能力的提高，因为穿斗木构架对室内空间提供了灵活分割的可能，对木楼营建在复杂地段也提供了方便。干栏楼居的兴起，也说明了先民在自觉不自觉的营建活动中，实际上进行着包括从自然到社会，从生产到生活，从建筑材料结构到空间形象，从最平凡的劳动到最深层的理念的一项整体思维和综合功利的认识和实践。

【展望——干栏的更替】

最先而又最深刻地感受到干栏木楼"危机"并寻求新的走向的，是生活居住在干栏木楼里的广大乡民。湘西、桂北、黔东南地区，试图摆脱"全身是木"居住模式，创造新民居的尝试，所在皆有。广西融水苗族自治县民房改建是一个较为突出的例子。这个杉木产量名列全国第二的山区县，1987年就广泛开展了干栏木楼改建。融水县人口迅速增长，林木资源锐减，火灾日益频繁，干栏木楼难以为继，笔者在融水曾耳闻目睹苗胞迫切要求改造旧木楼的强烈愿望。拙作《中国传统民居聚落的改造》对此已有论述和介绍。

笔者认为，人类文明发展进步的一个重要标志就是不断调整自然资源的使用结构。从随手使用原生材料到更多地使用加工过的劳动和技术密集型材料，从而充分合理地利用材料的物理、力学性能，节约材料。未来的年代，像木材、土壤、水等自然资源的紧缺将有增无减。作为亿万乡民居住要求的民居发展趋向，将直接影响到自然资源和生态环境。一部传统民居的发展、演变史，某种意义上说也就是乡土建筑材料不断更替、优化和节约的历史，如干栏代替巢居、砖瓦代替木材和生土、钢筋水泥代替黏土砖和木材等等。

"穴居文化"、"干栏文化"、"红砖文化"、"四合院文化"等也随之而创造与发展。中国传统民居任何一种模式的文化内涵及其表象特征，都是与其实际生活需要和可能的材料结构等物质条件共生的，它尤其是民居的特点，也是优点。

清华大学建筑学院与融水县民房改建工程公司合作，冀图在以往融水苗寨木楼改建的基础上进行新的探索。1991年，选择全县特贫村整垛寨现场调查，群众参与，做了规划和单体设计；由于拆卸旧楼木料代售折价，由于充分利用瓦顶旧料，以及组织民工就地取砂石制作水泥砖，造价降低为65元/m²，使这个人均收入仅200多元的30户人家村寨改建得以实现。整垛寨与以往融水县改建的民房相比，保留了坡屋顶、配置了半开敞楼梯间，不仅降低了造价，而且在空间形象上也有所改进（图5、图6、图7、图8）。

此后，我们又合作完成了稍富余的田头寨的改建设计。田头寨人均收入近400元，其单体设计比整垛寨提高了造价，预计80-90元/m²，因而能在保留干栏木楼建筑文化上传承更多

【干栏更替的思辨】

我们的探索业已证明：在干栏木楼改建中，某些传统民居构成因素（如木柱支撑和木壁板、木楼梯等），因与现代生活和营建的材料结构非常矛盾，勉强保留或以新材料结构加以仿造，必然过于虚假造作而失去魅力，也并非民居的建筑本质；某些构成因素（如挑廊、披檐乃至屋顶木构架）则可以加以改进保留；至于墙体的色彩、质感，即使面目全非，那也顺理成章。传承至今的、我们仍可见到的传统民居模式，如四合院、如干栏木楼、如徽州天井民居、如窑洞民居等等，也是在历史上某一时期演变、创造并逐步完善而成的，并非亘古就有。

因此，干栏的更替，包括我们在融水民房改建的探索，并非消极措施和权宜之计。现代的又是传统的新时代乡土民居创造，尽管它或许仅仅是雏形，或许很不成熟，比起那捉襟见肘的所谓保护，比起那用现代新材料新结构勉强地重复旧模式，终究是有生命力的。

传统民居本来就是一项综合的社会系统工程，它融礼仪制度、观察风水、图腾禁忌、风俗习惯、材料结构、施工营建和生活需要于一体；中国民居最宝贵的，也是最值得保留、发扬的，并非仅仅是某种空间形象，某些"符号"或装饰，而是蕴藏在这项系统工程之中的整体思维和综合功利的思想方法与价值观，现存中国民居的双重性也应该重视和强调，否则也会失误。所谓双重性，就是它既是传统建筑、特别是乡土建筑文化的历史遗存，从这个意义上看它带有历史文物的性质；然而它更是几亿乡民的居住现实，是人们基本的生活条件。

少量的、多种方式的（包括民俗文化村这种展示性的复制）保存是必要的，而大量地更新改造更需要引起我们的重视和兴趣；因为我们不能仅仅"消费"传统，我们更要"生产"和创造传统；因为传统民居的历史，也不会在今天就止步不前。

English

The Spatiotemporal Trajectory of ganlan — Reflections on the Reconstruction of Miao Wooden stilt houses in Rongshui, Guangxi

By Shan Deqi

(Paper presented at "Sino-Japanese traditional dwellings Academic Symposium", 1992.11)

[Tracing the Origins — The Rise of ganlan]

The ganlan-style through-column wooden frame dwellings, evolved from the ancient "building trees as nests," were once widely prevalent across vast areas of China south of the Yangtze River basin.

They are confirmed by大量 relics excavated in archaeological and ethnological research, unearthed artifacts and classical records, as well as traditional dwellings surviving today in various southern regions.

The earliest written record is from the Three Kingdoms era Wu native Shen Ying's Linhai Tushui Ji, which states: "The people of Anjia all live deep in the mountains, building houses on stacked frames like towers." The so-called "Anjia" refers to the ancient Yue people in today's southern Zhejiang and northern Fujian.

The earliest distinction between different residential models in northern and southern China was by Jin dynasty native Zhang Hua, who in his Bowuzhi (Records of Diverse Matters) Volume 3 proposed: "The southern Yue live in nests, the northern Shuo live in caves, to avoid cold and heat." As for the first record of the name "ganlan," it is attributed to Northern Qi native Wei Shou's Weishu · Liao Zhuan (Book of Wei · Biography of the Liao): "The Liao are a separate branch of the southern barbarians, from Hanzhong to Qiong, Ze, and river valleys, found everywhere," "building with trees on trees to live on, called ganlan.

The size of ganlan follows the number of family members." After the Tang and Song dynasties, records increased and descriptions became more detailed, such as in Jiu Tangshu · Nanman Xinanman Zhuan, Tongdian · Bianfang San, Wenxian Tongkao · Siyi Kao, Guihai Yuheng Zhi, Lingwai Daida · Fengtu, Taiping Huanyu Ji, etc.

Notably, in the late Ming dynasty, Xu Xiake personally investigated ganlan dwellings in the Long'an area of Guangxi, recording: "Natives all build houses as lan, raising cattle and pigs below, with cooking and sleeping places above; the frame is five to six chi high, using giant bamboo split open, over a chi in diameter, used for both frame and walls.

Cooking uses square boards of three or four chi laid on bamboo frames, with gray hot fire, supporting pots with stones, and above the pot three or four chi hangs a bamboo basket, daily roasting rice for pounding." (Xu Xiake Youji · Yuexi Youji San) What Xu recorded is very similar to the indoor and outdoor conditions of ganlan tower dwellings inherited today in Guangxi and Guizhou.

Looking at historical records, "ganlan" has many names, such as "ganlan," "ganlan," "maganlan," "gelan," "gelan," even "nest dwelling," "tower dwelling," "bamboo house," "lan house," "shed house," etc.; its流行 region covers Hunan, Hubei, Jiangsu, Anhui, Zhejiang, Fujian, Guangdong, Guangxi, Hainan, Taiwan, and even Yunnan, Guizhou, Sichuan in the southwest.

Research in Chinese ethnic history collectively calls the ancestors of this region "Baiyue" (Hundred Yue), with subdivisions such as "Yuyue," "Shanyue," "Minyue," "Luoyue," etc., and regards ganlan-style housing culture as an important feature of the Baiyue. Of course, the ancient Qiang and Chu peoples also lived in lan-style dwellings. (Fig. 1)

Archaeological discoveries provide physical evidence. The most important discovery is the Hemudu culture located in today's Yuyao, Zhejiang.

Its site excavated large areas of rectangular ganlan architectural remains, with大量 wooden components with mortise-and-tenon joints, demonstrating the sophistication of ganlan through-column wooden frame technology; together with unearthed stone adzes, stone axes, hand-made gray-black pottery, and carbonized rice grains, it all indicates that this region had quite advanced primitive society civilization as early as the Neolithic age.

Neolithic site cultures such as Qianshanyang in Wuxing, Zhejiang; Xiangcaohe in Danyang, Jiangsu; Meiyan in Wujiang; Maojiazui in Qichun, Hubei; and Chuankou in Jianchuan, Yunnan, all discovered ganlan architectural remains or wooden pillar relics.

The Hemudu culture and the slightly later Majiabang (Jiaxing, Zhejiang) culture indicate that as early as five to six thousand years ago, the Jiangsu-Zhejiang region had completed the development from natural nest dwelling to ganlan tower dwelling.

In addition, small ganlan bronze vessels unearthed at Shizhaishan in Jinning, Yunnan; Shang dynasty ganlan remains unearthed in the Chengdu region, Sichuan; ganlan pottery house models from Han tombs at Huangdigang on the outskirts of Guangzhou, Guangdong; Wenjingling in Guixian, Guangxi; and Changsha, Hunan — all prove that southern and southwestern China successively perfected the ganlan tower dwelling residential model.

Particularly noteworthy: architectural relic archaeology shows that a certain model of dwelling is connected with civilization in all fields of human production and life at that time, such as rice cultivation, boat building, coffin burial, production tools and daily utensils.

Wherever ganlan wooden houses were prevalent, the natural geographical conditions were hot, humid, and rainy mountains, hills, and riverine areas; the ecological resources were dense forests; the production mode developed from gathering and hunting to agricultural rice cultivation; and the technical level had created and popularized relatively advanced logging, processing tools and techniques.

The places with ganlan remains mentioned above all simultaneously unearthed大量 stone tools, such as adzes, axes, plows, etc.; the advanced Wu-Yue region, as early as the Spring and Autumn period, generally used bronze shovels, hoes, and sickles; in the Minyue and Nanyue regions, by the Qin-Han transition there were大量 bronze adzes, spears, axes, field tools, chisels, etc., and Han tombs in Guangdong and Guangxi even had iron axes and iron chisels.

Ancient architecture, especially dwelling physical remains, is extremely rare, and written records are like flowers in fog; these production tools provide strong evidence for our research on the时空 prevalence of ganlan wooden houses.

The development of through-column wooden frames and mortise-and-tenon technology (Fig.

2) was first出于 the needs of people's production and life at that time and the improvement of ability to adapt to nature, because through-column wooden frames provided the possibility of flexible division of interior space, and also facilitated the construction of wooden houses on complex terrain.

The rise of ganlan tower dwellings also shows that ancestors, in their conscious or unconscious construction activities, actually carried out a holistic thinking and comprehensive utilitarian understanding and practice — from nature to society, from production to life, from building material structure to spatial image, from the most ordinary labor to the deepest concepts.

[Outlook — The Replacement of ganlan]

The first and most profound to feel the "crisis" of ganlan wooden houses and seek a new direction were the vast villagers living in ganlan wooden houses. In western Hunan, northern Guangxi, and southeastern Guizhou, attempts to break away from the "all-wood" residential model and create new dwellings are everywhere.

The housing reconstruction in Rongshui Miao Autonomous County, Guangxi, is a prominent example. This mountainous county, whose fir production ranks second in the country, widely launched ganlan wooden house reconstruction as early as 1987.

Rongshui's population grew rapidly, forest resources sharply decreased, fires became increasingly frequent, and ganlan wooden houses became unsustainable. The author personally heard and saw the strong desire of Miao compatriots to transform old wooden houses in Rongshui.

My previous work "On the Reconstruction of Traditional Chinese dwelling Village Settlements" has discussed and introduced this.

The author believes that an important标志 of the development and progress of human civilization is the continuous adjustment of the use structure of natural resources.

From casually using raw materials to more using processed labor-intensive and technology-intensive materials, thereby fully and rationally utilizing the physical and mechanical properties of materials and conserving materials. In the coming years, the shortage of natural resources such as wood, soil, and water will continue to increase.

As the residential requirement for hundreds of millions of villagers, the development trend of dwellings will directly affect natural resources and the ecological environment.

A history of the development and evolution of traditional dwellings is, in a sense, a history of the continuous replacement, optimization, and conservation of vernacular building materials — such as ganlan replacing nest dwelling, bricks and tiles replacing wood and raw earth, reinforced concrete replacing clay bricks and wood, etc.

"Cave dwelling culture," "ganlan culture," "red brick culture," "siheyuan culture," etc., were随之 created and developed.

The cultural connotation and superficial characteristics of any model of traditional Chinese dwellings are symbiotic with their actual living needs and possible material conditions such as materials and structures — this is especially the feature and advantage of dwellings.

The School of Architecture of Tsinghua University and the Rongshui County Housing Reconstruction Engineering Company cooperated, hoping to conduct new exploration based on previous Miao wooden house reconstruction in Rongshui.

In 1991, we selected Zhengduo Village, the poorest village in the county, for on-site investigation, with mass participation, and made planning and individual building designs; due to dismantling old building wood for sale at a discount, due to full use of old tile roof materials, and due to organizing migrant workers to locally obtain sand and gravel to make cement bricks, the construction cost was reduced to 65 yuan/m², enabling the reconstruction of this village of 30 households with per capita income of only over 200 yuan.

Compared with previously reconstructed houses in Rongshui County, Zhengduo Village retained sloped roofs and configured semi-open stairwells, not only reducing construction costs but also improving spatial image (Figs. 5, 6, 7, 8).

Thereafter, we cooperated to complete the reconstruction design of the relatively wealthier Tiantou Village. Tiantou Village has per capita income of nearly 400 yuan, and its individual building design increased the cost compared to Zhengduo Village,预计 80-90 yuan/m², thus able to inherit more in preserving ganlan wooden house architectural culture

[Reflections on ganlan Replacement]

Our exploration has proven: in ganlan wooden house reconstruction, certain constituent factors of traditional dwellings (such as wooden pillar supports and wooden wall panels, wooden stairs, etc.), due to great contradiction with modern life and construction material structures,勉强 preserving or imitating with new material structures would inevitably be too false and artificial, losing charm, and is not the architectural essence of dwellings; certain constituent factors (such as projecting corridors, extended eaves, and even roof wooden frames) can be improved and preserved; as for wall color and texture, even if completely changed, that is only natural.

traditional dwelling models inherited today and still visible to us, such as siheyuan, ganlan wooden houses, Huizhou patio dwellings, cave dwellings, etc., also evolved, were created, and gradually perfected in a certain historical period, not existing since ancient times.

Therefore, the replacement of ganlan, including our exploration in Rongshui housing reconstruction, is not a passive measure or makeshift.

The creation of modern yet traditional new-era vernacular dwellings, although perhaps merely in embryonic form, perhaps very immature, is ultimately生命力 compared to the so-called protection that is stretched thin, compared to勉强 repeating old models with modern new materials and new structures.

traditional dwellings are inherently a comprehensive social systems engineering, integrating ritual systems, fengshui observation, totem taboos, customs and habits, material structures, construction, and living needs into one body; the most precious thing about Chinese dwellings, and the most worth preserving and carrying forward, is not merely a certain spatial image, certain "symbols" or decorations, but the holistic thinking and comprehensive utilitarian thought methods and values embedded in this systems engineering.

The dual nature of existing Chinese dwellings should also be emphasized and stressed, otherwise mistakes will occur.

The so-called dual nature means it is both a historical relic of traditional architecture, especially vernacular architectural culture — in this sense it has the nature of historical relics; yet it is even more the living reality of hundreds of millions of villagers, people's basic living conditions.

A small amount of preservation in various ways (including display-type replication such as folk culture villages) is necessary, but large-scale renewal and reconstruction deserves more attention and interest; because we cannot merely "consume" tradition, we must "produce" and create tradition; because the history of traditional dwellings will not stop progressing today.

欠发达地区传统民居聚落改造的求索——广西融水苗寨木楼改建的实践和理论探讨
中文

欠发达地区传统民居聚落改造的求索——广西融水苗寨木楼改建的实践和理论探讨

单德启

（原载于《建筑学报》，1993.4）

【融水苗寨木楼改建顺时势、得民心】

融水苗族自治县安太乡整垛寨这个深山之中的贫困苗寨，1992年12月10日是一个难忘的日子，苗胞放鞭炮、贴门联，寨门口摆下迎客的拦路酒，芦笙坪上跳起了踩堂舞，他们在欢庆民房改建成功、喜迁新居（图1）。31户苗胞住上了二层水泥空心砖混结构、青瓦坡顶的新楼，告别了世世代代人畜混居、阴暗潮湿、受火灾严重威胁的破旧木楼。寨门、芦笙坪和芦笙柱、道路、植树绿化被乡民整理一新，既保留了少数民族地区特色，又洋溢着现代新农村的生机。

全村用上了电和自来水，家家户户有了新畜棚和使用节柴灶的新厨房，其中11户还用上了沼气。包括附近两个寨子的孩子们再也不会在昏暗拥挤的木楼里上课了，他们有了安全、明亮、宽敞的教学楼。无论是老人还是青年，都发自内心向来访的客人表达旧貌换新颜的喜悦。一户新楼的门联

【改建的实践和经验】

整垛寨、田头屯寨这种就地改建、以旧更新、群众参与的改建模式，符合欠发达地区改建的经济承受力和实际情况。据整垛寨最早迁入使用的13户调查统计，对安全防火、采光通风隔热、经济承受力等项全部表示满意，对使用面积、牲畜棚布置等也大部分表示满意。充分发动群众参与改建，对顺利实现改建，对在贫困山区引进商品和市场观念、生态环境观念以及形成讲文明、讲卫生、讲科学的新风尚都有积极的作用。

墙局部露明穿斗架、二层小披檐挑廊木吊柱以及将沿坡改建户的底层半架空作为储藏间等。从正在施工的效果来看还比较理想。（图5、图6）

整垛寨、田头屯寨这种就地改建、以旧更新、群众参与的改建模式，符合欠发达地区改建的经济承受力和实际情况。据整垛寨最早迁入使用的13户调查统计，对安全防火、采光通风隔热、经济承受力等项全部表示满意，对使用面积、牲畜棚布置等也大部分表示满意。充分发动群众参与改建，对顺利实现改建，对在贫困山区引进商品和市场观念、生态环境观念以及形成讲文明、讲卫生、讲科学的新风尚都有积极的作用。

【协同努力，整体思维，改建和开发相结合】

参加了传统民居聚落改造这一社会系统工程的实践，笔者深刻地体会到：传统民居首先是一个经济现象，它的产生和发展、矛盾和冲突、更新和改造，无不与经济发展水平、生产方式、流通和消费息息相通，量大面广、就地取材、经

传统民居聚落的改造是一项复杂的社会系统工程，需要协同努力，整体思维，改建和开发相结合。具体来说：

1. 政府引导与群众参与相结合。政府应该制定政策，提供支持，引导改建工作；同时要充分发动群众参与，依靠乡民自身的力量来改善居住环境。

2. 就地改建与以旧更新相结合。改建应该在原有宅基地上进行，不占耕地；同时要充分利用旧料，降低造价，保留传统建筑的造型特征。

3. 物质改造与精神文明建设相结合。改建不仅要改善物质居住条件，还要促进精神文明建设，形成讲文明、讲卫生、讲科学的新风尚。

4. 改建与开发相结合。改建应该与经济开发相结合，通过改建促进当地经济发展，通过经济发展支持改建工作。

5. 整体规划与分步实施相结合。改建应该有整体规划，但要分步实施，循序渐进，逐步完善。

【理论探讨】

欠发达地区传统民居聚落改造的理论探讨：

1. 传统民居的双重性。传统民居既是历史文物，又是居住现实。对于欠发达地区来说，改善居住条件是首要任务，不能为了保护而保护。

2. 改建的必要性和紧迫性。欠发达地区的传统民居往往存在安全隐患、卫生条件差、设施不完善等问题，改建是必要的和紧迫的。

3. 改建的可行性。通过就地改建、以旧更新、群众参与等方式，欠发达地区的传统民居改建是可行的。

4. 改建的原则。改建应该遵循因地制宜、尊重传统、群众参与、以旧更新、循序渐进、整体思维等原则。

5. 改建的意义。改建不仅改善了乡民的居住条件，还促进了经济发展、社会进步和文化传承。

欠发达地区传统民居聚落改造是一个长期的历史过程，需要政府、建筑师、乡民和社会各界的共同努力。通过实践探索和理论探讨，我们一定能够找到适合欠发达地区的传统民居聚落改造之路，创造出具有时代特征又有民族特色和地方特色的新民居。

English

Exploration of traditional dwelling Settlement Reconstruction in Underdeveloped Areas — Practice and Theoretical Discussion of Miao Wooden stilt house Reconstruction in Rongshui, Guangxi

By Shan Deqi

(Originally published in Architectural Journal, 1993.4)

[Rongshui Miao wooden house Reconstruction Conforms to the Trend and Wins Popular Support]

December 10, 1992 was an unforgettable day for Zhengduo Village, a poor Miao village deep in the mountains of Antai Township, Rongshui Miao Autonomous County.

Miao compatriots set off firecrackers, pasted door couplets, placed welcoming road-blocking wine at the village gate, and danced the Caitang dance on the Lusheng Square — they were celebrating the successful housing reconstruction and moving happily into new homes (Fig. 1).

Thirty-one Miao households moved into two-story new buildings of cement hollow-brick mixed structure with blue-tile sloped roofs, bidding farewell to the dilapidated old wooden houses where humans and livestock lived together for generations, which were dark, damp, and seriously threatened by fire.

The village gate, Lusheng Square and Lusheng pillar, roads, and tree planting and greening were renovated by villagers, preserving ethnic minority characteristics while brimming with the vitality of a modern new countryside.

The whole village had electricity and tap water, every household had new livestock sheds and new kitchens using fuel-saving stoves, and 11 households even used biogas. Children from the nearby two villages no longer had classes in dark and crowded wooden houses — they had safe, bright, and spacious teaching buildings.

Whether elderly or young, all sincerely expressed to visiting guests the joy of the old appearance giving way to the new. The door couplet of one new building

[Practice and Experience of Reconstruction]

The reconstruction model of in-situ reconstruction, renewing with the old, and mass participation used in Zhengduo Village and Tiantou Village conforms to the economic affordability and actual conditions of reconstruction in underdeveloped areas.

According to a survey of the first 13 households that moved into use in Zhengduo Village, all expressed satisfaction with safety and fire prevention, lighting, ventilation and heat insulation, and economic affordability; most also expressed satisfaction with usable area and livestock shed layout.

Fully mobilizing the masses to participate in reconstruction has positive effects on smoothly实现 reconstruction, introducing commodity and market concepts, ecological environment concepts in poor mountainous areas, and forming new customs of emphasizing civilization, hygiene, and science.

Walls partially expose through-column frames, second-story small extended-eave projecting corridors with wooden hanging pillars, and the semi-raised ground floors of slope-side reconstructed households used as storage rooms, etc. From the effects under construction, it appears relatively ideal. (Figs. 5, 6)

The reconstruction model of in-situ reconstruction, renewing with the old, and mass participation used in Zhengduo Village and Tiantou Village conforms to the economic affordability and actual conditions of reconstruction in underdeveloped areas.

According to a survey of the first 13 households that moved into use in Zhengduo Village, all expressed satisfaction with safety and fire prevention, lighting, ventilation and heat insulation, and economic affordability; most also expressed satisfaction with usable area and livestock shed layout.

Fully mobilizing the masses to participate in reconstruction has positive effects on smoothly实现 reconstruction, introducing commodity and market concepts, ecological environment concepts in poor mountainous areas, and forming new customs of emphasizing civilization, hygiene, and science.

[Coordinated Efforts, Holistic Thinking, Combining Reconstruction and Development]

Having participated in the practice of traditional dwelling settlement reconstruction — this social systems engineering — the author deeply realizes: traditional dwellings are first and foremost an economic phenomenon; their occurrence and development, contradictions and conflicts, renewal and reconstruction are all closely linked to economic development level, production mode, circulation and consumption.

With large quantity and wide coverage, using local materials, economical

The reconstruction of traditional dwelling settlements is a complex social systems engineering requiring coordinated efforts, holistic thinking, and combining reconstruction with development. Specifically:

1. Combining government guidance with mass participation. The government should formulate policies, provide support, and guide reconstruction work; at the same time, fully mobilize the masses to participate, relying on villagers' own strength to improve the living environment.

2. Combining in-situ reconstruction with renewing with the old. Reconstruction should be carried out on original homesteads without occupying cultivated land; at the same time, make full use of old materials to reduce costs and preserve the formal characteristics of traditional architecture.

3. Combining material transformation with spiritual civilization construction. Reconstruction should not only improve material living conditions but also promote spiritual civilization construction, forming new customs of emphasizing civilization, hygiene, and science.

4. Combining reconstruction with development. Reconstruction should be combined with economic development, promoting local economic development through reconstruction, and supporting reconstruction through economic development.

5. Combining overall planning with phased implementation. Reconstruction should have overall planning but be implemented in phases, progressing step by step and gradually improving.

[Theoretical Discussion]

Theoretical discussion on traditional dwelling settlement reconstruction in underdeveloped areas:

1. The dual nature of traditional dwellings. traditional dwellings are both historical relics and living reality. For underdeveloped areas, improving living conditions is the primary task, and protection cannot be for the sake of protection.

2. The necessity and urgency of reconstruction. traditional dwellings in underdeveloped areas often have problems such as safety hazards, poor sanitary conditions, and inadequate facilities; reconstruction is necessary and urgent.

3. The feasibility of reconstruction. Through in-situ reconstruction, renewing with the old, mass participation, and other methods, traditional dwelling reconstruction in underdeveloped areas is feasible.

4. Principles of reconstruction. Reconstruction should follow principles such as adapting to local conditions, respecting tradition, mass participation, renewing with the old, progressive advancement, and holistic thinking.

5. Significance of reconstruction. Reconstruction not only improves villagers' living conditions but also promotes economic development, social progress, and cultural inheritance.

traditional dwelling settlement reconstruction in underdeveloped areas is a long historical process requiring the joint efforts of the government, architects, villagers, and all sectors of society.

Through practical exploration and theoretical discussion, we will surely find a path for traditional dwelling settlement reconstruction suitable for underdeveloped areas, creating new dwellings with era characteristics as well as ethnic and local features.

绍兴东浦水街的保护与更新
中文

绍兴东浦水街的保护与更新

单德启 姚红梅 芮文萱

（"第八届中国传统建筑园林学术研讨会"论文，1995.10）

【小集镇 大视野】

处于新经济"热地"长江三角洲的绍兴小镇东浦，近年来不仅经济持续发展，而且注重城镇建设，保护了几尽灭绝的传统水街，又提出"保护地方特色，抓好老街修复"，这是因为他们突破了短期和短视行为，放眼未来，立足长远……

东浦，位于历史文化名城绍兴市西郊，东连城区，西与中国轻纺城（柯桥）相接，镇域面积30.7平方公里，总人口3.99万（图1）。

东浦，历史悠久，人杰地灵，是南宋伟大爱国诗人陆游和辛亥革命先驱徐锡麟的故居。东浦又是典型的江南集镇，境内河道纵横，湖泊星布，具有"水乡"、"桥乡"之称。水佳则酒醇，当地名产绍兴黄酒，饮誉海内外，也为东浦赢得"酒乡"之美誉。东浦堪称经济强镇。改革开放以来，东浦跳出了传统的经济模式，先后兴办了五大行业、175家乡镇企业，并有"三资"企业17家，出口创汇企业18家。

20世纪80年代跨入"亿元镇"和浙江省"工业卫星镇"，1992年被省政府授予"综合经济实力百强乡镇"，1994年又被授予绍兴市"明星镇"荣誉称号。1994年出口贸易值达3.5亿元，初步形成了以农业为基础，纺织为主导，酿酒为特色，门类齐全、结构合理的乡镇工业格局。与此同时，东浦的农业也是全市的一面旗帜，保持了2.3万亩耕地，粮食生产实现了11年亩产超"吨粮"，"优高"农业发展迅速，并建成了年产商品鳖40万只、全省规模最大的特种水产养殖场。1994年全镇人均收入2958元。

全镇绿化覆盖率达到25%，人均绿地面积2平方米，1995年又被授予浙江省"绿色小城镇"称号。

近几年来，东浦在经济建设及社会各项事业得到飞速发展的同时，城镇建设出现了崭新面貌，老区面貌得到保护，新区区域不断扩大，基础设施日益完善，

【东浦水街的特色】

东浦水街虽然仅600米长，但对其进行适当的保护与更新却并非易事，需要深入地调查研究。首先我们必须弄清其特色：水陆相间的水街格局；小桥、流水、人家的城镇景观；淡雅、朴素的古镇民居；幽深曲折的街巷、水楼；丰富的地方特色文化，尤其是酒文化；浓郁的民俗、民风、民情等均是其特色所在，亦是我们要保护的。但保护却是"动态的"而非"静止的"，"多元的"而非"单一的"。

谈到"多元"，它具有多方面的含义：

- 保护价值的多元性——包括情感价值、文化价值、使用价值；

- 保护内容的多元性——包括生态（自然环境）、形态（人工物质环境）、情态（人文环境）；

- 保护方式的多元性——包括原状保护、移植保护、基因保护。

基于上述分析，我们在对东浦水街规划时，将其划分为三个区，即西区、中区、东区（图3）。

(1)西区（民俗生活展示区）

该区因传统民居保存较好，所以以居住用地为主，作为"民俗生活展示区"。在庙潭江的西北角，将少量民宅和棉纺厂的部分建筑拆除。设置小沈园、乃翁亭及绿化小品，以纪念伟大爱国诗人陆游。原卫生院迁出，改为文化站用地。

(2)中区（酒肆百业展销区）

该区在历史上即为"商贾辐辏"、"船舶云集"的集市区，商业用地较多。改建后作为"酒肆百业展销区"，保留传统的商贸格局，增设一些酒家及为旅游服务的休息用地，将原来的锻压厂及一些小作坊迁出。

(3)东区（酒文化展示区）

该区水域宽阔，居住用地相对少些，而仓库、厂房较多，建筑密度也不及西区与中区。改造后，作为"酒文化展示区"，将工厂、仓库迁出，增设为旅游服务的宾馆、酒店、茶庄、书法阁、名人字画展、酒文化陈列、酿酒工艺参观、水上戏台等等公共用地。

【景点设计】

东浦水街的景点设计别具一格，充分体现酒乡东浦的特色（图4）。

- 花雕探幽：酒文化街大门以别具匠心的一个花雕酒坛的框，做成大门形状，后衬以照壁，上书"东浦镇酒文化街"，从正面看酒字正在酒坛中心，造成一种"若想进水街，先入酒坛来"的气氛（图5）。

- 水乡唱舟：此景位于酒文化街两端船坞码头售票处，透过售票处可以看到水乡特有的乌篷船（图6）。

- 怡然如也：此景位于民俗生活展示区（西区），反映水乡人家的生活情调（图7）。

- 情满沈园：东浦是伟大爱国诗人陆游的故乡，而陆游与唐婉的爱情故事更是为后人称颂。为了纪念他们，在庙桥西侧设置乃翁亭（源自陆游的诗《示儿》中的"王师北定中原日，家祭勿忘告乃翁"）、小沈园、陆游纪念堂，游人从步行入口进入水街，首先映入眼帘的就是这组建筑（图8）。

- 桥头飘香：此景位于庙桥东侧，"醉乡宁

【保护与更新的启示】

绍兴东浦水街的保护与更新给我们以下启示：

1. 经济发展与文化保护可以并行不悖。东浦在经济飞速发展的同时，注重保护传统水街，实现了经济发展与文化保护的双赢。

2. 保护应该是动态的、多元的，而不是静止的、单一的。保护不仅包括物质环境，还包括人文环境；不仅包括原状保护，还包括移植保护和基因保护。

3. 保护与更新应该结合当地的特色文化。东浦水街的保护与更新充分利用了当地的酒文化、陆游文化等特色文化资源，使保护与更新具有了丰富的文化内涵。

4. 保护与更新应该注重功能的转换。东浦水街将传统的商贸功能转换为旅游服务功能，使传统水街在现代社会中焕发了新的生机。

5. 保护与更新需要政府、建筑师和群众的共同努力。东浦水街的保护与更新是在政府的引导下，由建筑师和群众共同参与完成的。

绍兴东浦水街的保护与更新是一个成功的案例，它为中国传统集镇的保护与更新提供了有益的经验和借鉴。

English

protection and renewal of Dongpu water street in Shaoxing

By Shan Deqi, Yao Hongmei, Rui Wenxuan

(Paper presented at "8th Chinese traditional architecture and Gardens Academic Symposium", 1995.10)

[small town, Broad Vision]

Dongpu, a small town in Shaoxing located in the "hot spot" of the new economy — the Yangtze River Delta — has not only achieved sustained economic development in recent years, but also paid attention to town construction, protected the nearly extinct traditional water street, and proposed "protecting local characteristics and doing a good job in old street restoration." This is because they broke through short-term and short-sighted behavior, looking to the future and taking a long-term perspective...

Dongpu is located in the western suburbs of Shaoxing, a famous historical and cultural city, connecting the urban area to the east and adjacent to China Light Textile City (Keqiao) to the west. The town covers an area of 30.7 square kilometers with a total population of 39,900 (Fig. 1).

Dongpu has a long history and outstanding people. It is the former residence of Lu You, the great patriotic poet of the Southern Song dynasty, and Xu Xilin, a pioneer of the 1911 Revolution.

Dongpu is also a typical Jiangnan (southern Yangtze) market town, with crisscrossing rivers and scattered lakes within its territory, known as a "water town" and "bridge town." Good water makes good wine; the local famous product Shaoxing yellow rice wine is renowned at home and abroad, earning Dongpu the reputation of "wine town." Dongpu can be called an economically strong town.

Since the reform and opening up, Dongpu has跳出 the traditional economic model, successively establishing five major industries and 175 township enterprises, with 17 "three-capital" enterprises and 18 export-oriented enterprises.

In the 1980s, it entered the ranks of "100-million-yuan towns" and Zhejiang Province's "industrial satellite towns." In 1992, it was awarded "Top 100 Towns in Comprehensive Economic Strength" by the provincial government, and in 1994, it was awarded the honorary title of "Star Town" by Shaoxing City.

In 1994, the export trade value reached 350 million yuan, initially forming a township industrial pattern with agriculture as the foundation, textiles as the leading industry, wine-making as the feature, with complete categories and reasonable structure.

At the same time, Dongpu's agriculture is also a banner in the city, maintaining 23,000 mu of cultivated land, achieving 11 consecutive years of grain output exceeding "one ton per mu," with rapid development of "excellent and high-yield" agriculture, and building the province's largest special aquatic product farm with an annual output of 400,000 commercial soft-shelled turtles.

In 1994, the per capita income of the town was 2,958 yuan. The town's green coverage rate reached 25%, with 2 square meters of green space per capita. In 1995, it was awarded the title of "Green small town" by Zhejiang Province.

In recent years, while Dongpu's economic construction and various social undertakings have developed rapidly, town construction has taken on a brand-new look, the old district's appearance has been protected, the new district area has continuously expanded, and infrastructure has become increasingly完善,

[Characteristics of Dongpu water street]

Although Dongpu water street is only 600 meters long, its proper protection and renewal is by no means easy and requires in-depth investigation and research.

First, we must clarify its characteristics: the water-land alternating water street pattern; the townscape of small bridges, flowing water, and households; elegant and simple ancient town dwellings; deep and winding streets and water buildings; rich local characteristic culture, especially wine culture; and strong folk customs, folk traditions, and folk sentiments — all are its characteristics and what we need to protect.

But protection is "dynamic" rather than "static," "pluralistic" rather than "singular."

Speaking of "pluralistic," it has multiple meanings:

- Pluralism of protection values — including emotional value, cultural value, and use value;

- Pluralism of protection content — including ecology (natural environment), form (artificial material environment), and sentiment (humanistic environment);

- Pluralism of protection methods — including original-state protection, transplantation protection, and gene protection.

based on the above analysis, in our planning of Dongpu water street, we divided it into three zones: the western zone, central zone, and eastern zone (Fig. 3).

(1) Western Zone (Folk Life Display Zone)

Because traditional dwellings are well preserved in this zone, it is mainly residential land, serving as the "Folk Life Display Zone." At the northwest corner of Miaotan River, a small number of residences and part of the cotton spinning factory buildings were demolished.

A small Shen Garden, Naiweng Pavilion, and greening ornaments were set up to commemorate the great patriotic poet Lu You. The original health center was relocated and converted to cultural station land.

(2) Central Zone (Wine shops and Various Trades Exhibition Zone)

This zone was historically a market area with "gathering merchants" and "gathering boats," with more commercial land. After reconstruction, it serves as the "Wine shops and Various Trades Exhibition Zone," retaining the traditional commercial pattern, adding some wine shops and rest areas for tourism services, and relocating the original forging factory and some small workshops.

(3) Eastern Zone (Wine Culture Display Zone)

This zone has broad water areas, relatively less residential land, but more warehouses and factories, with building density lower than the western and central zones.

After reconstruction, as the "Wine Culture Display Zone," factories and warehouses are relocated, adding public land for tourism services such as hotels, restaurants, tea houses, calligraphy pavilions, celebrity calligraphy and painting exhibitions, wine culture displays, wine-making工艺 visits, water stages, etc.

[Scenic Spot Design]

The scenic spot design of Dongpu water street is unique, fully reflecting the characteristics of Dongpu as a wine town (Fig. 4).

- Huadiao Exploration: The gate of the wine culture street uses an ingenious frame of a Huadiao wine jar to form the gate shape, with a screen wall behind it inscribed with "Dongpu Town Wine Culture Street." From the front, the character "wine" is right at the center of the wine jar, creating an atmosphere of "if you want to enter the water street, first enter the wine jar" (Fig. 5).

- water town Singing Boat: This scene is located at the dock ticket offices at both ends of the wine culture street. Through the ticket office, one can see the unique black-awning boats of the water town (Fig. 6).

- Contentment: This scene is located in the Folk Life Display Zone (western zone), reflecting the lifestyle of water town households (Fig. 7).

- Shen Garden Full of Emotion: Dongpu is the hometown of the great patriotic poet Lu You, and the love story between Lu You and Tang Wan is even more praised by later generations.

To commemorate them, west of Miao bridge, Naiweng Pavilion (derived from Lu You's poem "示儿" — "When the king's army recovers the Central Plains on the north, do not forget to tell your father in the family sacrifice"), Small Shen Garden, and Lu You Memorial Hall are set up.

When visitors enter the water street from the pedestrian entrance, the first thing that catches their eye is this group of buildings (Fig. 8).

- Fragrance at the bridgehead: This scene is located east of Miao bridge, "Drunk Town Peace

[Enlightenment from protection and renewal]

The protection and renewal of Dongpu water street in Shaoxing gives us the following enlightenment:

1. Economic development and cultural protection can go hand in hand. While achieving rapid economic development, Dongpu paid attention to protecting the traditional water street, achieving a win-win situation of economic development and cultural protection.

2. protection should be dynamic and pluralistic, rather than static and singular. protection includes not only the material environment but also the humanistic environment; it includes not only original-state protection but also transplantation protection and gene protection.

3. protection and renewal should combine local characteristic culture. The protection and renewal of Dongpu water street fully utilized local characteristic cultural resources such as wine culture and Lu You culture, giving protection and renewal rich cultural connotations.

4. protection and renewal should pay attention to functional transformation. Dongpu water street transformed the traditional commercial function into a tourism service function, giving the traditional water street new vitality in modern society.

5. protection and renewal require the joint efforts of the government, architects, and the masses. The protection and renewal of Dongpu water street were completed under the guidance of the government, with the joint participation of architects and the masses.

The protection and renewal of Dongpu water street in Shaoxing is a successful case, providing useful experience and reference for the protection and renewal of traditional Chinese market towns.

绍兴柯桥古镇"有机秩序"的建立
中文

绍兴柯桥古镇"有机秩序"的建立

单德启 卢强

（"香港·中国民居学术研讨会"论文，1997.8）

柯桥镇位于浙江省绍兴市，为绍兴县第一大镇，因经济发达、物产丰富、贸易兴隆，历史上被誉为"金柯桥"。它距绍兴市区12公里，古运河自西向东穿境而过，杭甬铁路、104国道、杭甬高速公路、杭温公路东西向并列横贯全境；镇中河网交错，古桥众多；镇内主街临河，商店多依河分布，民宅多傍水而建。传统居民区被河道分隔，又由石桥相连，构成了浓郁的小桥、流水、人家的江南水乡特色，已被建设部命名为"历史文化名镇"（图1、图2）。柯桥在解放前是一个典型的农贸集散地，每遇集市，人群拥满街头，乌篷船等停泊在河岸两旁，撑杆如林。

人们曾用"上有枫桥千条扁担，下有柯桥千支撑杆"分别形容当年地处山区的诸暨县枫桥镇和地处水乡的柯桥镇的盛况。改革开放后，乡镇企业迅速成长，集镇规模也与

【柯桥古镇的有机秩序】

柯桥古镇的"有机秩序"体现在以下几个方面：

1. 自然与人工的有机结合。柯桥古镇依水而建，河道纵横，古桥众多，建筑与自然环境有机结合，形成了"小桥、流水、人家"的江南水乡特色。

2. 功能与形式的有机统一。柯桥古镇的建筑既满足了居住、商业、交通等功能需求，又具有优美的形式和丰富的文化内涵。

3. 个体与整体的有机协调。柯桥古镇的每一栋建筑虽然各具特色，但整体上协调统一，形成了有机的整体。

4. 传统与现代的有机衔接。柯桥古镇在发展过程中，既保留了传统的格局和风貌，又适应了现代生活的需要，实现了传统与现代的有机衔接。

【旅游开发规划】

绍兴柯桥水乡古镇旅游开发规划（图7）将古镇划分为多个功能区：

- 古镇游览区：保留传统格局和风貌，展示江南水乡特色。

- 文化展示区：展示柯桥的历史文化和民俗风情。

- 商业服务区：为游客提供餐饮、购物、住宿等服务。

- 休闲度假区：为游客提供休闲度假的场所。

规划注重保护古镇的有机秩序，在保护的基础上进行合理的开发和利用，实现保护与开发的良性循环。

【有机秩序建立的启示】

绍兴柯桥古镇"有机秩序"的建立给我们以下启示：

1. 古镇的保护应该注重有机秩序的保护，而不仅仅是单体建筑的保护。

2. 古镇的开发应该在保护有机秩序的基础上进行，不能破坏古镇的整体格局和风貌。

3. 古镇的发展应该实现传统与现代的有机衔接，既保留传统特色，又适应现代生活需要。

4. 古镇的规划应该注重功能与形式的有机统一，个体与整体的有机协调。

5. 古镇的建设应该尊重自然、顺应自然，实现人与自然的和谐统一。

绍兴柯桥古镇"有机秩序"的建立是中国传统集镇保护与发展的成功案例，它为中国历史文化名镇的保护与发展提供了有益的经验和借鉴。

English

The Establishment of "Organic Order" in Keqiao Ancient Town, Shaoxing

By Shan Deqi, Lu Qiang

(Paper presented at "Hong Kong · Chinese dwellings Academic Symposium", 1997.8)

Keqiao Town is located in Shaoxing City, Zhejiang Province, and is the largest town in Shaoxing County. Due to its developed economy, abundant products, and prosperous trade, it was historically known as "Golden Keqiao." It is 12 kilometers from Shaoxing urban area, with the ancient Grand Canal passing through from west to east.

The Hangzhou-Ningbo Railway, National Highway 104, Hangzhou-Ningbo Expressway, and Hangzhou-Wenzhou Highway run parallel across the territory from east to west; within the town, river networks crisscross and ancient bridges are numerous; the main street in the town faces the river, shops are mostly distributed along the river, and residences are mostly built by the water.

Traditional residential areas are separated by rivers and connected by stone bridges, forming a strong Jiangnan (southern Yangtze) water town characteristic of small bridges, flowing water, and households. It has been named a "Historical and Cultural Town" by the Ministry of construction (Figs. 1, 2).

Before liberation, Keqiao was a typical agricultural trade distribution center. On market days, crowds filled the streets, and black-awning boats docked on both sides of the river banks, with poles like a forest.

People once used "above there are Fengqiao's thousand carrying poles, below there are Keqiao's thousand boat poles" to respectively describe the grand occasions of Fengqiao Town in Zhuji County located in the mountainous area and Keqiao Town located in the water town in those years.

After the reform and opening up, township enterprises grew rapidly, and the scale of the town also

[The Organic Order of Keqiao Ancient Town]

The "organic order" of Keqiao Ancient Town is embodied in the following aspects:

1. Organic combination of nature and artifice. Keqiao Ancient Town is built by the water, with crisscrossing rivers and numerous ancient bridges. Architecture and the natural environment are organically combined, forming the Jiangnan water town characteristic of "small bridges, flowing water, and households."

2. Organic unity of function and form. The architecture of Keqiao Ancient Town not only meets the functional needs of residence, commerce, and transportation, but also has beautiful forms and rich cultural connotations.

3. Organic coordination of individual and whole. Although each building in Keqiao Ancient Town has its own characteristics, they are coordinated and unified as a whole, forming an organic entirety.

4. Organic connection of tradition and modernity. In the development process, Keqiao Ancient Town has retained the traditional pattern and style while adapting to the needs of modern life, achieving an organic connection between tradition and modernity.

[Tourism Development planning]

The tourism development plan for Keqiao water town Ancient Town (Fig. 7) divides the ancient town into multiple functional zones:

- Ancient Town Tour Zone: retaining the traditional pattern and style, showcasing Jiangnan water town characteristics.

- Cultural Display Zone: showcasing Keqiao's historical culture and folk customs.

- Commercial Service Zone: providing dining, shopping, accommodation, and other services for tourists.

- Leisure and Vacation Zone: providing places for tourists to relax and vacation.

The plan focuses on protecting the organic order of the ancient town, carrying out reasonable development and utilization on the basis of protection, and achieving a virtuous cycle of protection and development.

[Enlightenment from the Establishment of Organic Order]

The establishment of "organic order" in Keqiao Ancient Town, Shaoxing gives us the following enlightenment:

1. The protection of ancient towns should focus on the protection of organic order, not just the protection of individual buildings.

2. The development of ancient towns should be carried out on the basis of protecting organic order, and cannot destroy the overall pattern and style of the ancient town.

3. The development of ancient towns should achieve an organic connection between tradition and modernity, retaining traditional characteristics while adapting to modern living needs.

4. The planning of ancient towns should focus on the organic unity of function and form, and the organic coordination of individual and whole.

5. The construction of ancient towns should respect nature and conform to nature, achieving harmonious unity between humans and nature.

The establishment of "organic order" in Keqiao Ancient Town, Shaoxing is a successful case of the protection and development of traditional Chinese market towns, providing useful experience and reference for the protection and development of Chinese historical and cultural towns.

本篇术语 Key Terms

"城市化"+"逆城市化"=有中国特色的现代化
中文

"城市化"+"逆城市化"=有中国特色的现代化

单德启

（原载于《特区与港澳经济》，1997.12）

关于"城市化"，提法很多，如"城市化进程"、"农村城市化"、"城乡一体化"等等；争论也不少，我们不必拘泥于概念，其实际内涵就是一个国家伴随着经济发展，其城市的规模、数量、发达程度也必然发展。

改革开放以来，我国经济发展速度很快，城市化的速度也很快。有各种统计数字，如1992年城市化达到32.33%，如预测到20世纪末达到35%，这些都是按城市人口比例测算的，不管精确与否，城市人口膨胀，经济持续高速增长，产业结构和社会结构的变化，这是不容怀疑的。

所有这一切，给我们国家带来了巨大的变化，带来了生机和希望。从建筑专业角度来讲，微观看时紧时松，宏观看始终是热门，可见一斑。

【"逆城市化"现象】

这里提到的"逆城市化"，似乎在唱反调，其实不然。

"逆城市化"现象，在我国已不是一种个别的、偶然的现象：

——广东珠江三角洲一带，要求"城转农"的人口不断增多；汕头出现"城中村"，众多已被征地的农民不想转为城市居民，宁愿留在自家的土地上，据说15.4万人中竟有8.3万人之多。

——类似的情况在浙东、苏南等地也有。因为农村收入和城里一般工作收入差不多，甚至还高，而农村人均40多平米的住宅、良好的环境是有很大吸引力的。

——北京建城集团，在离市区28公里的地方开发了"建城花园"，他们公然提出"逆城市化是城市发展的趋势"，提出"先建美园，再建美城"，希望掀起

市人口已降到31%，"已进入郊区时代"，同样现象在中国不是不可能出现。惠州的房地产开发片面追求高容积率，甚至不顾防火规范，中国城市学会的专家指出："这样搞下去十年就要毁掉一个城市"，虽然言之过激，但也不能说是"杞人忧天"。

【对城市化的疑问和反思】

疑问之三：建筑师谁都会说老子的那一段话，要旨就是"空间"。我们要空间，为什么我们辛勤劳动的后果是城市空间越来越恶劣，国土空间越来越恶劣？我想重温一下吴良镛先生在《广义建筑学》中的话是必要的："自从近代科学技术日益发达后……建筑学观念也越来越窄了，甚至只热衷于立面的艺术造型。"他还指出："传统建筑学与城市研究相割裂，这是一个重大的萎缩和缺陷，至今影响尚在。"……所以建城房地产公司有水平，他们认为现在的消费者绝不是只要水泥钢筋冷冰冰的住房，而要一个有人情味的都市的田园和田园的都市。

或许有人反诘：中国城市化进程还差得很远，"逆城市化"还不到火候。我认为：这正是问题的症结。中国这样一个人口"超级"大国，从千年传统农业社会向现代化发展，是否应该、而且是否可能沿相同或相似的模式走西方发达国家走过的城市化道路。想起了某辛格博士在北京"21世纪论坛"中对记者讲的一段话："中国这样一个拥有十多亿人口的大国在发展，这本身就是一个具有重大历史意义的事实，这意味着世界经济领域的许多规则不能完全适用于它。"

我想，某辛格这句话至少可以使我们怀疑一下，不仅我们的"建筑观"是否还没有脱离传统建筑学的影响，而且我们的所谓"现代化观"是否又落入一个"舶来品"模式之中。

【德国经验的借鉴】

我曾在德国汉诺威大学与建筑系的教授们探讨过这一问题，村镇研究所的教授们对"城市化"也有类似的思想。就我有限的所见所闻，在"现代化"、"城市化"了的德国，就连生存空间极为有限的荷兰也不追求密集的钢筋混凝土，印象最深的是：

——花园城市，生态城市，绿化城市，小城市；

——大郊区，生态村；

——传统村落激活了"生命力"，既保护了乡土环境又提高了现代化水平。德国艾斯拓夫村为此还获得了"欧洲传统村落保护与更新奖"；

——新建筑、新结构材料与本土风貌、传统建筑协调、共生；

——从对城市总体的把握来"创造"单栋建筑的个性，这种"总体"表现在城市的生态环境、城市的交通、城市的文脉。

【传统民居聚落保护、改造和更新的思路】

在这之前，我就考虑到传统民居聚落保护、改造和更新的背景——"城市化"和"逆城市化"，访欧之后更加坚定了这条思路。初步考虑是：

——农业产业化以及产业结构和社会结构调整后新民居村落的建设；传统

"城市化"+"逆城市化"=有中国特色的现代化，这是作者提出的一个重要观点。中国的现代化不能照搬西方的模式，而应该走自己的道路。在城市化的同时，也要注重"逆城市化"的发展，实现城乡协调发展。

传统民居聚落的保护、改造和更新，应该在"城市化"和"逆城市化"的大背景下进行。既要适应城市化的发展趋势，又要保留乡村的特色和优势；既要改善乡民的居住条件，又要保护传统的建筑文化和生态环境。

有中国特色的现代化，应该是城市和乡村共同发展、共同繁荣的现代化，应该是人与自然和谐统一的现代化，应该是传统与现代有机结合的现代化。

English

"urbanization" + "Counter-urbanization" = Modernization with Chinese Characteristics

By Shan Deqi

(Originally published in Special Zone and Hong Kong-Macao Economy, 1997.12)

Regarding "urbanization," there are many formulations, such as "urbanization process," "rural urbanization," "urban-rural integration," etc.; there are also many debates. We need not stick to concepts; its actual connotation is that as a country develops economically, the scale, quantity, and development level of its cities will inevitably develop.

Since the reform and opening up, China's economic development has been very fast, and the speed of urbanization has also been very fast. There are various statistics, such as urbanization reaching 32.33% in 1992, and predictions of reaching 35% by the end of the 20th century. These are all calculated based on the proportion of urban population.

Regardless of accuracy, urban population expansion, sustained rapid economic growth, and changes in industrial and social structures are beyond doubt.

All of this has brought tremendous changes to our country, bringing vitality and hope. From an architectural professional perspective, at the micro level it is sometimes tight and sometimes loose, but at the macro level it has always been a hot topic — this gives a glimpse.

[The Counter-urbanization Phenomenon]

The "counter-urbanization" mentioned here seems to be singing a different tune, but actually it is not.

The "counter-urbanization" phenomenon in China is no longer an individual or accidental phenomenon:

— In the Pearl River Delta region of Guangdong, the population requesting "urban-to-rural conversion" is continuously increasing; Shantou has出现 "urban villages," where many land-expropriated farmers do not want to become urban residents, preferring to stay on their own land — reportedly 83,000 out of 154,000 people.

— Similar situations exist in eastern Zhejiang and southern Jiangsu. Because rural income is similar to or even higher than average urban work income, and rural per capita housing of over 40 square meters with a good environment is very attractive.

— Beijing Jiancheng Group developed "Jiancheng Garden" 28 kilometers from the urban area. They openly proposed "counter-urbanization is the trend of urban development," and proposed "build beautiful gardens first, then build beautiful cities," hoping to set off

urban population has dropped to 31%, "having entered the suburban era." The same phenomenon is not impossible in China. Real estate development in Huizhou one-sidedly pursues high floor area ratios, even ignoring fire safety regulations.

Experts from the Chinese Urban Society pointed out: "Continuing like this will destroy a city in ten years." Although the words are extreme, it cannot be said to be "unfounded worry."

[Questions and Reflections on urbanization]

Question Three: Every architect can recite Laozi's passage, whose essence is "space." We want space, so why is the result of our hard work that urban space is getting worse and worse, and national territory space is getting worse and worse? I think it is necessary to revisit Mr.

Wu Liangyong's words in Generalized Architecture: "Since modern science and technology became increasingly developed...

architectural concepts have become narrower and narrower, even only keen on the artistic modeling of facades." He also pointed out: "The割裂 between traditional architecture and urban research is a major atrophy and defect, whose influence still exists today."...

Therefore, Jiancheng Real Estate Company has vision — they believe today's consumers are by no means satisfied with cold cement and steel housing, but want a city with human touch in the countryside and countryside in the city. Perhaps someone retorts: China's urbanization process is still far behind, and "counter-urbanization" is not yet timely.

I believe: this is precisely the crux of the problem. China, such a "super" populous country, developing from a thousand-year traditional agricultural society to modernization — should it, and is it possible to, follow the same or similar model of the urbanization path taken by developed Western countries? I recall a passage Dr.

Singer said to reporters at the "21st Century Forum" in Beijing: "China, such a large country with over one billion people developing, is itself a fact of great historical significance, which means many rules in the world economic field cannot be fully applied to it."

I think Dr. Singer's words at least make us doubt — not only whether our "architectural view" has not yet摆脱 the influence of traditional architecture, but also whether our so-called "modernization view" has fallen into a "imported" model.

[Learning from German Experience]

I once discussed this issue with professors from the Department of Architecture at the University of Hannover in Germany.

Professors at the Village and Town Research Institute had similar thoughts on "urbanization." From my limited observations, in "modernized" and "urbanized" Germany, even the Netherlands with extremely limited living space does not pursue dense reinforced concrete. What impressed me most was:

— Garden cities, ecological cities, green cities, small cities;

— Large suburbs, ecological villages;

— Traditional villages activated with "vitality," both protecting the vernacular environment and improving the level of modernization. The German village of Estorf won the "European Traditional Village protection and renewal Award" for this;

— New buildings, new structural materials coordinated and symbiotic with local style and traditional architecture;

— "Creating" the individuality of individual buildings from the grasp of the overall city — this "overall" manifests in the city's ecological environment, urban transportation, and urban context.

[Ideas for the protection, transformation, and renewal of traditional dwelling Settlements]

Before this, I had considered the background for the protection, transformation, and renewal of traditional dwelling settlements — "urbanization" and "counter-urbanization" — and after visiting Europe, I became more firm in this line of thinking. Initial considerations are:

— construction of new dwelling villages after agricultural industrialization and adjustment of industrial and social structures; traditional

"urbanization" + "counter-urbanization" = modernization with Chinese characteristics — this is an important viewpoint proposed by the author. China's modernization cannot copy Western models but should follow its own path. While urbanizing, attention should also be paid to the development of "counter-urbanization," achieving coordinated urban-rural development.

The protection, transformation, and renewal of traditional dwelling settlements should be carried out under the broad background of "urbanization" and "counter-urbanization." It is necessary to both adapt to the development trend of urbanization and retain rural characteristics and advantages; both improve villagers' living conditions and protect traditional architectural culture and the ecological environment.

Modernization with Chinese characteristics should be a modernization where cities and villages develop and prosper together, a modernization of harmonious unity between humans and nature, and a modernization of organic combination between tradition and modernity.
