第三篇 城市化背景下传统聚落和小城镇建设

Part II: Xiping and Surrounding Villages
1
城市化和农业产业化背景下传统村镇和街区的结构更新
中文

城市化和农业产业化背景下传统村镇和街区的结构更新

单德启 赵若焱

（《国家自然科学基金项目结题报告（部分）》，2003.2）

【"现代化传统"和"当代乡土"村镇居住环境规划和建筑设计】

本项目研究的主要成果是完成了安徽省芜湖市两个重点小城镇的规划设计。

随着经济的发展，目前城市化加速的趋势已从东南沿海发达地区向中部地区延伸，中部地区正在经历着这一剧烈的变革。以中部地区中等城市为研究对象，既可以借鉴东南部发达地区的经验，又可以指导西部不发达地区未来的发展，起到承前启后的作用。芜湖市位于安徽省东南部，与长江毗邻，该市历史上素以运输、商贸发达著称，被称为"长江巨埠，皖之中坚"。随着1993年城市北部国家级经济开发区的建立与发展，以及2000年9月芜湖长江大桥的建成通车，给芜湖带来前所未有的发展机遇，呈现出城市化加速的态势。周边大部分村镇都受到城市发展的直接影响，处于向城市集聚的过程中，迫切需要把自身发展与城市的发展结合起来，矛盾与问题都更加集中。

项目一：安徽省繁昌县三山镇总体规划（2000~2020年）

该项目从2000年7月开始，2000年11月结束。在总体规划中，项目组深入基层调研，多次与镇级、县级及市级领导交流，以提高规划的可操作性。该项目已通过审批，进入实施阶段，执行情况良好，获得省级小城镇规划设计奖。

国家强调"小城镇，大战略"的政策给小城镇发展带来新的契机，三山镇作为省级小城镇建设试点镇、重点镇、中心建制镇，芜湖市五个卫星镇之一，在城市经济发展和城市化背景下，要求与芜湖的整体发展结合起来。

三山镇位于芜湖市和繁昌县之间，为繁昌北大门，距芜湖市西南19公里，距繁昌县城20公里，总面积40.5平方公里，芜(湖)铜(陵)公路(S321省道)横贯境内。三山镇辖13个村委会和一个街道居委会，总人口28787人，镇区人口约

1万人(其中常住人口8千人)，占总人口的34.7%。近两年全镇先后完成商品房开发约7万余平方米，80%以上的家庭通过新建、改建住上了楼房，人均住房面积28平方米。建成区面积1.5平方公里，集镇道路初步形成格局。

三山镇地处一级城镇发展轴上，由于交通便利具有明显的区位优势，一直是周边乡镇的商业购物中心和农副产品集散地，每年吸引全国各地大批蔬菜批发商来此经营蔬菜及农副产品的批发兼零售。但是由于城镇人口规模偏小，三山镇城镇基础设施和社会服务设施不够完善，乡镇企业规模小，产业特色不突出，产业结构不甚合理，缺乏与中心城市芜湖互补的二、三产业，镇区建设松散，土地开发强度低，镇区建筑大都沿路建设，既阻碍交通，又出现很多"空心"地块，土地闲置，浪费严重，城镇结构和布局不甚合理。

规划在详细分析该镇现状、优势和劣势的基础上，依托建成区发展，强调借助城市发展的辐射，与芜湖市和周边地区形成互补型相互协调发展的格局。考虑规划的可实施性和灵活性，分近、中、远三期进行规划（图1）：近期2005年建设用地安排为3.5平方公里，人均建设用地165平方米(考虑南部三华山作为城镇大型公共绿地进行相应折减，人均建设用地146平方米)；中期2010年建设用地为7.1平方公里，人均136平方米；远期2020年建设用地为11.8平方公里，人均118平方米。

提出近期2005年镇区人口达到2.1万人；中期2010年达到5.2万人；远期2020年达到10万人。确定新建和改造居住区的建筑密度不高于35%；居住区容积率，新区不高于1.0，旧区不高于0.8；规划工业用地191.0公顷，人

项目二：芜湖市澛港新镇控制性详细规划（2000~2020年）及修建性详细规划（2000~2005年）

(3)近期开发的低层次、改革中的起步现实与远期的高层次目标是无法避免的矛盾。分阶段规划、"近细远粗"是符合实际的方法。近期目标与规划最好具有相当弹性，是概念性规划。

(4)区域发展是小城镇规划不可忽视的背景和影响因素。"小城镇，大眼界，大战略，小步走"。

(5)发展进程的时序——时间意义上的"速度"，发展空间的结构；土地利用和城镇形态上的"尺度"；发展的内涵，经济和社会进步目标的"程度"，应该是有机的、统一的。即"速度"、"尺度"与"程度"协调同步。

(6)"观念"、"管理和制度"以及"科技文化教育"三个不同层次的精神文明建设是小城镇规划的有机组成部分，又是它实施的保证。对于在经济、社会和文化转型中的小城镇的精神文明，应当说比城市更重要、更艰难又更容易被忽视，但舍此是不行的。

(7)调查研究，综合多方信息；上下结合，群众参与；多次反复，集中各方智慧，是小城镇规划的重要方法。它本身既是一种目标，其过程又是一项提高社会整体规划意识和民主意识的重要内容。

(8)主要经济技术指标，如下表所示。

【传统村镇和街区的结构更新理论】

城市尺度一般都大于城郊、乡镇的尺度。这种尺度从街道、广场等外部空间的三维大小，从建筑容积率、密度、高度等方面体现出来。在规划建设中，应该充分考虑到这些要素，建立一个从城市到乡村的有序的空间结构。如果善加规划，既可以保证一定程度的规整，又可承续乡镇空间亲近自然、空间尺度宜人的优点，"规整而不失活泼"，兼顾自然美与人工美，创造出既不同于一般乡镇，又不同于城市的景观空间，可谓"又城又乡"。

(2)有序的时间——分期建设和基础设施建设的时序问题。城市发展的动态过程决定了有序的框架不仅表现在空间上，还表现在时间的进程上。集中规划、分期建设、基础先行是城市建设有步骤发展的基本原则，特别是交通设施、市政基础设施、配套服务设施的建设应该优先进行，要在时间上建立一个有序的发展软件，这对城市发展的影响同样至关重要。在城乡结合部由于农村与城市管理制度的不同甚至相对立，不仅造成地段的封闭发展，也产生一些"两不管"的地段；由于行政区划、各自管理，还容易造成发展的矛盾。这种划分与管理的弊端对于全局的发展是很不利的，应该逐步打破这种状况，建立和谐有序的管理体制，提高管理效率。

【绍兴鲁迅故里保护规划案例】

提出详细保护要求的范围包括重点保护区和传统风貌协调区，共28.9公顷。考虑到鲁迅故里历史街区用地的特殊性，在28.9公顷范围内规划常住人口为3959人，1308户，人均城市建设用地约73平方米。其中，重点保护区范围内规划常住人口为1199人，共395户，人均城市建设用地为67.8平方米。计划在街区内迁出居住人口1009人，333户。其中，重点保护区迁出居住人口343人，113户；传统风貌协调区迁出居住人口656人，220户。

规划由整体到局部，分历史街区保护规划，街区重点地段规划设计和重点民居保护设计三部分进行。

在保护规划中，以"两横一纵"的步行街为街区的交通骨架，将鲁迅青少年时代生活环境展示区、鲁迅笔下风情展示区、鲁迅文化研究展示区、旅游商业服务区、旅游管理服务区有机地组织起来（图5），并划分为鲁迅文化和绍兴民俗风情两大环境主题，以保护街区的环境形态、街区格局。在保护框架结构中包括重要节点、沿河和沿街的风貌带、特色风貌环境区域三部分，以及它们相互间的有机联系所共同构成的街区整体环境特色，并概括为一区、二河、二街、五节点。

【传统村镇和街区结构更新的启示】

城市化和农业产业化背景下传统村镇和街区的结构更新给我们以下启示：

1. 传统村镇和街区的更新应该在城市化和农业产业化的大背景下进行，不能孤立地看待。

2. 更新应该注重有序的空间结构和有序的时间进程，实现空间和时间的有机统一。

3. 更新应该注重"又城又乡"的景观空间创造，既不同于一般乡镇，又不同于城市。

4. 更新应该注重分期建设和基础设施先行，保证发展的有序性。

5. 更新应该注重精神文明建设，这是小城镇规划的有机组成部分和实施保证。

6. 更新应该注重调查研究、群众参与、多方智慧，提高规划的可操作性和民主性。

7. 历史街区的保护应该由整体到局部，分层次进行，保护街区的环境形态和格局。

English

Structural renewal of Traditional Villages, Towns, and Districts Under the Background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization

By Shan Deqi, Zhao Ruoyan

(Partial Report of National Natural Science Foundation Project, 2003.2)

["Modernized Tradition" and "Contemporary Vernacular" Village and Town Living Environment planning and Architectural Design]

The main achievement of this project research is the completion of planning and design for two key small towns in Wuhu City, Anhui Province.

With economic development, the current trend of accelerating urbanization has extended from the developed southeastern coastal areas to the central region, which is undergoing this dramatic transformation.

Taking medium-sized cities in the central region as research objects allows us to both learn from the experience of the developed southeast and guide the future development of underdeveloped western regions, serving as a link between past and future. Wuhu City is located in southeastern Anhui Province, adjacent to the Yangtze River.

Historically known for its developed transportation and commerce, it is called the "Great Port on the Yangtze, the Pillar of Anhui." With the establishment and development of the national-level economic development zone in the northern part of the city in 1993, and the completion and opening of the Wuhu Yangtze River bridge in September 2000, Wuhu has encountered unprecedented development opportunities, showing a trend of accelerating urbanization.

Most surrounding villages and towns are directly affected by urban development, in the process of agglomerating toward the city, and urgently need to combine their own development with urban development, with contradictions and problems becoming more concentrated.

Project One: Master plan of Sanshan Town, Fanchang County, Anhui Province (2000-2020)

This project began in July 2000 and ended in November 2000. In the master plan, the project team conducted in-depth grassroots research and communicated multiple times with town, county, and city leaders to improve the operability of the plan. The project has been approved and entered the implementation phase, with good execution, and won a provincial-level small town planning and design award.

The national emphasis on the policy of "small towns, great strategy" has brought new opportunities for small town development.

As a provincial-level pilot town, key town, central established town for small town construction, and one of Wuhu's five satellite towns, Sanshan Town, under the background of urban economic development and urbanization, requires integration with Wuhu's overall development.

Sanshan Town is located between Wuhu City and Fanchang County, serving as the northern gateway of Fanchang, 19 kilometers southwest of Wuhu City and 20 kilometers from Fanchang County seat, with a total area of 40.5 square kilometers. The Wuhu-Tongling Highway (S321 provincial road) runs through it.

Sanshan Town governs 13 village committees and one street neighborhood committee, with a total population of 28,787 and a town area population of approximately

10,000 (including 8,000 permanent residents), accounting for 34.7% of the total population. In the past two years, the town has completed about 70,000 square meters of commercial housing development, with over 80% of families moving into buildings through new construction or renovation, and per capita housing area of 28 square meters.

The built-up area is 1.5 square kilometers, and the town road network has initially taken shape.

Located on the first-class urban development axis, Sanshan Town has obvious location advantages due to convenient transportation, and has long been a commercial shopping center and agricultural and sideline product distribution center for surrounding towns, attracting large numbers of vegetable wholesalers from across the country every year for wholesale and retail of vegetables and agricultural and sideline products.

However, due to the small urban population scale, Sanshan Town's urban infrastructure and social service facilities are not perfect, township enterprises are small in scale, industrial characteristics are not prominent, the industrial structure is not very reasonable, lacking secondary and tertiary industries complementary to the central city Wuhu.

Town construction is loose, land development intensity is low, most buildings in the town are built along roads, which both hinders traffic and creates many "hollow" plots, with idle land and serious waste, and the urban structure and layout are not very reasonable.

based on a detailed analysis of the town's current situation, advantages, and disadvantages, the plan relies on the built-up area for development, emphasizes leveraging the radiation of urban development, and forms a complementary and coordinated development pattern with Wuhu City and surrounding areas.

Considering the implementability and flexibility of the plan, it is divided into three phases: near, medium, and far (Fig.

1): near-term 2005 construction land arranged at 3.5 square kilometers, per capita construction land 165 square meters (considering Sanhua Mountain in the south as a large urban public green space for corresponding reduction, per capita construction land 146 square meters); medium-term 2010 construction land 7.1 square kilometers, per capita 136 square meters; far-term 2020 construction land 11.8 square kilometers, per capita 118 square meters.

Proposed near-term 2005 town population reaching 21,000; medium-term 2010 reaching 52,000; far-term 2020 reaching 100,000.

Determined that the building density of new and renovated residential areas should not exceed 35%; residential area floor area ratio, new area not higher than 1.0, old area not higher than 0.8; planned industrial land 191.0 hectares, per

Project Two: Regulatory Detailed plan of Lugang New Town, Wuhu City (2000-2020) and Site plan (2000-2005)

(3) The low level of near-term development, the starting reality in reform, and the high-level long-term goal are unavoidable contradictions. Phased planning and "detailed near-term, rough long-term" is a practical method. Near-term goals and plans should preferably have considerable flexibility and be conceptual plans.

(4) Regional development is a background and influencing factor that cannot be ignored in small town planning. "small towns, broad vision, great strategy, small steps."

(5) The timing of development process — "speed" in the temporal sense; the structure of development space; "scale" in land use and urban form; the connotation of development, the "degree" of economic and social progress goals — should be organic and unified. That is, "speed," "scale," and "degree" coordinated synchronously.

(6) Three different levels of spiritual civilization construction — "concepts," "management and institutions," and "science, technology, culture, and education" — are organic components of small town planning and also guarantees for its implementation.

For small towns in economic, social, and cultural transformation, spiritual civilization is arguably more important, more difficult, and more easily overlooked than in cities, but without it, nothing works.

(7) Investigation and research,综合多方 information; top-down and bottom-up combination, mass participation; multiple iterations, concentrating wisdom from all parties — are important methods for small town planning. It is itself both a goal, and its process is an important content for improving social overall planning awareness and democratic awareness.

(8) Main economic and technical indicators, as shown in the table below.

[Theory of Structural renewal of Traditional Villages, Towns, and Districts]

Urban scale is generally larger than that of suburban and township areas. This scale is reflected in the three-dimensional size of external spaces such as streets and squares, and in building floor area ratio, density, height, etc.

In planning and construction, these elements should be fully considered, establishing an orderly spatial structure from city to countryside.

If well planned, it can ensure a certain degree of regularity while continuing the advantages of township space being close to nature and human-scaled — "regular without losing liveliness" — balancing natural beauty and artificial beauty, creating a landscape space that is different from both ordinary towns and cities, which can be called "both urban and rural."

(2) Orderly time — the issue of phased construction and the timing of infrastructure construction. The dynamic process of urban development determines that an orderly framework is manifested not only in space but also in the temporal process.

Centralized planning, phased construction, and infrastructure first are basic principles for step-by-step urban development.

In particular, transportation facilities, municipal infrastructure, and supporting service facilities should be constructed first, establishing an orderly development software in time, which is equally crucial for urban development.

In urban-rural fringe areas, due to the different甚至 opposing management systems of rural and urban areas, not only does it cause closed development of areas, but also some "neither-man's-land" areas; due to administrative divisions and separate management, it is also easy to cause development contradictions.

The drawbacks of this division and management are very不利 to overall development, and this situation should be gradually broken to establish a harmonious and orderly management system and improve management efficiency.

[Case of Shaoxing Lu Xun's Hometown protection plan]

The scope for detailed protection requirements includes key protection zones and traditional style coordination zones, totaling 28.9 hectares.

Considering the particularity of land use in the Lu Xun's Hometown historic district, the planned permanent population within the 28.9 hectares is 3,959 people in 1,308 households, with per capita urban construction land of about 73 square meters.

Among them, the planned permanent population within the key protection zone is 1,199 people in 395 households, with per capita urban construction land of 67.8 square meters. It is planned to relocate 1,009 residents in 333 households within the district.

Among them, the key protection zone relocates 343 residents in 113 households; the traditional style coordination zone relocates 656 residents in 220 households.

The plan proceeds from overall to local, divided into three parts: historic district protection plan, key district section planning and design, and key residential building protection design.

In the protection plan, with the "two horizontal and one vertical" pedestrian streets as the district's transportation skeleton, the Lu Xun youth living environment display area, Lu Xun's written style display area, Lu Xun culture research display area, tourism commercial service area, and tourism management service area are organically organized (Fig.

5), and divided into two major environmental themes of Lu Xun culture and Shaoxing folk customs, to protect the district's environmental form and district pattern.

The protection framework structure includes three parts: important nodes, riverside and street-side style belts, and characteristic style environmental areas, as well as the organic connections between them that together constitute the district's overall environmental characteristics, summarized as one district, two rivers, two streets, and five nodes.

[Enlightenment from Structural renewal of Traditional Villages, Towns, and Districts]

The structural renewal of traditional villages, towns, and districts under the background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization gives us the following enlightenment:

1. The renewal of traditional villages, towns, and districts should be carried out under the broad background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization, and cannot be viewed in isolation.

2. renewal should focus on orderly spatial structure and orderly temporal process, achieving organic unity of space and time.

3. renewal should focus on creating "both urban and rural" landscape spaces, different from both ordinary towns and cities.

4. renewal should focus on phased construction and infrastructure first, ensuring orderly development.

5. renewal should focus on spiritual civilization construction, which is an organic component and implementation guarantee of small town planning.

6. renewal should focus on investigation and research, mass participation, and多方 wisdom, improving the operability and democracy of planning.

7. The protection of historic districts should proceed from overall to local, carried out in layers, protecting the district's environmental form and pattern.

城郊视野中的乡村——安徽省芜湖市澛港镇龙华中心村规划设计
中文

城郊视野中的乡村——安徽省芜湖市澛港镇龙华中心村规划设计

单德启 赵之枫

（原载于《建筑学报》，1999.11）

【城市化和农业产业化背景下的中心村建设】

改革开放后，农村经济飞速发展，农村居民的经济状况大为改观，再加上我国农民对住房的一贯重视，农村很快掀起了建房的热潮，20世纪80年代中期更是达到了高潮，至今这种热潮仍在延续，推动了农村居住条件的迅速改善。1980年到1997年我国农村人均居住面积从9.4平方米增长为13.69平方米。但是，在这股热潮背后，走近农村，会发现有很多问题值得思考。

长期以来，由于农民建房筹措资金、规划设计、施工组织的自发性而又缺乏指导，村庄建设呈现出强烈的自主性特点，有相当普遍的盲目性和无序性。在居民居住状况明显改观的同时，也产生了农村住宅盲目消费、乱占耕地、村落布局散乱、卫生环境差、单体造型或单一攀比，或追求时髦、功能不合理、质量低劣等问题，几千年沉积下来的我国传统民居的精髓也正在消失（图1、图2）。更重要的是，这种只顾自身，与区域规划相脱节的无序发展对我国城市化的进一步发展、城乡一体化的进程产生不利影响。

我国的传统村镇是在小农经济基础上发展起来的，历史悠久，带有规模小而分散的特点。现在，随着经济的发展，社会化和产业化进程加快，农村的生产要素也要从分散走向集聚，由"同质同构"（单一小农经济）向"异质异构"（一、二、三产合理配置，人口资源等生产要素的集聚与重新组合）转变，这种转变的投影即是传统村镇的结构更新——因地制宜，适当集聚。人口向镇区集中，工业向园区集中。围绕城镇，组团式布局，或者采取撤村并点、撤乡建镇等措施，进行规模和结构调整。既节约用地，促进土地复垦，保护环境，又利于基础设施的建设和农民生活质量的提高，保证居住环境的可持续发展。中心村的建设就是采

【龙华中心村的规划设计】

龙华中心村的规划设计是在设计人员多次进行现场深入调研的基础上完成的。从芜湖市、澛港镇、龙华村的整体社会经济情况，直到各个自然村一户户居民家中，涉及产业结构与经济收入、人口增长与劳动力转移、土地利用与环境变迁，以及社会、家庭、生活方式等各个方面，从宏观到微观进行了多层次的细致调查，避免就村论村，试图从区域整体发展的角度，在城市化和农业产业化背景下考察中心村规划，防止城乡脱节。深入细致地调查还能避免规划脱离村庄实际情况。

长期以来，建筑师们更多地从事城市建设，再加上城市的规划设计理论较成熟，因此，在城市住宅设计中可以信手拈来。农村则完全不同，且不说农村情况建筑师不甚熟悉，农村居民点规划设计缺乏成熟理论，尤为重要的是，村落的地域差异很大，再加上农村处于社会转型期，各地的社会经济条件不同、发展速度不同，因此对村落进行细致的调查研究是必要的，也是规划成功的保证。

中心村规划依地势变化、高压线的走向、水渠的位置，并考虑到可操作性，将中心村划分的四个组团，依时间分期建设与依地段的逐步完整结合起来。以现有小山丘作为小区的标志性景观，结合在其南侧设村委会、活动站等公共建筑，形成小区社会文化中心；中部则结合水塘、中心绿地设幼儿园、小商店等，形成小区生活休闲中心，方便生活，以利交往。两个中心相互呼应，带动整个小区的发展，使小区既结构清晰、又生动活泼，富有情趣，增强社区的凝聚力。

小区主路为流线型，以简洁、流畅的线型达到"通而不畅"的效果。次要道路呈平行状或放射状与主路联系，结合地形、地貌顺势布置，便捷通达，曲折自然，使人产生步移景异的景观效果，使得传统村落中自然而富有人情味的道路空

附设车库，顶层设有观景平台。造型丰富，错落有致。

所有住宅户型均为成套设计，包括厨房和浴厕。

造型设计以坡顶为主，平、坡结合，高低错落，造型生动活泼，便于多样组合。几种户型建筑外观既统一又有区别，有利于形成小区的整体和谐特色。

【家园归属感的继承与发扬】

中心村的建设不仅要解决物质层面的居住问题，精神层面的新型乡村社区的建立同样重要。原来分属不同自然村的村民集中聚居，新社区要保持原有村落文化的延续性，包括邻里关系、风俗习惯、凝聚力、认同感与归属感等，以促进新社区的持续发展。

中心村规划依地势变化、高压线的走向、水渠的位置，并考虑到可操作性，将中心村划分的四个组团，依时间分期建设与依地段的逐步完整结合起来。以现有小山丘作为小区的标志性景观，结合在其南侧设村委会、活动站等公共建筑，形成小区社会文化中心；中部则结合水塘、中心绿地设幼儿园、小商店等，形成小区生活休闲中心，方便生活，以利交往。两个中心相互呼应，带动整个小区的发展，使小区既结构清晰、又生动活泼，富有情趣，增强社区的凝聚力。

【不可阻挡又不可逾越的历史脚步】

我们还体会到：在中心村的规划与建设中，发动居民参与十分重要。在城市住宅建设中由于住宅建造前住户对象不易明确，住户参与设计不易实施。而在农村，住宅建设资金的自主性使得住户参与设计成为可能，且非常重要。虽然居民缺乏建筑专业知识，但他们参与的热情很高，而且农村民主制度的健全，也使其更为现实可行。同时，通过居民在设计过程中的参与，也可提高其对住区环境和居住质量的认识水平，是一个逐步改变农民认为房子越大越好等传统意识的过程。通过居民参与，建立一个社区自主的理解聚落环境的方向努力，使之有利于新村建设工作的顺利实施，以达到长久的聚落环境的改善和发展。

回顾规划的全过程，深感中心村的规划建设是一项十分迫切的任务，也是一项十分艰难的工作。农村居民点的整治是社会经济发展的一个必然阶段，涉及到经济政策方面，如户籍制度、土地制度、资金投入和产权；物质建设方面，如基础设施建设、农村住宅产业化；精神层面方面，如居民搬迁问题、移风易俗与社区场所精神的重建等等多方面因素，是一项十分复杂的系统工程。当前我国对于占人口80%的农村居民点的规划建设以及农村居民点如何与城市协同发展的研究还很不够，规划师和建筑师对这一现实而又迫切的问题应该引起足够的重视，更多地投入到关系国计民生的建设中去。

城郊视野中的乡村，是城市化进程中不可忽视的重要组成部分。中心村的建设，既是城市化的必然要求，也是农业产业化的必然结果。在城郊视野中，乡村不再是城市的对立面，而是城市的延伸和补充。通过中心村的建设，可以实现城乡协调发展，共同繁荣。

English

The Countryside in the Suburban Vision — planning and Design of Longhua central village, Lugang Town, Wuhu City, Anhui Province

By Shan Deqi, Zhao Zhifeng

(Originally published in Architectural Journal, 1999.11)

[central village construction Under the Background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization]

After the reform and opening up, the rural economy developed rapidly, and the economic conditions of rural residents greatly improved. Coupled with Chinese farmers' consistent emphasis on housing, a wave of house building quickly arose in rural areas, reaching a climax in the mid-1980s.

This wave continues to this day, promoting the rapid improvement of rural living conditions. From 1980 to 1997, China's rural per capita living area increased from 9.4 square meters to 13.69 square meters. However, behind this wave, approaching rural areas, one finds many problems worth thinking about.

For a long time, due to the spontaneity of farmers raising funds, planning and design, and construction organization, coupled with a lack of guidance, village construction has shown strong autonomous characteristics, with相当普遍的 blindness and disorder.

While residents' living conditions have明显 improved, problems have also arisen, such as blind consumption of rural housing, illegal occupation of cultivated land, scattered village layout, poor sanitary environment, monotonous or攀比 individual building forms, or pursuit of fashion with unreasonable functions and poor quality.

The essence of China's traditional dwellings accumulated over thousands of years is also disappearing (Figs. 1, 2). More importantly, this self-centered, disorderly development disconnected from regional planning has an adverse impact on the further development of China's urbanization and the process of urban-rural integration.

China's traditional villages and towns developed on the basis of a small-scale peasant economy, with a long history and characteristics of small scale and dispersion.

Now, with economic development and accelerated socialization and industrialization, rural production factors must also move from dispersion to agglomeration, transforming from "homogeneous isomorphism" (single small-scale peasant economy) to "heterogeneous isomorphism" (rational allocation of primary, secondary, and tertiary industries, agglomeration and recombination of production factors such as population and resources).

The projection of this transformation is the structural renewal of traditional villages and towns — adapting to local conditions and appropriate agglomeration. Population concentrates in town areas, industry concentrates in industrial parks.

Around towns, group-style layout, or measures such as merging villages and points, merging townships and building towns, carry out scale and structural adjustment.

This not only saves land, promotes land reclamation, and protects the environment, but also facilitates infrastructure construction and improvement of farmers' quality of life, ensuring sustainable development of the living environment. central village construction is adopted

[planning and Design of Longhua central village]

The planning and design of Longhua central village was completed on the basis of multiple in-depth on-site investigations by design personnel.

From the overall socio-economic conditions of Wuhu City, Lugang Town, and Longhua Village, to individual households in each natural village, involving industrial structure and economic income, population growth and labor transfer, land use and environmental changes, as well as social, family, lifestyle and other aspects, multi-level detailed investigations were conducted from macro to micro, avoiding discussing the village in isolation.

The attempt was to examine central village planning from the perspective of regional overall development, under the background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization, preventing urban-rural disconnection. In-depth and detailed investigations can also avoid planning脱离 the actual situation of the village.

For a long time, architects have been more engaged in urban construction, and coupled with the relatively mature urban planning and design theory, they can handle urban residential design with ease. Rural areas are completely different.

Not to mention architects' unfamiliarity with rural conditions, rural settlement planning and design lacks mature theory.

Most importantly, the regional differences of villages are great, and coupled with rural areas being in a period of social transformation, with different socio-economic conditions and development speeds in various places, detailed investigation and research of villages is necessary and also the guarantee of successful planning.

The central village plan follows terrain changes, the direction of high-voltage lines, and the position of water channels, and considering operability, divides the central village into four groups, combining phased construction over time with gradual completion by location.

The existing small hill is used as the landmark landscape of the residential area, combined with the village committee, activity station and other public buildings on its south side, forming the community social and cultural center; the central part combines ponds and central green space to set up kindergartens, small shops, etc., forming the community living and leisure center, facilitating life and social interaction.

The two centers echo each other, driving the development of the entire community, making the community both structurally clear and lively, full of charm, and enhancing community cohesion.

The main road of the community is streamlined, achieving the effect of "connected but not through" with simple and smooth alignment.

Secondary roads are parallel or radial, connecting with the main road, arranged following the terrain and landform, convenient and accessible, winding naturally, creating a landscape effect of changing scenery with each step, making the natural and human-touch road space in traditional villages

with attached garages, and observation platforms on the top floor. Rich in form, well-proportioned and varied.

All residential unit types are complete-set designs, including kitchens and bathrooms.

The form design mainly uses sloped roofs, combining flat and sloped roofs, with high and low staggered arrangement, lively and varied forms, facilitating diverse combinations. The architectural appearances of several unit types are both unified and differentiated, conducive to forming the overall harmonious characteristics of the community.

[Inheritance and Development of Sense of Home Belonging]

The construction of central villages must not only solve the material-level living problems, but the establishment of a new-type rural community at the spiritual level is equally important. Villagers originally belonging to different natural villages live together in concentration.

The new community must maintain the continuity of original village culture, including neighborhood relations, customs and habits, cohesion, sense of identity and belonging, to promote the sustainable development of the new community.

The central village plan follows terrain changes, the direction of high-voltage lines, and the position of water channels, and considering operability, divides the central village into four groups, combining phased construction over time with gradual completion by location.

The existing small hill is used as the landmark landscape of the residential area, combined with the village committee, activity station and other public buildings on its south side, forming the community social and cultural center; the central part combines ponds and central green space to set up kindergartens, small shops, etc., forming the community living and leisure center, facilitating life and social interaction.

The two centers echo each other, driving the development of the entire community, making the community both structurally clear and lively, full of charm, and enhancing community cohesion.

[Inevitable and Insurmountable Historical Footsteps]

We also realized that in the planning and construction of central villages, mobilizing resident participation is very important. In urban residential construction, because the residents are not easily identified before housing construction, resident participation in design is not easy to implement.

In rural areas, the autonomy of housing construction funds makes resident participation in design possible and very important. Although residents lack architectural expertise, their enthusiasm for participation is high, and the improvement of rural democratic systems also makes it more feasible.

At the same time, through resident participation in the design process, their awareness of the living environment and housing quality can be improved, which is a process of gradually changing farmers' traditional consciousness such as thinking the bigger the house the better.

Through resident participation, establishing a community-autonomous understanding of the settlement environment direction is conducive to the smooth implementation of new village construction, achieving long-term improvement and development of the settlement environment.

Reviewing the entire planning process, I deeply feel that the planning and construction of central villages is both a very urgent task and a very difficult job.

The remediation of rural settlements is an inevitable stage of socio-economic development, involving economic policy aspects such as household registration system, land system, capital investment and property rights; material construction aspects such as infrastructure construction and rural housing industrialization; spiritual aspects such as resident relocation issues, changing customs and the reconstruction of community place spirit, and many other factors — it is a very complex systems engineering.

Currently, China's research on the planning and construction of rural settlements accounting for 80% of the population, and how rural settlements develop collaboratively with cities, is still insufficient.

planners and architects should pay sufficient attention to this realistic and urgent issue, and invest more in construction related to the national economy and people's livelihood.

The countryside in the suburban vision is an important component that cannot be ignored in the urbanization process. The construction of central villages is both an inevitable requirement of urbanization and an inevitable result of agricultural industrialization.

In the suburban vision, the countryside is no longer the opposite of the city, but an extension and supplement of the city. Through the construction of central villages, coordinated urban-rural development and common prosperity can be achieved.

本篇术语 Key Terms

中心村建设 / Central Village Construction 城郊视野中的乡村 / Countryside in Suburban Vision 龙华中心村 / Longhua Central Village 澛港镇 / Lugang Town 同质同构异质异构 / Homogeneous Isomorphism / Heterogeneous Isomorphism 撤村并点撤乡建镇 / Merging Villages and Points / Merging Townships and Building Towns 家园归属感 / Sense of Home Belonging 社区凝聚力 / Community Cohesion 居民参与 / Resident Participation 农村住宅产业化 / Rural Housing Industrialization 城乡一体化 / Urban-Rural Integration 通而不畅 / Connected but Not Through (road design)
3
"空心村"改造规划设计——以安徽省巢湖地区空心村改造为例
中文

"空心村"改造规划设计——以安徽省巢湖地区空心村改造为例

单德启 张军英

（原载于《建筑学报》，1999.11）

改革开放以来，随着农村经济的发展，村庄面貌普遍发生变化；但部分村庄整体发展缺乏活力、管理薄弱、村庄中心衰败、外围建设无序蔓延，一般称之为空心村现象。空心村是农村现代化过程中，由于农业经济和就业结构的转变造成村庄内部建设用地闲置的一种聚落空间形态的异化现象。

由于我国耕地资源十分紧缺，伴随住宅高频率、大规模更新而出现的空心村无序扩展，必将对节约宝贵的耕地资源产生消极的影响。根据我国城市化进程的实际情况，村庄在相当长的一段时间之内仍然将是大量农村人口生活和居住的主要地点。改造空心村，对聚落空间结构进行重组，可以有效地节约耕地、改善农村居住环境和生活品质，这对维护社会稳定和发展、推进农村现代化建设等具有重要意义。

【"空心村"改造的几种类型】

从巢湖地区等地村庄的调查和空心村改造规划，以及新型农村实用住宅设计实践分析，改造空心村要结合当地实际，突出个性和特色。只有这样，才能使空心村的改造易于实施。根据改造前后的相对关系等因素，空心村的改造主要包括四种类型：就地改造；部分易地改造；易地改造；转型改造。

（一）就地改造

具备以下特征的空心村在改造时应当首选就地改造：(1)村庄所在地位于平原地区，尤其是国家的重点产粮区；(2)村庄选址基本合理，有利于发展；(3)村庄规模较大，但布局松散，人均建设用地超标；(4)平房多，或者质量较差的旧房多，

村改造规划，确保新区与旧村改造的连贯和一致。部分易地改造最终应当使村庄建设用地有所节余，数量至少能够补偿新区所占土地；同时，对返还的土地要进行改造，提高土地质量，实现复耕或还林。

（三）易地改造

当村庄原址由于某些原因不适合继续作为村庄建设用地的时候，可以考虑进行易地改造，一般情况是：(1)由于自然环境恶化、人民生命财产受到威胁，比如1998年特大洪涝灾害中泄洪区的村庄必须搬迁；(2)由于自然条件制约、难以进一步发展村庄经济和提高人民生活水平，比如一些偏远山区中的贫困村庄；(3)由于影响国家或地区发展的重点工程建设而必须搬迁的村庄，比如三峡工程涉及的大规模移民；(4)由于国家发展中小城市或小城镇建设的需要，必须进行易地改造的村庄。这类村庄，例如芜湖市龙华村，易地改造有利于更加合理地选址，更便利人民群众生产和生活，更加有利于经济、文化等各项事业发展。

易地改造必然牵扯到诸如选址、规划设计、建设、搬迁等等许多方面的问题，必须谨慎决策。易地改造必须经过科学的可行性研究；必须解决搬迁动员、新村筹建、生产发展、居民生活等具体问题；必须制定完善的搬迁规划和后续发展规划；必须对新址制定科学的规划和居住环境设计，避免重复旧村的错误，造成更大的浪费。

（四）转型改造

空心村改造不仅是我国广大农村腹地面临的问题，对于受到城市辐射影响，正在进行乡村——城市转型的地区同样重要。这些村庄地处城市郊区甚至城镇建成区内部，不仅人口职业发生转变，而且聚落空间也发生转变；不仅用地性质转变为城市用地，而且生产生活方式、基础设施条件也发生巨大变化。这些巨变反映在聚落形态上，往往表现出更为严重的空心化，被称为"都市中的村庄"，阻碍了城镇的进一步发展。针对这类空心村可以采用转型改造。

一般来说，转型改造属于就地改造的一种特殊情况，但是与一般空心村的改造相比，面临的城市住宅小区类似的基础设施、配套公用设施、容积率、户型等更高的要求；与城市住宅小区相比，则又在住户人群组成、收入水平、建筑层数、建筑密度等方面存在差异。考虑到土地升值的必然趋势，在这类聚落的改造中，完全按照农村住宅的要求进行考虑必将是短视的；完全按照城市一般住宅区进行设计也是不合理的做法。一种可行的思路是多元化设计，即户型与空间结构多样化，同时满足不同层次人群的需求。

例如，巢湖市槐林镇尚店村，该村处在槐林镇建成区用地范围内，全村没有耕地，绝大多数村民在村外有新建住宅；同时，槐林镇是巢湖地区经济最为活跃的两个镇之一，发展速度快，因此，该村面临从农村向城镇转型的改造问题(图4)。

【空心村改造的社会意义】

因此空心村的改造必将涉及到社会改造的方方面面，决非单纯从技术角度出发或者短时期内能够解决的问题，还需要规划和设计人员与各方面的协同合作，以及有关法规的逐步建立健全。但无论如何，加强对空心村现象及其对策的研究和实践指导是当前迫切需要的，同时也是意义深远的，这也是从建筑学角度出发对整个社会的现代化改造应尽的责任。

（注：本文为作者硕士学位论文《空心村现象及对策初探》的主要部分，指导教师为单德启教授。该论文被清华大学评为研究生优秀论文。）

【空心村改造的启示】

"空心村"改造规划设计给我们以下启示：

1. 空心村现象是农村现代化过程中的必然产物，需要正视和解决。

2. 空心村改造应该因地制宜，根据不同情况采取不同的改造类型（就地改造、部分易地改造、易地改造、转型改造）。

3. 空心村改造不仅是技术问题，更是社会问题，需要各方面的协同合作。

4. 空心村改造应该注重节约耕地、改善农村居住环境和生活品质。

5. 空心村改造应该注重规划的科学性和可操作性，避免重复旧村的错误。

6. 转型改造是城郊地区空心村改造的特殊类型，需要多元化设计，满足不同层次人群的需求。

"空心村"改造是中国农村现代化进程中的重要课题，需要建筑师、规划师、政府和村民的共同努力。通过科学的规划设计和合理的改造实施，可以实现农村聚落空间的优化重组，促进农村经济社会的可持续发展。

English

planning and Design of "hollow village" Reconstruction — A Case Study of hollow village Reconstruction in Chaohu Region, Anhui Province

By Shan Deqi, Zhang Junying

(Originally published in Architectural Journal, 1999.11)

Since the reform and opening up, with the development of the rural economy, the appearance of villages has generally changed; however, some villages lack vitality in overall development, have weak management, declining village centers, and disorderly outward expansion of construction — generally called the "hollow village" phenomenon.

hollow villages are an alienation phenomenon of settlement spatial form, caused by the transformation of agricultural economy and employment structure during rural modernization, resulting in idle construction land within villages.

Due to the very scarce arable land resources in China, the disorderly expansion of hollow villages accompanying high-frequency and large-scale housing renewal will inevitably have a negative impact on conserving precious arable land resources.

According to the actual situation of China's urbanization process, villages will remain the main living and residential locations for a large rural population for a considerable period of time.

Reconstructing hollow villages and reorganizing settlement spatial structure can effectively save arable land, improve rural living environment and quality of life, which is of great significance for maintaining social stability and development, and promoting rural modernization.

[Several Types of "hollow village" Reconstruction]

From the investigation of villages in Chaohu region and other areas, hollow village reconstruction planning, and the practice analysis of new-type rural practical housing design, reconstructing hollow villages must结合 local reality, highlighting individuality and characteristics.

Only in this way can hollow village reconstruction be easily implemented. According to factors such as the relative relationship before and after reconstruction, hollow village reconstruction mainly includes four types: in-situ reconstruction; partial relocation reconstruction; relocation reconstruction; transformation reconstruction.

(1) In-Situ Reconstruction

hollow villages with the following characteristics should首选 in-situ reconstruction: (1) The village is located in a plain area, especially a national key grain-producing area; (2) The village site is basically reasonable and conducive to development; (3) The village is large in scale but loosely laid out, with per capita construction land exceeding standards; (4) Many single-story houses, or many old houses of poor quality,

village reconstruction planning, ensuring coherence and consistency between new area and old village reconstruction.

Partial relocation reconstruction should ultimately result in savings in village construction land, at least enough to compensate for the land occupied by the new area; at the same time, the returned land should be improved to enhance land quality, achieving reclamation or reforestation.

(3) Relocation Reconstruction

When the original village site is unsuitable to continue as village construction land for certain reasons, relocation reconstruction can be considered.

General situations include: (1) Due to deterioration of the natural environment threatening people's lives and property, such as villages in flood discharge areas during the 1998 catastrophic floods that must be relocated; (2) Due to natural condition constraints making it difficult to further develop village economy and improve people's living standards, such as impoverished villages in some remote mountainous areas; (3) Villages that must be relocated due to key national or regional development projects, such as the large-scale resettlement involved in the Three Gorges Project; (4) Villages that must undergo relocation reconstruction due to national development of small and medium-sized cities or small town construction.

Such villages, such as Longhua Village in Wuhu City, benefit from relocation reconstruction with more reasonable site selection, more convenient production and life for the people, and more favorable development of economic, cultural and other undertakings.

Relocation reconstruction inevitably involves many aspects such as site selection, planning and design, construction, relocation, etc., and must be carefully decided.

Relocation reconstruction must undergo scientific feasibility studies; must solve specific problems such as relocation mobilization, new village preparation, production development, and resident life; must formulate complete relocation plans and subsequent development plans; must formulate scientific planning and living environment design for the new site, avoiding repeating old village mistakes and causing greater waste.

(4) transformation Reconstruction

hollow village reconstruction is not only a problem faced by China's vast rural hinterland, but also equally important for areas affected by urban radiation and undergoing rural-urban transformation.

These villages are located in urban suburbs or even within urban built-up areas; not only does the population's occupation change, but the settlement space also changes; not only does land use change to urban land, but production and lifestyle, and infrastructure conditions also undergo tremendous changes.

These great changes, reflected in settlement form, often show more severe hollowing, called "villages in the city," hindering further urban development. For such hollow villages, transformation reconstruction can be adopted.

Generally speaking, transformation reconstruction is a special case of in-situ reconstruction.

However, compared with general hollow village reconstruction, it faces higher requirements similar to urban residential communities in terms of infrastructure, supporting public facilities, floor area ratio, and unit types; compared with urban residential communities, it differs in resident population composition, income level, building stories, and building density.

Considering the inevitable trend of land appreciation, in the reconstruction of such settlements, considering entirely according to rural housing requirements would be short-sighted; designing entirely according to general urban residential areas is also unreasonable.

A feasible approach is diversified design, that is, diversified unit types and spatial structures, simultaneously meeting the needs of different levels of people.

For example, Shangdian Village in Huailin Town, Chaohu City, is located within the built-up area of Huailin Town. The whole village has no arable land, and the vast majority of villagers have new houses outside the village; at the same time, Huailin Town is one of the two most economically active towns in Chaohu region, with rapid development.

Therefore, this village faces the reconstruction problem of transforming from rural to urban (Fig. 4).

[Social Significance of hollow village Reconstruction]

Therefore, hollow village reconstruction will inevitably involve all aspects of social transformation, by no means a problem that can be solved purely from a technical perspective or in a short period of time.

It also requires collaborative cooperation between planners and designers and all parties, as well as the gradual establishment and improvement of relevant regulations.

In any case, strengthening research and practical guidance on the hollow village phenomenon and its countermeasures is urgently needed at present, and is also of far-reaching significance — this is also the responsibility that architecture should fulfill for the modernization transformation of the entire society from an architectural perspective.

(Note: This article is the main part of the author's master's thesis "A Preliminary Study on the hollow village Phenomenon and Countermeasures," supervised by Professor Shan Deqi. The thesis was rated as an excellent graduate thesis by Tsinghua University.)

[Enlightenment from hollow village Reconstruction]

The planning and design of "hollow village" reconstruction gives us the following enlightenment:

1. The hollow village phenomenon is an inevitable product of the rural modernization process, requiring recognition and resolution.

2. hollow village reconstruction should adapt to local conditions, adopting different reconstruction types (in-situ, partial relocation, relocation, transformation) according to different situations.

3. hollow village reconstruction is not only a technical issue but also a social issue, requiring collaborative cooperation from all parties.

4. hollow village reconstruction should focus on saving arable land and improving rural living environment and quality of life.

5. hollow village reconstruction should focus on the scientific nature and operability of planning, avoiding repeating old village mistakes.

6. transformation reconstruction is a special type of hollow village reconstruction in suburban areas, requiring diversified design to meet the needs of different levels of people.

"hollow village" reconstruction is an important topic in China's rural modernization process, requiring the joint efforts of architects, planners, government, and villagers. Through scientific planning and design and reasonable reconstruction implementation, the optimal reorganization of rural settlement space can be achieved, promoting the sustainable development of rural economy and society.

本篇术语 Key Terms

空心村 / Hollow Village 空心村改造 / Hollow Village Reconstruction 就地改造 / In-Situ Reconstruction 部分易地改造 / Partial Relocation Reconstruction 易地改造 / Relocation Reconstruction 转型改造 / Transformation Reconstruction 巢湖地区 / Chaohu Region 都市中的村庄 / Village in the City 槐林镇尚店村 / Shangdian Village, Huailin Town 聚落空间形态异化 / Alienation of Settlement Spatial Form 耕地资源节约 / Arable Land Resource Conservation 农村现代化 / Rural Modernization

网络内外的传统街区——文化整体网络内外的价值评析
中文

网络内外的传统街区——文化整体网络内外的价值评析

单德启 范霄鹏

（原载于《建筑学报》，2001.9）

民族或地域文化复合体，包括政治、社会、经济和思想四个体系。文化复合体的发展变化来自这四个体系的发展和交叠，其中某一分支体系的任何变化不仅会牵动整个复合体，还会牵动其他体系的变化，各体系之间存在着相互制衡的关系。

【价值网络】

在某一时代特征的大背景下，文化复合体有其总的价值观，并由此形成强烈的共同认可氛围。各个组成体系处在此总体环境之中，各自建立起与总价值观相协同的自身价值观网络。在整个网络中价值观是无处不在的，它虽有级差和关注方面上的区别，但本身是连贯的，它施加作用的方向是由整体指向局部、联合指向个别的。文化复合体的总价值观不可能凭空存在，它建立在对各子体系价值网络整合的基础之上，子体系价值网络的发展直接促成和推动总价值观的发展与变化。

如果将由复合体指向子体系的价值观作用方向视为"正方向"，那么由子体系指向整个复合体的价值观作用方向就是"逆方向"，它是由个别指向联合，局部指向整体的。无论何时正逆两个方向的作用都是同时存在的，它们之间动态的相互作用共同构成了价值观念的发展，但两个方向施加出的作用都不会为被作用者所完全接受，因为在整个文化复合体和其各子体系之间存在有价值观的筛选和接纳机制。

该机制是一整套复杂的价值判断标准，它与民族或地域文化心理密切相关，随着文化复合体与其各子体系之间关系的不同而侧重不同，在此机制基础上建立起来的相对稳定和连贯的价值网络是对所面对的事物或事件的判断标准。

【网络外的传统街区】

人们交往和流动的增加，社会体系中人与人的关系已不再以家族血缘关系为中心；邻里关系、生活方式和生活节奏等的改变；加之伦理道德、法律制度等观念的变化；人与自然资源的关系也由初级的依赖趋向综合的利用，使得对这些关系有所表达的人们聚居形态也与以往相异(图7)。传统街区已经不能承载和反映这些变化，在政治、经济和社会的体系中体现不出它的现实价值和发展价值，因此就不能被纳入它们的价值网络之中。时有传统街区被拆除的消息见诸报端，导致其发生的主要原由是：传统街区对各种变化的适应性不足，同时又占用了一些土地资源，在以上体系的价值评判中被否定。

【网络内的传统街区】

从思想体系形成的历程上考量，其价值网络构成是通过积累达成的，代代延承、演化发展和完善更新。价值网络不仅展现出该民族和地域独特的观念构成，还直接影响了对现实事物的判断标准，隐含了未来思想观念的发展走向。思想体系中价值网络的发生、建立和发展有其特征，而这正是构成民族和地域传统的内核，它的作用无处不在，不仅显现于精神领域，还表达于所创造的实体环境方面。

和栖居的要求，而不是要成为记忆符号或审美的寄托物。由于街区建立的存在意义是立足于现实需求的，那么它的存在方式和存在价值必定随着现实条件的需求而改变，更新是其发展的途径。

由此可见，传统和街区都着眼于现实的发展——传统要求创新出合乎历史渊源的现时新观念，形成新的文化凝聚结构；街区的存在价值要以能否满足现时的需求为判断标准，因此传统街区的时代化更新是必然的。从上面的分析可以看出，传统街区的存在意义被文化复合体中的某些子系统的价值网络所否定，同时又为其他子系统的价值网络所肯定，那么其存在就不能简单地依据部分系统的价值网络的评判而肯定或否定，而应由整个文化复合体的价

【传统街区的现代化更新】

传统街区的现代化更新是必然的，但更新应该遵循以下原则：

1. 立足现实需求。传统街区的存在意义是立足于现实需求的，更新应该以满足现时需求为判断标准。

2. 传承历史渊源。传统要求创新出合乎历史渊源的现时新观念，形成新的文化凝聚结构。更新不应该割断历史，而应该在传承历史的基础上创新。

3. 整体价值评判。传统街区的存在不能简单地依据部分系统的价值网络的评判而肯定或否定，而应由整个文化复合体的价值网络来评判。

4. 动态发展。传统街区的更新是一个动态的发展过程，不是一次性的、静态的。应该随着时代的发展不断调整和完善。

5. 多元共存。传统街区的更新应该允许多元文化的共存，不应该追求单一的模式和风格。

传统街区是文化复合体的重要组成部分，它承载着历史记忆和文化传统。在现代化进程中，传统街区面临着诸多挑战，但也蕴含着发展的机遇。通过科学的价值评析和合理的更新改造，传统街区可以在现代社会中焕发新的生机，成为文化传承和创新的重要载体。

网络内外的传统街区，反映了文化复合体中不同价值网络的冲突和协调。传统街区的保护与更新，需要在文化整体网络的框架下进行，综合考虑政治、社会、经济和思想四个体系的价值评判，实现传统与现代的有机统一。

English

Traditional Districts Inside and Outside the Network — Value Evaluation Inside and Outside the Overall Cultural Network

By Shan Deqi, Fan Xiaopeng

(Originally published in Architectural Journal, 2001.9)

A national or regional cultural complex includes four systems: political, social, economic, and ideological. The development and change of a cultural complex come from the development and overlap of these four systems. Any change in one branch system will not only affect the entire complex but also trigger changes in other systems, with mutual checks and balances among the systems.

[Value Network]

Under the broad background of a certain era's characteristics, a cultural complex has its overall values, thereby forming a strong atmosphere of common recognition. Each component system, situated in this overall environment, establishes its own value network coordinated with the overall values. Values are ubiquitous throughout the entire network.

Although they differ in hierarchy and areas of concern, they are inherently coherent, and their direction of influence is from the whole to the part, from the collective to the individual.

The overall values of a cultural complex cannot exist in a vacuum; they are built on the integration of the value networks of each subsystem, and the development of subsystem value networks directly promotes and drives the development and change of overall values.

If the direction of value influence from the complex to subsystems is regarded as the "positive direction," then the direction of value influence from subsystems to the entire complex is the "reverse direction" — from the individual to the collective, from the part to the whole.

At all times, both positive and reverse directions of influence coexist, and their dynamic interaction jointly constitutes the development of value concepts.

However, the influence exerted in both directions will not be fully accepted by the recipient, because there exists a value screening and acceptance mechanism between the entire cultural complex and its subsystems.

This mechanism is a complex set of value judgment standards, closely related to national or regional cultural psychology, with different emphases as the relationship between the cultural complex and its subsystems changes.

The relatively stable and coherent value network established on the basis of this mechanism serves as the judgment standard for things or events encountered.

[Traditional Districts Outside the Network]

With increased interaction and mobility of people, interpersonal relationships in the social system are no longer centered on family blood ties; changes in neighborhood relations, lifestyles, and life rhythms; coupled with changes in concepts of ethics, morality, and legal systems; and the relationship between humans and natural resources shifting from primary dependence to comprehensive utilization — all these make the settlement forms that express these relationships different from before (Fig.

7). Traditional districts can no longer承载 and reflect these changes, failing to demonstrate their practical and developmental value in the political, economic, and social systems, and therefore cannot be incorporated into their value networks. News of traditional districts being demolished appears in the media from time to time.

The main reasons are: traditional districts lack adaptability to various changes, while occupying some land resources, and are thus negated in the value evaluation of the above systems.

[Traditional Districts Inside the Network]

Considering the formation process of the ideological system, its value network is constructed through accumulation, inherited generation after generation, evolving, developing, and improving.

The value network not only reveals the unique conceptual composition of that nation and region, but also directly influences the judgment standards for real things, implying the future development direction of ideological concepts.

The occurrence, establishment, and development of the value network in the ideological system have its characteristics, which precisely constitute the core of national and regional tradition. Its influence is ubiquitous, manifesting not only in the spiritual realm but also in the created physical environment.

and dwelling requirements, rather than becoming memory symbols or aesthetic repositories. Since the existence significance of districts is based on practical needs, their mode of existence and value will inevitably change with the demands of real conditions, and renewal is their path of development.

Thus, both tradition and districts focus on practical development — tradition requires innovating contemporary new concepts consistent with historical origins, forming new cultural cohesion structures; the existence value of districts should be judged by whether they can meet contemporary needs.

Therefore, the contemporary renewal of traditional districts is inevitable. From the above analysis, the existence significance of traditional districts is negated by the value networks of some subsystems in the cultural complex, while affirmed by the value networks of other subsystems.

Therefore, their existence cannot be simply affirmed or negated based on the evaluation of partial systems' value networks, but should be judged by the value network of the entire cultural complex

[Modernization renewal of Traditional Districts]

The modernization renewal of traditional districts is inevitable, but renewal should follow the following principles:

1. based on practical needs. The existence significance of traditional districts is based on practical needs, and renewal should use meeting contemporary needs as the judgment standard.

2. Inheriting historical origins. Tradition requires innovating contemporary new concepts consistent with historical origins, forming new cultural cohesion structures. renewal should not sever history, but innovate on the basis of inheriting history.

3. Overall value evaluation. The existence of traditional districts cannot be simply affirmed or negated based on the evaluation of partial systems' value networks, but should be judged by the value network of the entire cultural complex.

4. Dynamic development. The renewal of traditional districts is a dynamic development process, not a one-time, static one. It should be continuously adjusted and improved with the development of the times.

5. Pluralistic coexistence. The renewal of traditional districts should allow the coexistence of diverse cultures, and should not pursue a single model and style.

Traditional districts are an important component of the cultural complex, carrying historical memory and cultural traditions. In the modernization process, traditional districts face many challenges but also contain development opportunities.

Through scientific value evaluation and reasonable renewal and transformation, traditional districts can焕发 new vitality in modern society, becoming important carriers of cultural inheritance and innovation.

Traditional districts inside and outside the network reflect the conflicts and coordination of different value networks within the cultural complex.

The protection and renewal of traditional districts need to be carried out under the framework of the overall cultural network, comprehensively considering the value evaluation of the four systems of politics, society, economy, and ideology, achieving organic unity of tradition and modernity.

本篇术语 Key Terms

文化复合体 / Cultural Complex 价值网络 / Value Network 传统街区 / Traditional District 网络内外 / Inside and Outside the Network 正方向逆方向 / Positive Direction / Reverse Direction 筛选和接纳机制 / Screening and Acceptance Mechanism 政治社会经济思想体系 / Political/Social/Economic/Ideological Systems 传统街区现代化更新 / Modernization Renewal of Traditional Districts 文化凝聚结构 / Cultural Cohesion Structure 时代化更新 / Contemporary Renewal 历史渊源传承 / Inheritance of Historical Origins 多元共存 / Pluralistic Coexistence

乡土的希望——从传统民居到小城镇建设
中文

乡土的希望——从传统民居到小城镇建设

单德启

（"建筑与地域文化国际研讨会"论文，2001.12）

进入快速发展的城市化和农业产业化，对传统村镇体系和传统民居带来了巨大的冲击；新世纪随着中国加入WTO以及西部大开发等将持续这种冲击。这是严峻的挑战，又是难逢的机遇，是中国乡土大地的希望。

乡土聚落和传统民居是地域文化最集中、最普遍的物质载体。"一方水土养一方人"，人们的生活起居、邻里交往、生产交换、风俗习惯乃至审美、伦理，无不打上地域的烙印。当代保留下来的中国各地传统民居，诸如北方的四合院、西北的窑洞、西南干栏木楼、江浙水乡和徽州民居等等，无不特色鲜明，是我们祖先传承下来的一份宝贵遗产。

然而我们不能不看到，这些民居的有形载体和无形内涵毕竟是建立在封闭的、自给自足的农业社会和自然经济基础上的；是建立在封建家族和以家庭为生产单位的时代。

我们珍视和尊重中国乡土聚落和传统民居，是由于它在历史上适合农耕社会生产力和生产关系，在当时人口、资源和环境的大背景下，由当时人们的功能需要和技术手段营造并传承、完善的。这种传统村镇体系和民居模式，代表着一段历史，承载着一份厚重的农业文明。

和任何事物一样，"模式"不是永恒的。《易》曰："累迁，变动不居"，又曰："大象无形"。可以这么说，在农耕社会的生产力和生产关系以及建立在它基

乃至新民居强调的适宜技术和地方材料，离不开生态资源和生态经济的支撑。传统地域文化的保护和发展，当代地域文化的创造，不建立在这种基础之上，只能是空中楼阁。理论上的探讨是重要的，然而"批判的武器"终究代替不了"武器的批判"。几代人包括成功和失败的实践，是完成时代大课题的必由之路。我们多年来针对当代乡土复杂情况，从传统民居到小城镇建设做了多方面的探索和实践：

精品乡土聚落和传统民居的保护——如安徽的西递宏村已录入世界文化遗产名录，浙江桃渚古镇已列入国家文化保护单位。这类聚落虽然也面临农民致富、乡村现代化和保存乡土"软件"的矛盾，但"挂牌"以后毕竟提高了从地方官员到乡民大众对保护传统乡土聚落和地域文化的重视。

传统乡土聚落的旅游转型——如浙江绍兴东浦水街（图7、图8）、安徽巢湖中庙渔镇。

欠发达地区扶贫更新改造——如广西融水干栏木楼寨的总体更新(图9、图10)。

城市边缘地区(城乡结

【传统民居保护的矛盾和思考】

它也是在中国还不到1亿人口的情况下发生的，它的建筑及整个城市非常优秀，从生产到生活、到精神生活都与它的载体非常和谐。但是今天大不一样了，它的物质载体与生活在其中的人已经是两码事。绍兴八字桥水街（图1），规定50米之内不准动，我带着同学一家家访问，同学告诉我，想不到，经济名县绍兴还有这么个贫民窟。他们能怎么办?拆又不能拆，翻又不能翻，还是涮马桶、烧柴火灶，然后扛着自行车翻过八字桥回家，有摩托车的就更费劲了。这个矛盾很尖锐，难就难在这儿。就是这个地方，带我们去的人都不敢讲他是市里人，老百姓要打他，我房子要倒了，你不让我动，我怎么办?这个矛盾在保护时要考虑到。

目前我国正处于经济和社会转型期，文化也要随之发展。如果说那些村落的载体都不能动，这个矛盾就会很尖锐。这个问题我痛苦了很久，我非常喜欢那些乡土文明，下去以后发现有些事确确实实不好办。例如讲干栏木楼，我们在苗寨搞了一个改造试验，当时在系里压力很大。但你心理就不平衡，那个木楼破烂不堪(图2)，为什么不盖新的，老百姓的生活惨不忍睹啊!建造一个木楼需要60-80方原木，山上树都砍光了，为什么1992年、1996年柳州两次发大水?桂北的生态环境被破坏得差不多了。人口增加那么多，又盖那么多木楼，每栋木楼60-80方还是小型的；然后烧的也是这些木头，卖的也是这些木头，受不了这么搞。

我的看法可能跟有些先生的看法不一样，腐败是有的，挪用保护款是有的，建筑师瞎搞的也是有的，但是民居作为历史进程中的矛盾是存在的。传统民居的模式，跟城市的模式，跟历史前进的趋势和现实社会生活中的内容是错了一个时代的位的。这个问题要妥善处理才能保护好。这个问题最早教育我的是我的学生。我给同学讲民居课，让他们写总结，有个同学说：民居保护是不存在的，只能说是民居建筑的保护，因为民居是建筑和人的双向交流，它不是一个壳。

你说徽州民居好、四合院好，难道还要四世同堂?将来家庭结构怎么变，还是四世同堂?所以说，要研究，要搞清楚哪些东西是值得保护的。保护的同时还要考虑保护可能带来的问题，就像上午郑孝燮先生讲的那样，一定要开辟新区，新区作为老镇区或城区的一个发展或疏导。新区就是现代设计，老区就是一个历史街区的保护。

【从传统民居到小城镇建设】

从传统民居到小城镇建设，是中国乡土大地的希望所在。具体来说：

1. 精品乡土聚落和传统民居的保护。对于具有重要历史文化价值的乡土聚落和传统民居，应该进行严格的保护，录入世界文化遗产名录或国家文化保护单位。

2. 传统乡土聚落的旅游转型。对于具有旅游价值的传统乡土聚落，可以通过旅游转型实现保护与发展的良性循环。

3. 欠发达地区扶贫更新改造。对于欠发达地区的传统民居，应该通过扶贫更新改造，改善乡民的居住条件，同时保护传统建筑文化。

4. 城市边缘地区的城乡结合。对于城市边缘地区的传统村落，应该实现城乡结合，促进城市化进程。

5. 开辟新区，保护老区。在保护传统街区的同时，应该开辟新区，作为老镇区或城区的发展或疏导。新区采用现代设计，老区进行历史街区保护。

乡土的希望，在于传统与现代的有机结合，在于保护与发展的良性循环，在于从传统民居到小城镇建设的渐进发展。中国的乡土大地，在城市化和农业产业化的浪潮中，一定能够找到适合自己的发展道路，创造出具有时代特征和地域特色的新建筑文化。

English

The Hope of the Vernacular — From traditional dwellings to small town construction

By Shan Deqi

(Paper presented at "International Symposium on Architecture and regional culture", 2001.12)

Entering the rapid development of urbanization and agricultural industrialization has brought tremendous impact to the traditional village-town system and traditional dwellings; in the new century, with China's accession to the WTO and the Great Western Development, this impact will continue. This is both a severe challenge and a rare opportunity — the hope of China's vernacular land.

vernacular settlements and traditional dwellings are the most concentrated and universal material carriers of regional culture.

"The waters and soil of a place nurture the people of that place" — people's daily life, neighborhood interactions, production exchange, customs and habits, and even aesthetics and ethics, all bear the imprint of the region.

The traditional dwellings preserved today across China, such as northern siheyuan (courtyard houses), northwest cave dwellings, southwest ganlan stilt wooden houses, Jiangnan water towns, and Huizhou dwellings, all have distinctive characteristics — a precious heritage passed down by our ancestors.

However, we must recognize that the tangible carriers and intangible connotations of these dwellings were, after all, built on the foundation of a closed, self-sufficient agricultural society and natural economy; established in the era of feudal clans and family-based production units.

We cherish and respect Chinese vernacular settlements and traditional dwellings because they were historically suited to the productive forces and relations of agricultural society.

Under the broad background of population, resources, and environment at that time, they were constructed, inherited, and perfected according to people's functional needs and technical means of the time. This traditional village-town system and dwelling model represents a period of history and carries a rich agricultural civilization.

Like everything else, "models" are not eternal. The I Ching says: "Constant change, never resting," and also: "The great form has no shape." It can be said that under the productive forces and relations of agricultural society and the foundation built upon it

and even new dwellings emphasize appropriate technology and local materials, which cannot be separated from the support of ecological resources and ecological economy. The protection and development of traditional regional culture, and the creation of contemporary regional culture, without this foundation, can only be castles in the air.

Theoretical discussion is important, but "the weapon of criticism" ultimately cannot replace "the criticism of weapons." Several generations of practice, including both successes and failures, are the inevitable path to completing the great task of the times.

Over the years, we have conducted various explorations and practices from traditional dwellings to small town construction, addressing the complex contemporary vernacular situation:

protection of premium vernacular settlements and traditional dwellings — such as Xidi and Hongcun in Anhui, which have been inscribed on the World cultural heritage List, and Taozhu Ancient Town in Zhejiang, which has been listed as a national cultural protection unit.

Although such settlements also face contradictions between farmers' prosperity, rural modernization, and preserving vernacular "software," after being "listed," they have after all raised the importance attached by local officials to villagers for the protection of traditional vernacular settlements and regional culture.

Tourism transformation of traditional vernacular settlements — such as Dongpu water street in Shaoxing, Zhejiang (Figs. 7, 8), and Zhongmiao Fishing Town in Chaohu, Anhui.

Poverty-alleviation renewal and reconstruction in underdeveloped areas — such as the overall renewal of ganlan stilt wooden house villages in Rongshui, Guangxi (Figs. 9, 10).

Urban fringe areas (urban-rural junction

[Contradictions and Reflections on traditional dwelling protection]

It also occurred when China's population was less than 100 million. Its architecture and the entire city were excellent, from production to life to spiritual life, all very harmonious with its carrier. But today it is very different — its material carrier and the people living in it are already two different things.

At Shaoxing's Bazi bridge water street (Fig. 1), regulations prohibit any changes within 50 meters. I took students to visit door to door, and students told me they never expected that Shaoxing, an economically famous county, still had such a slum. What can they do?

They can't demolish, can't renovate — still using chamber pots, burning firewood stoves, then carrying bicycles over the Bazi bridge to go home; those with motorcycles have an even harder time. This contradiction is very sharp, and that's the difficulty.

In this place, even the people who took us there didn't dare say they were from the city — the common people would beat them: "My house is about to collapse, you won't let me move it, what should I do?" This contradiction must be considered in protection.

Currently China is in a period of economic and social transformation, and culture must develop accordingly. If the carriers of those villages cannot be touched at all, this contradiction will become very sharp. This problem pained me for a long time.

I deeply love those vernacular civilizations, but after going down I found some things are truly difficult to handle. For example, regarding ganlan stilt wooden houses, we conducted a reconstruction experiment in a Miao village, which put great pressure on the department at that time.

But psychologically you feel unbalanced — that wooden house is dilapidated (Fig. 2), why not build a new one? The common people's living conditions are unbearable! Building one wooden house requires 60-80 cubic meters of logs, all the trees on the mountains have been cut down. Why were there two major floods in Liuzhou in 1992 and 1996?

The ecological environment in northern Guangxi has been almost destroyed. The population has increased so much, and so many wooden houses have been built, each requiring 60-80 cubic meters even for small ones; then they burn these same woods and sell these same woods — it cannot sustain this.

My views may differ from some gentlemen's. Corruption exists, misappropriation of protection funds exists, and architects messing things up also exists. But dwellings as contradictions in the historical process do exist.

The model of traditional dwellings, compared with the urban model, with the trend of historical progress and the content of real social life, is错位 by one era. This problem must be properly handled to ensure good protection. This problem was first taught to me by my students. I taught dwelling courses and asked students to write summaries.

One student said: dwelling protection does not exist; it can only be called the protection of dwelling architecture, because dwellings are a two-way interaction between architecture and people — it is not just a shell. You say Huizhou dwellings are good, siheyuan are good — should we still have four generations living under one roof?

How will family structures change in the future, still four generations under one roof? Therefore, we must research and clarify what things are worth protecting. While protecting, we must also consider the problems that protection may bring. As Mr.

Zheng Xiaoxie said this morning, we must definitely open up new areas, with the new area serving as development or relief for the old town or urban area. The new area uses modern design, and the old area is a historic district protection.

[From traditional dwellings to small town construction]

From traditional dwellings to small town construction is where the hope of China's vernacular land lies. Specifically:

1. protection of premium vernacular settlements and traditional dwellings. For vernacular settlements and traditional dwellings with important historical and cultural value, strict protection should be implemented, inscribed on the World cultural heritage List or national cultural protection units.

2. Tourism transformation of traditional vernacular settlements. For traditional vernacular settlements with tourism value, a virtuous cycle of protection and development can be achieved through tourism transformation.

3. Poverty-alleviation renewal and reconstruction in underdeveloped areas. For traditional dwellings in underdeveloped areas, poverty-alleviation renewal and reconstruction should improve villagers' living conditions while protecting traditional architectural culture.

4. Urban-rural integration in urban fringe areas. For traditional villages in urban fringe areas, urban-rural integration should be achieved to promote the urbanization process.

5. Opening up new areas while protecting old areas. While protecting traditional districts, new areas should be opened up as development or relief for old towns or urban areas. New areas use modern design, and old areas are protected as historic districts.

The hope of the vernacular lies in the organic combination of tradition and modernity, the virtuous cycle of protection and development, and the gradual development from traditional dwellings to small town construction.

China's vernacular land, amid the waves of urbanization and agricultural industrialization, will surely find a development path suited to itself, creating a new architectural culture with era characteristics and regional features.

本篇术语 Key Terms

乡土的希望 / The Hope of the Vernacular 传统民居到小城镇建设 / From Traditional Dwellings to Small Town Construction 地域文化物质载体 / Material Carrier of Regional Culture 一方水土养一方人 / The Waters and Soil Nurture the People 精品乡土聚落保护 / Protection of Premium Vernacular Settlements 旅游转型 / Tourism Transformation 扶贫更新改造 / Poverty-Alleviation Renewal and Reconstruction 城市边缘地区城乡结合 / Urban-Rural Integration in Urban Fringe Areas 开辟新区保护老区 / Opening New Areas, Protecting Old Areas 民居建筑的保护 / Protection of Dwelling Architecture 建筑和人的双向交流 / Two-Way Interaction Between Architecture and People 批判的武器武器的批判 / Weapon of Criticism / Criticism of Weapons
6
在"小城镇，大战略"论坛与小城镇旅游高级论坛上的两次发言
中文

在"小城镇，大战略"论坛与小城镇旅游高级论坛上的两次发言

单德启

（原载于《小城镇建设》，2000.4和2002.4）

【小城镇及村庄规划建设中历史文化城镇和古民居保护】

"小城镇，大战略"是中华民族一个巨大的、激烈的矛盾，又是一个巨大的、激烈的机遇，搞得好等于是"文艺复兴"，搞得不好一塌糊涂。

对历史文化名城、历史文化民居保护刻不容缓，第一条是赶紧把家底弄清楚，然后分出一、二、三，要搞就一定要搞，不能笼统地讲。笼统地讲讲不清楚，因为民居的保护，我多年研究体会是它有二重性。在文化层面，它是历史遗产，但是它作为住宅区来说，它是现代人的生活。我们干嘛要赶超世界?就是要使老百姓的生活好起来。那就是要改善物质的居住条件，同时保存我们的优秀文化遗产。这个问题在其他传统建筑类型里碰不到。例如故宫，绝对不会把一个机关放进去，领导不会同意，小车开不进去，电话、各种保卫也不可能嘛。

唯有民居和村镇，它居住着人。这是最大的区别。不把这个区别研究清楚，不给出路，保护就是空谈。历史文化名城、名镇、民居，它是在农业社会经济基础上发生的，它是在家族制社会形态下发生的，

【小城镇旅游开发】

小城镇旅游开发有三种类型：

第一种是本身具有历史文化价值的小城镇，如历史文化名镇、古村落等。这类小城镇的旅游开发应该以保护为主，在保护的基础上进行合理的开发利用。

第二种是为周边大的山水旅游目的地提供服务的接待型小城镇，我们曾经做过的安徽巢湖就是在巢湖风景区边上的一个小城镇，还有黄山周边的聚落；

第三种是与当地生产经营项目结合的参与型生态、度假旅游转型的小城镇。大城市周边的这种发展潜力很大。例如我曾经去过台湾两次，台湾一些少数民族聚居区的生态农业搞得很好，叫做立体农业，例如垂钓、种花、种茶叶、露营等等，周末提供帐篷，家长带着孩子去玩的很多。这几类现在我们都有搞的，也有搞得不错的，但是总的来说属于起步过程，层次不高，也有瞎搞的。

（三）小城镇的旅游开发方式

我在黄山花山迷窟开发时就提出，学术文章洞里做，旅游文章洞外做，不要在洞里搞很多鬼怪等等，要尽量保持原汁原味，要使游客有神秘感，不要闹笑话；洞外搞旅游开发(图5、图6、图7)。

所以在小城镇的旅游规划中，要规划好、引导好，而且要从体制上引导。现在村镇规划很多都不合适。旅游是一个非常复杂的问题，涉及到很多部门，有土地、文物、水利、农业等等，不能只靠镇上的几个人就搞了。一个镇的镇长要管很多事，信息量和知识量都有限，怎么知道区域怎么发展。所以必须要有资金、技术和信息等方面的支持。小城镇规划应该由更高一级的部门来做，最少也要是地级市、旅游局等来做。而且要搞高水平的规划，像深圳龙岗，花了50万元做的规划，经过前期调研、分析等等，才能做好。那些花5000元逼着设计院做的方案肯定是会有很大问题的。

另外，必须做到城乡一体考虑。有些农村问题要城市来解决。不能两方面完全割裂开，两张皮，各干各的。不能够让农民自己搞，需要一个统一规

【小城镇旅游开发的启示】

小城镇旅游开发给我们以下启示：

1. 小城镇旅游开发应该因地制宜，根据不同类型采取不同的开发方式。

2. 小城镇旅游开发应该注重保护，在保护的基础上进行合理的开发利用。

3. 小城镇旅游开发应该注重体制引导，由更高一级的部门来做规划，提供资金、技术和信息支持。

4. 小城镇旅游开发应该做到城乡一体考虑，不能将城市和农村完全割裂开。

5. 小城镇旅游开发应该注重高水平的规划，避免低水平的重复建设和盲目开发。

6. 小城镇旅游开发应该注重参与型、体验型旅游，提高旅游的层次和质量。

"小城镇，大战略"是中华民族面临的巨大挑战和机遇。小城镇的规划建设、历史文化保护和旅游开发，需要政府、建筑师、规划师和乡民的共同努力。通过科学的规划和合理的开发，小城镇一定能够成为中国城市化进程中的重要力量，成为城乡协调发展的重要纽带。

English

Two Speeches at the "small towns, Great Strategy" Forum and the Advanced Forum on small town Tourism

By Shan Deqi

(Originally published in small town construction, 2000.4 and 2002.4)

[protection of Historical and Cultural Towns and Ancient dwellings in small town and Village planning and construction]

"small towns, great strategy" is both a huge and intense contradiction for the Chinese nation and a huge and intense opportunity. If done well, it equals a "Renaissance"; if done poorly, it will be a mess.

The protection of historical and cultural cities and historical and cultural dwellings is extremely urgent. The first thing is to quickly clarify the inventory, then categorize them into levels one, two, three. If we are going to do it, we must do it well, and cannot speak in generalities.

Speaking in generalities cannot clarify things, because the protection of dwellings, based on my years of research, has a dual nature. At the cultural level, it is historical heritage, but as residential areas, it is modern people's lives. Why do we want to catch up with and surpass the world? It is to improve the lives of ordinary people.

That means improving material living conditions while preserving our outstanding cultural heritage. This problem is not encountered in other traditional building types.

For example, the Forbidden City would absolutely never have a government agency placed inside — leaders would not agree, cars cannot drive in, telephones and various security measures would be impossible. Only dwellings and villages are inhabited by people. This is the biggest difference.

Without researching this difference clearly and providing a way out, protection is empty talk. Historical and cultural cities, towns, and dwellings emerged on the basis of agricultural society and economy, and under the social form of the clan system,

[small town Tourism Development]

There are three types of small town tourism development:

The first type is small towns with inherent historical and cultural value, such as historical and cultural towns and ancient villages. The tourism development of such small towns should focus on protection, with reasonable development and utilization on the basis of protection.

The second type is reception-type small towns serving surrounding large landscape tourism destinations. The Anhui Chaohu project we worked on is a small town beside the Chaohu Scenic Area, and there are also settlements around Huangshan;

The third type is participatory ecological and vacation tourism transformation small towns combined with local production and business projects. The development potential around big cities is great.

For example, I have been to Taiwan twice, where the ecological agriculture in some ethnic minority settlements is very well done, called three-dimensional agriculture, such as fishing, flower growing, tea planting, camping, etc., providing tents on weekends, with many parents taking their children to play.

These types are now being developed in our country, some done quite well, but overall they are in the initial stage, with low levels and some blind development.

(3) Tourism Development Methods for small towns

When developing the Huashan Mystery Cave near Huangshan, I proposed: academic articles should be done inside the cave, tourism articles should be done outside the cave. Don't put many ghosts and monsters inside the cave, try to maintain the original flavor, make tourists feel a sense of mystery, and don't make a laughingstock; do tourism development outside the cave (Figs. 5, 6, 7).

Therefore, in the tourism planning of small towns, we must plan well and guide well, and guide from the institutional level. Current village and town planning is often inappropriate. Tourism is a very complex issue involving many departments — land, cultural relics, water conservancy, agriculture, etc.

— and cannot be done by just a few people in the town. A town mayor has many things to manage, with limited information and knowledge, so how can they know how the region should develop? Therefore, support in terms of funding, technology, and information is necessary.

small town planning should be done by higher-level departments, at least by prefecture-level cities and tourism bureaus. And high-level planning must be done. Like Longgang in Shenzhen, they spent 500,000 yuan on planning, with preliminary research and analysis, to do it well.

Those plans that cost 5,000 yuan and force design institutes to do them will definitely have major problems.

In addition, urban-rural integrated consideration must be achieved. Some rural problems need to be solved by cities. The two sides cannot be completely separated, like two skins, each doing its own thing. Farmers cannot be left to do it themselves; a unified plan is needed

[Enlightenment from small town Tourism Development]

small town tourism development gives us the following enlightenment:

1. small town tourism development should adapt to local conditions, adopting different development methods according to different types.

2. small town tourism development should focus on protection, with reasonable development and utilization on the basis of protection.

3. small town tourism development should focus on institutional guidance, with higher-level departments doing the planning and providing funding, technology, and information support.

4. small town tourism development should achieve urban-rural integrated consideration, and cannot completely separate cities and rural areas.

5. small town tourism development should focus on high-level planning, avoiding low-level redundant construction and blind development.

6. small town tourism development should focus on participatory and experiential tourism, improving the level and quality of tourism.

"small towns, great strategy" is a huge challenge and opportunity facing the Chinese nation. The planning and construction, historical and cultural protection, and tourism development of small towns require the joint efforts of the government, architects, planners, and villagers.

Through scientific planning and reasonable development, small towns will surely become an important force in China's urbanization process and an important link for coordinated urban-rural development.

本篇术语 Key Terms

小城镇大战略 / Small Towns, Great Strategy 历史文化城镇保护 / Historical and Cultural Town Protection 古民居保护二重性 / Dual Nature of Ancient Dwelling Protection 小城镇旅游开发 / Small Town Tourism Development 接待型小城镇 / Reception-Type Small Town 参与型生态度假旅游 / Participatory Ecological Vacation Tourism 花山迷窟 / Huashan Mystery Cave 巢湖风景区 / Chaohu Scenic Area 城乡一体考虑 / Urban-Rural Integrated Consideration 高水平规划 / High-Level Planning 学术文章洞里做旅游文章洞外做 / Academic Articles Inside Cave, Tourism Articles Outside Cave 立体农业 / Three-Dimensional Agriculture
7
滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇规划初探——结合巢湖市中庙镇总体规划实例
中文

滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇规划初探——结合巢湖市中庙镇总体规划实例

单德启 王小斌

（原载于《清华大学学报》，2002.1）

随着经济发展和改革开放的不断深入，全国各地撤乡并镇，迁村并点的步伐加快。在新的经济形势下，风景旅游区小城镇的规划建设面临着机遇和挑战。这些风景旅游区小城镇（包括滨湖旅游区小城镇），由于缺乏科学的城镇规划，造成了风景旅游资源和景观生态的严重破坏，建设用地极大浪费，乱搭乱建，低层次的"小煤窑"式的重复开发，破坏性开发建设和建设性的破坏屡见不鲜，镇区大量极有历史文化价值的庙堂建筑和民居建筑被拆毁。因此，我们深感，必须把滨湖风景旅游区小城镇规划工作做好，引导其可持续发展。

如何保护滨湖风景旅游区的自然资源、人文景观和生态资源以及民居遗产，塑造旅游特色并加以现代化的表现，以旅游促生产，并带动城镇经济的全面发展，解决近期低水平低档次建设和远期高档次高水平建设的矛盾，是我们必须思考的问题。同时，如何借鉴发达国家先进的经验和方法，更好地保护、继承和弘扬地区传统文化，创造优美的景观生态环境，改善人们的生活质量，提高居民的文化素质和公众参与意识，从硬件和软件全方位建设中，把观念、制度和文化设施的建设纳入城镇规划建设系统工程中来，真正做到旅游兴镇。如果没有正确思想指导规划和建设，想建设景色优美、功能完善齐全、健康持续发展的小城镇是不可能的，至少存在着缺陷。

巢湖位于江淮之间，地跨合肥、巢湖二市和肥东、肥西、庐江三县。巢湖又称金湖，巢湖因湖面形态呈鸟巢状故名巢湖，面积约8平方公里，是我国五大淡水湖之一，1987年巢湖被列入安徽省级风景名胜区。中庙镇位于巢湖市居巢区的西部，巢湖北岸，似半岛状伸入巢湖。中庙镇依山傍水，临湖而立。现状，建成区0.51平方公里，镇区总人口4386人。而距中庙镇2.4公里的姥山岛，具有三山九峰之景，海拔105米，岛上生态植被保护良好，环境优美，是一望无际的

住。巢湖及中庙地区的水文化博大精深，丰富精彩，巢湖烟波浩瀚，景色秀丽，湖面波光粼粼，渔帆点点，岸边港汉弯弯，加上水中的姥山岛、文峰塔以及遥相对应的凤凰台上的中庙镇，风光如画。由阳光、沙滩、柳林、港湾、水岛、渔帆、古建组成了一幅绝妙的天然山水画的景观特色。中国古代山水文化对城市规划产生过重大影响，换言之，古代城镇具有的特色是长期受山水文化影响的结果。在中国人的传统观念里，水被看成具有灵性和人性之物，它代表了天地自然智慧和美德神物总称，水文化是社会文化总体中一个重要组成部分。

在中庙镇规划中（图2），制定以下原则：(1)特色原则。在对中庙镇、姥山岛旅游景区的区位分析、自身特色、资源、环境、社会经济情况等深入细致调查分析的基础上，抓住生态观光旅游这个主线，以线带面，确立中庙镇生态旅游独具特色的自然整体氛围，民俗风情，建设风貌等。(2)传统风貌的保护和更新原则。中庙寺院、昭忠祠和白衣庵等建筑群要严格保护，在修建过程中，整旧如旧，保持其原有的建筑特色，这也是中庙镇旅游景区中独占性的人文景源。同时对其周围的地形、地貌、植被等加以保护。

【自然生态与系统】

然资源是吸引旅游者的最根本力量"，可见保护自然生态系统对旅游业发展的重要性。对生态系统的剖析研究，能为旅游城镇的规划设计提供精髓的思想和原则。

（一）自然生态与系统

自然生态系统就是包括特定地段中的全部生物和物理环境的统一体，著名生态学家E·P·奥德姆1971年指出：地球上有无数大大小小的生态系统，大至整个生物圈（biosphere）、整个海洋、大陆、湖泊，小到一片森林、一片草地、一个小池塘都可以看成是一个自然生态系统。中庙镇的总体规划中的自然生态系统就包含巢湖湖泊，姥山岛森林，中庙镇的岗地、圩滩和池塘、湿地系统，尤其是姥山岛独特的森林生态（图3），在整个华东区域都是稀有的。这是兴办旅游产业的资源和依据。

自然生态系统是开放的"自持系统"，它所需要的能源是生产者对光能的"巧妙"转化，消费者取食植物，而动、植物残体和代谢物通过分解又归还到环境（土壤）中，重新供植物利用。整个自然生态系统除了有能量流动和物质循环的关系外，还存在着信息的传递。信息传递是指生态系统中各生命成分之间及生命成分与环境之间都存在信息传递，也就是说整个自然生态系统中能量流和物质流的行为由信息决定，物质流和能量流是信息流的载体。

从自然生态系统的剖析中可以看到，生态平衡具有重要的意义，生态平衡是生态系统在一定时期内结构和功能的相对稳定状态，其物质和能量的输入、输出近似相等。在外来干扰下，能通过自我调节（或人为控制）恢复到原初稳定状态。生态平衡是动态的，维护生态的平衡不只是保持其原始状态。所以生态系统在人为有益的状态下，可以建立新的平衡，能达到更合理的结构，更高效的功能和更好的生态效益。而当外来干扰超过生态系统自我调节能力，而不能恢复到原初状态称为生态失调，即生态平衡遭到破坏，系统中各群落，组织将在失衡中灭亡或遭重创。

今天，随着人类技术力量的不断强大，人类对自然的高强度开发使得自然生态系统屡遭破坏。美国学者W·福格特在《生存之路》一书中阐述了人类和自然生态环境的紧张关系，强调人类要维护和恢复自然生态的平衡，为争取明天的生存和发展做准备。1970年初，罗马俱乐部发表《增长的极限》报告，高呼人类面临经济增长与环境质量下降的生态危机。在这之后不久，联合国发表《只有一个地球》，呼吁人类面对全球自然生态危机的险境，揭开了世界大规模系

【滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇规划的启示】

滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇规划给我们以下启示：

1. 滨湖风景旅游区小城镇规划应该以保护自然资源、人文景观和生态资源为前提，不能以牺牲环境为代价换取经济发展。

2. 规划应该注重特色原则，抓住生态观光旅游这个主线，确立独具特色的自然整体氛围和民俗风情。

3. 规划应该注重传统风貌的保护和更新原则，对有历史文化价值的建筑严格保护，整旧如旧。

4. 规划应该注重自然生态系统的保护和维护，实现生态平衡。

5. 规划应该注重可持续发展，解决近期低水平低档次建设和远期高档次高水平建设的矛盾。

6. 规划应该注重公众参与，提高居民的文化素质和公众参与意识。

滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇是中国小城镇体系中的重要组成部分，具有独特的自然景观和文化资源。通过科学的规划和合理的开发，滨湖园林旅游风景区小城镇一定能够实现保护与发展的良性循环，成为中国小城镇建设的典范。
