A Brief Discussion on Sustainable Development of Historical and Cultural Districts — Reflections and Explorations on the conservation planning of Lu Xun's Hometown in Shaoxing

By Shan Deqi

(Originally published in Taiwan Architecture Magazine, No. 089, 2003.5)

(1) Yue Capital Shaoxing is one of China's first batch of 24 famous historical and cultural cities, with over 4,000 years of history and 2,500 years of city-building history.

From Yu the Great controlling floods, Goujian sleeping on brushwood and tasting gall, to Emperor Gaozong of the Southern Song arriving in Yue and "continuing the Zhongxing prosperity," until the prosperity of the Ming and Qing dynasties, it has constructed a profound history of the ancient city.

The ancient city began with the Yue City in the Spring and Autumn period, renovated through successive dynasties, with its pattern remaining unchanged — rare in the history of Chinese urban construction.

The city has a crisscrossing river network with numerous bridges, known for "three mountains, ten thousand households, winding alleys; a hundred bridges, a thousand streets, crisscrossing waters." "Small bridges, flowing water, households" is the metaphor for the city's characteristics, and "black, white, gray" is the sublimation of the city's colors (Fig.

1).

Shaoxing, "nurtured by its spiritual landscape," has written an extremely profound cultural history: scholar culture, private secretary culture, Duimin (degraded class) culture, bridge culture, "taimen" (local traditional dwelling) culture, wine culture, etc., from elegant to vulgar, from material to spiritual levels

typical "taimen" courtyard houses for tourists to admire, with water piers added for tourist use.

(3) The reason historical and cultural districts receive more attention is that they are also places where more "outsiders" visit and tour. The reason heritage is precious is that it has the functions of display and enlightenment, and cannot simply be enshrined. Modern tourism activities provide a platform for realizing this function.

Shaoxing has become an important part and even a tourist destination on the Hangzhou tourism route: Lu Xun's Hometown, Shen Garden (Fig. 10), Keqiao Ancient Town, and a large number of other historical and cultural attractions have great appeal both at home and abroad.

Lu Xun's Hometown, through the physical environment and historical relics, displays Lu Xun's life and spirit through pictures, especially the natural and social environment that nurtured the great man,凝聚 the cultural tradition of the Chinese nation — both broad-minded and emotionally delicate, both bold and graceful.

In Shaoxing, this is Lu Xun, yet more than Lu Xun — from the patriotic poet Lu You to the heroine Qiu Jin, it can be said to be in the same lineage. Characteristics are the life of tourism, culture is the core of characteristics, and tradition is the foundation of culture.

The concentrated refinement of characteristics, culture, and tradition, supplemented by corresponding mechanisms and service facilities, appropriately integrated into the district, can be regarded as another sign of the vitality of historical and cultural districts.

In our conservation and development planning, on the one hand, we maximally protect and restore cultural relic buildings and the historical environment in their original flavor, restoring pedestrian streets and even stone slab pavements, renovating waterways and other historical environment features; on the other hand, we also integrate necessary facilities for convenient tourism activities into the district, such as parking lots at the east and west ends, rest pavilions and corridors along the river side of the pedestrian street, water piers, and small items for guidance, signage, and explanation; as for tourism routes — the organization of walking tours and black-awning boat tours, is even more in the plan

[Reflections and Explorations on conservation planning of Historical and Cultural Districts]

The reflections and explorations on the conservation planning of Lu Xun's Hometown in Shaoxing give us the following enlightenment:

1. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should focus on protecting and restoring cultural relic buildings and the historical environment in their original flavor, restoring pedestrian streets, stone slab pavements, renovating waterways, and other historical environment features.

2. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should integrate necessary facilities for convenient tourism activities into the district, such as parking lots, rest pavilions and corridors, water piers, guidance signage, etc.

3. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should focus on the organization of tourism routes, including the organization of walking tours and black-awning boat tours.

4. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should focus on the concentrated refinement of characteristics, culture, and tradition, supplemented by corresponding mechanisms and service facilities, appropriately integrated into the district.

5. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should focus on the functions of display and enlightenment, and cannot simply enshrine heritage. Modern tourism activities provide a platform for realizing this function.

6. The conservation of historical and cultural districts should focus on sustainable development, achieving a virtuous cycle of conservation and development.

Lu Xun's Hometown in Shaoxing is a typical representative of Chinese historical and cultural districts, carrying rich historical memories and cultural traditions. Through scientific conservation planning and reasonable development and utilization, Lu Xun's Hometown will surely achieve sustainable development, becoming a model for the conservation of Chinese historical and cultural districts.

从传统乡土民居聚落到现代人居环境——关于住区环境和居住品质
中文

从传统乡土民居聚落到现代人居环境——关于住区环境和居住品质

单德启 杨绪波

（原载于《百年建筑》，2003.8）

从人居环境的视角看传统聚落。"天人合一，物我同一"的中国哲学思想反映着人与环境的协调关系。在聚落的选址、布局、住宅的设计和营建中追求天、地、人的和谐，强调人与物的同构，从物质的层面到精神的层面，把"外适内和"当成住区环境和居住品质的最高境界。下面从人居环境的视角，试分析一二。

（一）依山傍水与自然山水环境融合

依山傍水是传统乡土民居聚落最基本的原则之一。聚落依山就势，负阴抱阳，这就拥有了大自然赋予人类的一切生机：良好的地势，充沛的阳光，不竭的源泉，绿色的视野。

民居傍水而建，取水之灵秀（图1）。以浙江水乡民居聚落为例，江南自古就有"人间天堂"的美誉，民居与河水形成一河一街，或一河二街的空间关系。又如安徽徽州民居聚落适应水流的形态形成跨水、傍水、顺应溪水弯曲的布局。宏村前有溪流环绕形如"腰带水"，村中有绕家穿巷的水圳。

聚落依山而筑，三面群山环绕，南面敞开，聚落争取更多的阳光和通风。建筑物的大面积开窗均朝南北向，结合当地地域气候特点，设外墙出檐遮阳及屋顶棚架等外遮阳方式，减少外墙吸热。

（二）淡雅的色彩

淡雅的色彩，几乎是民居的天然品质。

传统乡土民居几乎都是就地、因材施工，这就与地域自然色彩如西北黄土、江南青绿融为一体，打成一片。

如北京紫禁城皇家建筑，顶部是黄色琉璃瓦、墙是红色，基座是白色，鲜明而煊赫的色彩显示了皇家的风范与威严；而普通老百姓的民宅则一律灰砖青瓦，淡雅古朴；江南水乡，则是白粉墙、小青瓦。淡雅的色彩不仅带给人宁静平和的

更深入挖掘华立水乡建筑的文化品位，把握水乡特色和尺度关系，创造宜人的空间环境。住宅建筑处理以二层尺度手法，沿水底层立面处理采用利用商店架空层公共空间、储藏间、南向游廊、斜屋面等手法，创造了宜人的建筑尺度，使建筑空间更加丰富，在空间上反映出水乡居住氛围，以白色的建筑立面为主基调，配以淡赭色的底层基座和窗台栏杆木结构装修，整体呈现清新淡雅的色彩旋律，淡淡透射出睿智，恰如其分的表现水乡的特性。

利用建筑物的首层架空及空中穿透部分，引入自然风；利用内庭院形成对流，增加建筑物通风换气性能。尽可能方便住户与自然环境的沟通，使开放性楼梯与户外活动有便捷联系。绿化的共生性，积极采用平台绿化、垂直绿化、窗下花台绿化，与小区组团绿地相辅相存，融为一体。（图7、图8）

【从传统乡土民居聚落到现代人居环境的启示】

从传统乡土民居聚落到现代人居环境——关于住区环境和居住品质给我们以下启示：

1. 现代人居环境应该追求"天人合一，物我同一"的中国哲学思想，反映人与环境的协调关系。

2. 现代住区环境和居住品质应该追求天、地、人的和谐，强调人与物的同构，从物质的层面到精神的层面，把"外适内和"当成最高境界。

3. 现代人居环境应该注重依山傍水与自然山水环境融合，聚落依山就势，负阴抱阳，拥有大自然赋予人类的一切生机。

4. 现代人居环境应该注重淡雅的色彩，与地域自然色彩融为一体，打成一片。

5. 现代人居环境应该注重挖掘地域建筑的文化品位，把握地域特色和尺度关系，创造宜人的空间环境。

6. 现代人居环境应该注重通风换气性能和绿化的共生性，方便住户与自然环境的沟通。

从传统乡土民居聚落到现代人居环境，是中国建筑文化传承和创新的重要课题。通过深入研究传统乡土民居聚落的智慧和经验，结合现代生活需求和技术手段，一定能够创造出具有中国特色和时代特征的现代人居环境。

English

From Traditional Vernacular dwelling Settlements to Modern human settlements — On residential environment and living quality

By Shan Deqi, Yang Xubo

(Originally published in Centenary Architecture, 2003.8)

Looking at traditional settlements from the perspective of human settlement environment. The Chinese philosophical thought of "unity of heaven and humanity, identity of subject and object" reflects the harmonious relationship between humans and the environment.

In the site selection, layout, residential design, and construction of settlements, the harmony of heaven, earth, and humanity is pursued, emphasizing the isomorphism of humans and things, from the material level to the spiritual level, regarding "external adaptation and internal harmony" as the highest realm of residential environment and living quality.

Below, from the perspective of human settlement environment, let me analyze one or two points.

(1) Nestling Under Mountains and Beside Waters, Integrating with Natural landscape environment

Nestling under mountains and beside waters is one of the most basic principles of traditional vernacular dwelling settlements. Settlements follow the mountain terrain, embracing the yin and facing the yang, which endows them with all the vitality that nature bestows upon humanity: favorable terrain, abundant sunlight, inexhaustible springs, and green views.

dwellings are built beside water, taking the elegance of water (Fig. 1). Taking Zhejiang water town dwelling settlements as an example, Jiangnan has been known as "paradise on earth" since ancient times. dwellings and rivers form a spatial relationship of one river with one street, or one river with two streets.

Another example is Anhui Huizhou dwelling settlements, which adapt to the form of water flow to form layouts spanning water, beside water, and following the curves of streams. Hongcun has a stream surrounding it in front, shaped like a "belt water," and water channels winding through homes and alleys in the village.

Settlements are built against mountains, surrounded by mountains on three sides, open to the south, striving for more sunlight and ventilation. Large-area windows of buildings face north-south, combined with local regional climate characteristics, with exterior wall eaves for shading and roof canopies and other external shading methods, reducing exterior wall heat absorption.

(2) Elegant and Simple Colors

Elegant and simple colors are almost the natural quality of dwellings.

Traditional vernacular dwellings are almost all constructed locally and according to materials, which integrates with regional natural colors such as the loess of the northwest and the green of Jiangnan, merging into one.

For example, the imperial architecture of the Forbidden City in Beijing has yellow glazed tiles on top, red walls, and white bases — the bright and prominent colors display the imperial style and majesty; while the residences of ordinary people are all gray bricks and blue tiles, elegant and simple; in Jiangnan water towns, there are white plaster walls and small blue tiles.

Elegant and simple colors not only bring people tranquility and peace

Further explore the cultural taste of Huali water town architecture, grasp water town characteristics and scale relationships, and create a pleasant spatial environment. Residential building treatment uses a two-story scale approach.

The facade treatment along the water's ground floor uses methods such as utilizing the elevated public space of shops, storage rooms, south-facing verandas, and sloped roofs, creating a pleasant architectural scale, enriching the architectural space, and reflecting the water town living atmosphere in space.

With white building facades as the main tone, complemented by light ochre ground floor bases and window sill railing wood structure decoration, the overall presentation is a fresh and elegant color melody, faintly radiating wisdom, appropriately expressing the characteristics of the water town.

Utilize the ground floor elevation and空中 penetration parts of buildings to introduce natural wind; utilize internal courtyards to form convection, increasing the ventilation and air exchange performance of buildings.

Facilitate communication between residents and the natural environment as much as possible, making open stairs have convenient connections with outdoor activities.

The symbiosis of greening — actively adopt platform greening, vertical greening, and flower bed greening under windows, complementing and coexisting with the community group green space, merging into one (Figs. 7, 8).

[Enlightenment from Traditional Vernacular dwelling Settlements to Modern human settlements]

From traditional vernacular dwelling settlements to modern human settlements — on residential environment and living quality gives us the following enlightenment:

1. Modern human settlement environments should pursue the Chinese philosophical thought of "unity of heaven and humanity, identity of subject and object," reflecting the harmonious relationship between humans and the environment.

2. Modern residential environment and living quality should pursue the harmony of heaven, earth, and humanity, emphasizing the isomorphism of humans and things, from the material level to the spiritual level, regarding "external adaptation and internal harmony" as the highest realm.

3. Modern human settlement environments should focus on nestling under mountains and beside waters, integrating with natural landscape environment. Settlements follow the mountain terrain, embracing the yin and facing the yang, possessing all the vitality that nature bestows upon humanity.

4. Modern human settlement environments should focus on elegant and simple colors, integrating with regional natural colors and merging into one.

5. Modern human settlement environments should focus on exploring the cultural taste of regional architecture, grasping regional characteristics and scale relationships, and creating a pleasant spatial environment.

6. Modern human settlement environments should focus on ventilation and air exchange performance and the symbiosis of greening, facilitating communication between residents and the natural environment.

From traditional vernacular dwelling settlements to modern human settlements is an important topic for the inheritance and innovation of Chinese architectural culture.

Through in-depth research on the wisdom and experience of traditional vernacular dwelling settlements, combined with modern living needs and technical means, we will surely create modern human settlement environments with Chinese characteristics and era features.

本篇术语 Key Terms

传统乡土民居聚落 / Traditional Vernacular Dwelling Settlement 现代人居环境 / Modern Human Settlement Environment 住区环境和居住品质 / Residential Environment and Living Quality 天人合一物我同一 / Unity of Heaven and Humanity, Identity of Subject and Object 外适内和 / External Adaptation and Internal Harmony 依山傍水 / Nestling Under Mountains and Beside Waters 负阴抱阳 / Embracing Yin and Facing Yang 淡雅的色彩 / Elegant and Simple Colors 腰带水 / Belt Water 水圳 / Water Channel 华立水乡建筑 / Huali Water Town Architecture 绿化共生性 / Symbiosis of Greening

旅游设施与景观环境
中文

旅游设施与景观环境

单德启

说是一种真善美的顿悟，人们从这种顿悟中获得了对自己、对社会生存和发展本质的体验。这种顿悟在许多文学作品中俯拾皆是。李白在黄鹤楼感叹："孤帆远影碧空尽，惟见长江天际流。"尽又不尽，诗人决不正是在写景，他看到了人的永恒。王勃在滕王阁写出"秋水共长天一色，落霞与孤鹜齐飞"的绝唱，抒发了人类携手并进、和谐发展的愿望。这种情景交融、寄情于物的文学表达，无疑对景观资源升华为陶冶情操、净化灵魂带来难以替代的价值。

哲学的思考更是一种理性的顿悟。被称为中国最古老又最年青的哲学图像太极图，居然是国际基本粒子学术会议的会徽。万事万物就是在相互依存中发展的，没有黑就没有白，没有你就没有我。风景资源没有开发，就不存在保护的问题；保护的提出，就足以承认必要的开发、必要的利用为前提。而这种开发和利用，使更多的人能享受到它的价值，也才能使风景成为资源。

历史的思考常常被误认为怀旧、保守，实际上这种思考太重要了。认识和探究一个事物的来龙去脉，对于价值判断和价值取向可以说是必不可少的。《徐霞客游记》的"游黄山记"中，记述了几百年前他登攀黄山的艰难经历。山路没有、宾馆没有、安全设施没有，那确实很少有开发、很少有建设，但同时也可以说许多景观在人迹罕至的地方也不存在。十几年前，黄山开发了登天都峰的新道，既缓解了登山一条路拥挤不堪、走回头路的弊端，又开发了许多新景点。从徐霞客登山无路可行到开辟新的登山道，都将汇成一条历史的轨迹，留给后人无尽的思考、无穷的启迪。

（三）人文思考的本质就是发现世界、发现人、发现人和世界相互关系的本源

世界就是不断演变的，人也是在不断演变的；人和物质世界的关系也是在不断变化和调整的。人文思考就是以人为本，一切随时间、地点、条件的变化而变化的价值重估，而不是形而上学的多元价值的叠加和排斥。人类文明就是会有新事物、新因素不断凸现，我们应该正视它、承认它、容纳它；容纳的前提就是社会重塑一个建立在人文思考支撑上的价值评估体系。

旅游设施和景观环境的话题，从物质技术的层面或角度看，有很多议题，也有很多专业学问，比如环境保护、景观取舍、旅游市场、投资效益、景区建设、体制管理等等，等等。但是它们的背后都必须有一个正确的理念，正确的思考问题的途径和方法，这就是人文思考。说穿了就是一切要体现对人的关怀，对更多的人的更多关怀，对今天的人和将来的人的关怀；当然，也包含着对人的生存和发展的天地环境的关怀。

旅游设施与景观环境

（一）一曰"统"，对立统一："虽由人作，宛自天成"

中国文化深刻地影响着风景区内旅游设施与景观环境的关系，这从名山佛寺如九华山、峨嵋山等可以得到许多启发。"万物与我合一"，要求自身与自然合为一体，顺应自然高于矫揉造作，丘园泉石长于院落笙歌。所谓"外适内和"这一"适"一"和"，是保护、开发、利用风景资源的一份极宝贵的传统经验。

建于20世纪80年代初的黄山"云谷山庄"忠实地诠释了这一思想，如云谷山庄的选址藏而不露，布局与山水打成一片，体量化整为零，庭园跨水溪保留山涧野趣，等等。（图3、图4）

可以展开来说，这种"中和"体现在：

——人为场所自然化，自然之物人格化；所谓移情于物，借物抒情。

——人与自然的相互依存、相互渗透、相互转化。

（二）二曰"和"，中和："和，故百物皆化"

——道路，"山无道则不活"（图12）；

——亭，"空亭翼然，吐纳云气"，"江山无限景，都聚一亭中"（图13）；

——楼，昆明大观楼有长联"五百里滇池，奔来眼底……"，"九千年往事，注到心头……"；

——院，"十笏茅斋，一方天井，修竹数竿，石笋数尺，然风中雨中有声，日中月中有影，诗中酒中有情，闲中闷中有伴，非唯我爱竹石，即竹石也爱我也。"（郑板桥句）

——窗，"轩楹高爽，窗户邻虚，纳千顷之汪洋，收四时之烂漫"（图14）。

然而，这些抒发的情感，又是"环境"景象的触发，所以《礼记》认为："人之心动，物使之然也"。

风景区的旅游设施，我们不应当只把它看作是一个经济的平台，它更是一个文化的平台，把人和景观环境联系在一起，通过自身的功能与形象，像触媒剂一样，起到陶冶情操、教化品德的作用，这是任何书本和政治报告所无法替代的。

"意"和"文"密不可分，照我的理解，"意境"和"文采"表述了不同意思，但如果意境缺乏文采，显然品格不高；而文采没有高深的意境，也可能流于游戏，这不是在钻文字的牛角尖，而是对风景区的保护与利用，对旅游设施决策和设计，都有重要影响。

仅举黄山"花山谜窟"为例，最早有"石窟"、"古窟"等各种命名方案，后来定为"谜"，一个"谜"字，不仅具有了市场效应，而且提升了景观的品位；我曾在花山谜窟开发初期，向黄山老总进言："旅游文章洞外做，学术文章洞内做"，蒙得采纳；意思也是不宜在洞内搞经济旅游设施和人工之物，保留"一团

【旅游设施与景观环境的启示】

旅游设施与景观环境给我们以下启示：

1. 旅游设施与景观环境的关系应该追求"虽由人作，宛自天成"的境界，实现人与自然的和谐统一。

2. 旅游设施与景观环境的关系应该追求"和，故百物皆化"的中和境界，实现人与自然的相互依存、相互渗透、相互转化。

3. 风景区的旅游设施不应该只被看作是一个经济的平台，它更是一个文化的平台，把人和景观环境联系在一起。

4. 旅游设施应该通过自身的功能与形象，像触媒剂一样，起到陶冶情操、教化品德的作用。

5. "意"和"文"密不可分，意境和文采都很重要，对风景区的保护与利用，对旅游设施决策和设计，都有重要影响。

6. 旅游设施的命名和设计应该注重文化品位，提升景观的品位。

旅游设施与景观环境是中国传统建筑文化的重要组成部分，体现了人与自然和谐相处的哲学思想。通过科学的规划和合理的设计，旅游设施一定能够与景观环境实现有机统一，成为中国建筑文化的瑰宝。

English

Tourism Facilities and landscape environment

By Shan Deqi

It can be said to be a kind of epiphany of truth, goodness, and beauty, from which people gain an essential experience of themselves and of social survival and development. Such epiphanies are everywhere in many literary works.

Li Bai sighed at the Yellow Crane Tower: "The lone sail's distant shadow vanishes into the blue sky, only the Yangtze River flowing to the horizon remains." Endless yet ending — the poet was certainly not merely describing scenery; he saw the eternity of humanity.

Wang Bo wrote at the Tengwang Pavilion the masterpiece: "Autumn waters share the same color as the endless sky, sunset clouds fly together with the lone wild goose," expressing the aspiration for humanity to advance hand in hand and develop harmoniously.

Such literary expressions of blending scene and emotion, placing feelings in objects, undoubtedly bring irreplaceable value to the sublimation of landscape resources into cultivating temperament and purifying the soul.

Philosophical thinking is even more a kind of rational epiphany. The taiji diagram, known as China's oldest yet youngest philosophical image, is surprisingly the emblem of an international elementary particle academic conference. All things develop in interdependence; without black there is no white, without you there is no me.

Without development of scenic resources, the issue of protection does not exist; the very proposal of protection is sufficient to acknowledge necessary development and necessary utilization as prerequisites. And this kind of development and utilization enables more people to enjoy its value, and only then can scenery become a resource.

Historical thinking is often mistaken for nostalgia and conservatism, but in fact this kind of thinking is extremely important. Understanding and exploring the origin and development of a thing is arguably indispensable for value judgment and value orientation.

In "Travel to Huangshan" from The Travel Diaries of Xu Xiake, he recorded the difficult experience of climbing Huangshan hundreds of years ago.

No mountain paths, no hotels, no safety facilities — there was indeed very little development and very little construction, but at the same time it can be said that many landscapes did not exist in places rarely visited by people.

Over a decade ago, Huangshan developed a new path to Tiandu Peak, which both alleviated the problem of overcrowding on one climbing path and the disadvantage of retracing one's steps, and developed many new scenic spots.

From Xu Xiake climbing with no path to the opening of new climbing paths, all will merge into a historical trajectory, leaving endless thoughts and infinite enlightenment for future generations.

(3) The Essence of Humanistic Thinking is to Discover the World, Discover People, and Discover the Origin of the Relationship Between People and the World

The world is constantly evolving, and people are also constantly evolving; the relationship between people and the material world is also constantly changing and adjusting.

Humanistic thinking is people-oriented, a revaluation of all values that changes with time, place, and conditions, rather than a metaphysical superposition and exclusion of multiple values.

Human civilization will have new things and new factors constantly emerging, and we should face them squarely, acknowledge them, and accommodate them; the premise of accommodation is that society reshapes a value evaluation system built on the support of humanistic thinking.

The topic of tourism facilities and landscape environment, from the material and technical level or perspective, has many issues and many professional disciplines, such as environmental protection, landscape trade-offs, tourism market, investment benefits, scenic area construction, institutional management, and so on.

But behind them all there must be a correct concept, a correct way and method of thinking about problems — this is humanistic thinking.

To put it bluntly, everything must reflect care for people, more care for more people, care for people today and people in the future; of course, it also includes care for the heaven and earth environment in which people survive and develop.

Tourism Facilities and landscape environment

(1) First Called "Unity," Opposites in Unity: "Though Made by Humans, As If Created by Nature"

Chinese culture has profoundly influenced the relationship between tourism facilities and landscape environment in scenic areas, which can be much inspired by famous mountain Buddhist temples such as Jiuhua Mountain and Emei Mountain.

"All things and I are one," requiring oneself to be integrated with nature, conforming to nature is superior to artificial affectation, and hills, gardens, springs, and rocks are superior to courtyard music.

The so-called "external adaptation and internal harmony" — this "adaptation" and this "harmony" — is an extremely precious traditional experience for the protection, development, and utilization of scenic resources.

The "Yungu Villa" in Huangshan, built in the early 1980s, faithfully interprets this thought. For example, the site selection of Yungu Villa is hidden and not exposed, the layout integrates with mountains and waters, the volume is broken into parts, and the courtyard spans streams to preserve the wild charm of mountain ravines, etc. (Figs. 3, 4)

It can be expanded to say that this "harmony" is embodied in:

— Human places being naturalized, natural things being personalized; so-called placing feelings in objects, expressing feelings through objects.

— The interdependence, mutual penetration, and mutual transformation of humans and nature.

(2) Second Called "Harmony," the Mean: "Harmony, Therefore All Things Transform"

— Roads: "A mountain without paths is not alive" (Fig. 12);

— Pavilions: "An empty pavilion stands gracefully, breathing in and out clouds and mist," "The infinite scenery of rivers and mountains all gathers in one pavilion" (Fig. 13);

— Towers: Kunming Daguan Tower has a long couplet: "Five hundred li of Dianchi Lake, rushing to the eyes...," "Nine thousand years of past events, pouring into the heart...";

— courtyards: "A ten-inch thatched study, one square patio, several bamboo poles, a few feet of stone shoots, yet in wind and rain there is sound, in sun and moon there is shadow, in poetry and wine there is emotion, in leisure and melancholy there is companionship; not only do I love bamboo and stone, but bamboo and stone also love me." (Zheng Banqiao's quote)

— Windows: "High and bright pillars and eaves, windows adjacent to emptiness, embracing a thousand hectares of vast waters, gathering the splendor of four seasons" (Fig. 14).

However, these expressed emotions are triggered by the "environment" scene, so The Book of Rites says: "People's hearts are moved, caused by things."

Tourism facilities in scenic areas should not be regarded merely as an economic platform; they are even more a cultural platform, linking people and the landscape environment together. Through their own functions and images, like a catalyst, they play a role in cultivating temperament and moral character — this is irreplaceable by any books or political reports.

"Meaning" and "literary grace" are inseparable. In my understanding, "artistic conception" and "literary grace" express different meanings, but if artistic conception lacks literary grace, its character is obviously not high; and literary grace without profound artistic conception may also become mere play.

This is not splitting hairs over words, but has important implications for the protection and utilization of scenic areas, and for the decision-making and design of tourism facilities.

Take Huangshan's "Huashan Mystery Cave" as an example. Initially there were various naming proposals such as "Stone Cave" and "Ancient Cave," later it was named "Mystery" — one word "mystery" not only has market effect but also elevates the taste of the landscape.

In the early development of Huashan Mystery Cave, I once advised the general manager of Huangshan: "Tourism articles should be done outside the cave, academic articles inside the cave," which was adopted; the meaning is also that it is not appropriate to build economic tourism facilities and artificial objects inside the cave, preserving "a mass of

[Enlightenment from Tourism Facilities and landscape environment]

Tourism facilities and landscape environment give us the following enlightenment:

1. The relationship between tourism facilities and landscape environment should pursue the realm of "though made by humans, as if created by nature," achieving harmonious unity between humans and nature.

2. The relationship between tourism facilities and landscape environment should pursue the mean realm of "harmony, therefore all things transform," achieving the interdependence, mutual penetration, and mutual transformation of humans and nature.

3. Tourism facilities in scenic areas should not be regarded merely as an economic platform; they are even more a cultural platform, linking people and the landscape environment together.

4. Tourism facilities should, through their own functions and images, like a catalyst, play a role in cultivating temperament and moral character.

5. "Meaning" and "literary grace" are inseparable; both artistic conception and literary grace are important, with important implications for the protection and utilization of scenic areas, and for the decision-making and design of tourism facilities.

6. The naming and design of tourism facilities should focus on cultural taste, elevating the taste of the landscape.

Tourism facilities and landscape environment are important components of traditional Chinese architectural culture, embodying the philosophical thought of harmonious coexistence between humans and nature. Through scientific planning and reasonable design, tourism facilities will surely achieve organic unity with the landscape environment, becoming a treasure of Chinese architectural culture.

画家·画室·画境 构思·构图·构建——从黄山艺术家中心方案设计看徽州当代乡土建筑创作
中文

画家·画室·画境 构思·构图·构建——从黄山艺术家中心方案设计看徽州当代乡土建筑创作

单德启 袁牧

（作于2003年11月，修改于2004年5月）

【时空一体的乡土建筑观】

中国乡土民居源远流长，有着灿烂的历史和辉煌的成就。对比当今国内建筑设计领域普遍存在的设计粗糙、缺乏特色以至千城一面的状况，具有鲜明地方特色又曾经极为合理、实用的乡土民居不仅仅是一种能给我们一般建筑实践以借鉴的珍贵的历史遗存，更是一个生机勃勃而范围广阔、正需要我们投身其中的建筑领域。在乡土民居的研究中，我们一般会把不同地点但风格类似的建筑归为一个地域整体来研究，却较少地把同一地区的传统民居和当代建筑看作一个整体。

实际上，如果我们把某一地域的特色民居看作一个在相当地域范围内不断繁衍、进化的整体，我们就不会把传统民居和新的地区建筑对立起来；从而会发现，其实新老建筑本来就是一个整体，只是随时代延伸而生长、变化，并在不同时代体现出不同的局部特征。

所以说中国建筑师有责任不仅要保护好、利用好乡土民居这份"遗产"，更要创造出当代和未来的新乡土建筑。根植于本乡本土的特殊环境去探索新乡土建筑，无疑是中国现代建筑从平庸、模仿中"突围"的重要途径。所以黄山艺术家中心的设计委托就成为一个创造新乡土建筑的绝好机会。

2002年5月，我们接到黄山汤口镇一位画家的委托，要在黄山风景区大门外的汤口镇设计一个"黄山艺术家接待中心"。"黄山无小事"（"黄山无小事，事事通北京"是流传于安徽省建设厅、黄山市政府和黄山管委会等管理者和建设者中的一句戏言，体现了他们对于黄山建设严肃认真的态度），因为位处要冲，所

际上，由于画家本身从事的特殊画种——壁画的创作，更使这一方案的基本功能要求变得饶有趣味。画幅一般都在1米见方，宽度常常达到2米，而比较大的甚至达到4米以上。最大的壁画创作达到27米×3米巨大尺寸。根据接待各地前来写生交流的画家的要求，画室将被用来进行中西多种不同画种的创作要求。相应的也需要不同的展览空间，以及艺术家交流聚会的沙龙。正是这些内部艺术功能的要求，启发了我们对整个建筑的室内外空间的特性和形态进行独特的设计。

（三）画境

最具特色的乡土建筑，往往来自于最具特色的乡土环境。这里的环境，不仅仅包括自然环境，更包括当地的历史渊源、文化传统、艺术风格等等人文环境；这里的环境也不止是一个背景，而应该是创作的基本前提和主要源泉。乡土建筑和聚落最动人的地方，也正是它们所表现出来的那种画一般的意境。建筑因环境而生，也创造出更好的环境。

提及徽州，首先涌进我们脑海里的是黄山的奇丽风景（图2）、

这里最重要的是把自然、建筑的各个部分当作一个统一的整体，而且是具有统一生命的不断循环代谢的一个整体，而不仅仅是对自然的一般调和、适应或者利用。这一观念对于整个黄山景区的建设尤其具有积极的指导意义，建筑背后的山体，穿越地段的山涧，前部的小河，都被紧密纳入到建筑构图之内，建筑的各个立面造型都是与环境形象同步设计的。特别是在地段内，我们发现可以直接看到黄山诸峰，令人欣喜。经过仔细调整，最后的方案里主要的庭院和客房都可以直望黄山，这也算景观和自然一体考虑的一点吧（图8）。

2. 徽州文化

徽州是朱熹的故乡，开创了发达的新安理学，对整个徽州文化有着深远的影响；新安画派的山水画影响着徽州的审美趣味；还有徽州的版画、篆刻、戏剧、盆景都直接影响到建筑的形象和装饰。这里的乡土建筑无论从外在形态还是内在气质，都需要从地方文化里汲取营养，在本设计中，主要是寻找理学的严整秩序和山水长卷的气韵生动之间的平衡。而相应的徽州传统雕刻、盆景都尽可能予以应用。

3. 徽州传统建筑

这是我们将要创作的当代徽州乡土的"根"。一方面大量的历史遗存给我们很多直接的设计材料和元素，另一方面也很可能掉入简单重复传统符号的陷阱，被传统形式的表象所迷惑，而忘记了更加内在和更有价值的传统。这里主要汲取的是传统徽居形态的"虚实相生"的审美趣味：

（1）朴素自然

功能、材料、工艺、色彩、造型都出于自然和实际需要，朴素真实而不做作，这样的美是真实而永久的，也恰好符合现代主义的建筑理念。整个建筑使用白色涂料，深色砖雕是取材传统，而采用自然实用的平屋顶更是取材传统的精神。

（2）有机随机

利用不同的客观条件和功能需要因地制宜，创造自由变幻的建筑形态，不但

徽州传统乡土建筑的精髓？！

——尚未结束的思考和实践

（一）思考——什么是徽州传统乡土建筑的精髓？

(1) 和山水打成一片：自然、生态、建筑……天人合一，理念、手法，概括为这一点。

(2) 宜人的尺度：适合人类居住生活，按居住者的需求和工艺建造，发自本来需要的，当然尺度宜人。

(3) 组合的体量：天井、马头墙，韵律的美，节奏的美，时间的因素，随时空展开的画卷，都落实到这里了。

(4) 淡雅的色彩：中国画"以墨代色"，徽州建筑的审美趣味，材料的忠实反映以及功能的实际要求完美地统一了。

(5) 少而精的装饰：徽州的装饰含义丰富，装饰结合功能，结合结构，结合文化，实在是点睛之笔。

以上是我们在设计开始之前总结的徽州民居的内在特征，并在整个设计过程中得到了尽可能地实践，效果是明显的。只不过当我们再深入研究，可以发现这五点之外，还需要更多地思考。

首先可以想见，既然传统是一个不断流动向前发展的过程，那么必定也没有一个固定不变的答案。"在乡土民居这一量大面广的领域内，形态、风格、技艺、风俗乃至更深层的精神信仰、心理因素等等被称为文化一类的东西，或迟或早、或多或少都要被改变。"不但如此，而且时代也在改变着我们自身的思维方法和价值观念。不过在这个永恒变化的过程中，我们还是可能抓住一些相对稳定和有价值的东西——一个临时的答案。而这个答案也会有复杂的层次：其实整个这

【徽州当代乡土建筑创作的启示】

从黄山艺术家中心方案设计看徽州当代乡土建筑创作给我们以下启示：

1. 中国建筑师有责任不仅要保护好、利用好乡土民居这份"遗产"，更要创造出当代和未来的新乡土建筑。

2. 根植于本乡本土的特殊环境去探索新乡土建筑，是中国现代建筑从平庸、模仿中"突围"的重要途径。

3. 乡土建筑和聚落最动人的地方，是它们所表现出来的那种画一般的意境。建筑因环境而生，也创造出更好的环境。

4. 徽州传统乡土建筑的精髓包括：和山水打成一片、宜人的尺度、组合的体量、淡雅的色彩、少而精的装饰。

5. 传统是一个不断流动向前发展的过程，没有一个固定不变的答案。在永恒变化的过程中，我们还是可能抓住一些相对稳定和有价值的东西。

6. 当代徽州乡土建筑创作应该从地方文化里汲取营养，寻找理学的严整秩序和山水长卷的气韵生动之间的平衡。

徽州当代乡土建筑创作是中国建筑文化传承和创新的重要课题。通过深入研究徽州传统乡土建筑的精髓，结合现代生活需求和艺术功能，一定能够创造出具有徽州特色和时代特征的新乡土建筑。

English

painter · studio · painting realm conception · composition · construction — Viewing Contemporary Huizhou Vernacular Architectural Creation from the Design Scheme of Huangshan Artist Center

By Shan Deqi, Yuan Mu

(Written November 2003, revised May 2004)

[Time-space Integrated vernacular architecture View]

Chinese vernacular dwellings have a long history, with brilliant history and splendid achievements.

Compared with the current situation of rough design, lack of characteristics, and even a thousand cities with one face generally existing in the domestic architectural design field, vernacular dwellings with distinctive local characteristics and once extremely reasonable and practical features are not only a precious historical heritage that can provide reference for our general architectural practice, but also a vibrant and broad architectural field that requires our devotion.

In the research of vernacular dwellings, we generally classify buildings in different locations but with similar styles as a regional whole for research, but rarely regard traditional dwellings and contemporary architecture in the same region as a whole.

In fact, if we regard the characteristic dwellings of a certain region as a whole that continuously reproduces and evolves within a considerable regional scope, we will not oppose traditional dwellings and new regional architecture; thus we will find that in fact old and new buildings are originally one whole, only growing and changing with the extension of the times, and embodying different local characteristics in different eras.

Therefore, Chinese architects have the responsibility not only to protect and utilize the "heritage" of vernacular dwellings well, but also to create new vernacular architecture for the present and future.

Exploring new vernacular architecture rooted in the special environment of the local homeland is undoubtedly an important way for Chinese modern architecture to "break through" from mediocrity and imitation. Therefore, the design commission of Huangshan Artist Center became an excellent opportunity to create new vernacular architecture.

In May 2002, we received a commission from a painter in Tangkou Town, Huangshan, to design a "Huangshan Artist Reception Center" in Tangkou Town outside the gate of Huangshan Scenic Area.

"Nothing in Huangshan is trivial" ("Nothing in Huangshan is trivial, everything connects to Beijing" is a joke circulating among managers and builders such as the Anhui Provincial Department of construction, Huangshan Municipal Government, and Huangshan Management Committee, reflecting their serious attitude toward Huangshan construction), because of its strategic location, the

In fact, due to the special painting genre the painter himself engages in — mural creation — the basic functional requirements of this scheme became even more interesting. paintings are generally about 1 meter square, with widths often reaching 2 meters, and larger ones even reaching over 4 meters.

The largest mural creation reaches a huge size of 27 meters × 3 meters. According to the requirements of receiving painters from various places coming for sketching and exchange, the studios will be used for creation requirements of various Chinese and Western painting genres.

Correspondingly, different exhibition spaces and salons for artist exchange and gatherings are also needed. It is precisely these internal artistic functional requirements that inspired us to conduct unique design of the characteristics and forms of the entire building's interior and exterior spaces.

(3) painting realm

The most distinctive vernacular architecture often comes from the most distinctive vernacular environment.

The environment here includes not only the natural environment, but also the local historical origin, cultural tradition, artistic style, and other humanistic environments; the environment here is not just a background, but should be the basic premise and main source of creation.

The most touching aspect of vernacular architecture and settlements is precisely the painting-like artistic conception they express. Architecture is born from the environment and also creates a better environment.

Mentioning Huizhou, the first thing that comes to mind is the magnificent scenery of Huangshan (Fig. 2),

The most important thing here is to treat all parts of nature and architecture as a unified whole, and a whole with unified life that continuously circulates and metabolizes, not merely general harmonization, adaptation, or utilization of nature.

This concept has particularly positive guiding significance for the construction of the entire Huangshan scenic area. The mountain behind the building, the mountain stream crossing the site, and the small river in front are all closely integrated into the architectural composition.

The facade forms of all parts of the building are designed synchronously with the environmental image. Especially within the site, we found that we can directly see the peaks of Huangshan, which is delightful.

After careful adjustment, in the final scheme, the main courtyards and guest rooms can directly look at Huangshan — this can be considered a point of integrated consideration of landscape and nature (Fig. 8).

2. Huizhou Culture

Huizhou is the hometown of Zhu Xi, who founded the developed Xin'an Neo-Confucianism, which has had a profound influence on the entire Huizhou culture; the landscape paintings of the Xin'an painting school influence Huizhou's aesthetic taste; and Huizhou's printmaking, seal carving, drama, and miniatures all directly affect the image and decoration of architecture.

The vernacular architecture here, whether in external form or internal temperament, needs to draw nourishment from local culture. In this design, the main approach is to find a balance between the rigorous order of Neo-Confucianism and the vivid charm of landscape scrolls.

Correspondingly, traditional Huizhou carvings and miniatures are applied as much as possible.

3. Traditional Huizhou Architecture

This is the "root" of the contemporary Huizhou vernacular architecture we are about to create.

On the one hand, a large number of historical remains give us many direct design materials and elements; on the other hand, it is also easy to fall into the trap of simply repeating traditional symbols, being confused by the appearance of traditional forms, and forgetting the more internal and valuable tradition.

Here, the main absorption is the aesthetic taste of "virtual and real generating each other" in traditional Huizhou dwelling forms:

(1) Simple and Natural

Function, materials, craftsmanship, color, and form all arise from nature and actual needs, simple and真实 without affectation. Such beauty is real and permanent, and also恰好 conforms to modernist architectural concepts. The entire building uses white paint, dark brick carvings draw from tradition, and the use of natural and practical flat roofs is even more drawing from the spirit of tradition.

(2) Organic and Random

Utilizing different objective conditions and functional needs to adapt to local conditions, creating freely changing architectural forms, not only

The Essence of Traditional Huizhou vernacular architecture?!

— Unfinished Reflections and Practice

(1) Reflection — What is the essence of traditional Huizhou vernacular architecture?

(1) Integrating with mountains and waters: nature, ecology, architecture... unity of heaven and humanity, concepts and techniques, summarized as this point.

(2) Pleasant scale: suitable for human living, built according to the needs of residents and craftsmanship, arising from inherent needs, naturally with pleasant scale.

(3) Composed volumes: patios, horse-head walls, rhythmic beauty, beauty of cadence, factors of time, scrolls unfolding with time and space — all are realized here.

(4) Elegant and simple colors: Chinese painting "uses ink to represent color," the aesthetic taste of Huizhou architecture, the faithful reflection of materials, and the actual requirements of function are perfectly unified.

(5) Few but refined decorations: Huizhou decorations have rich meanings, combining function, structure, and culture — truly the finishing touch.

The above are the internal characteristics of Huizhou dwellings we summarized before starting the design, and they were practiced as much as possible throughout the entire design process, with obvious results. However, when we research more deeply, we find that beyond these five points, more thinking is needed.

First of all, it is conceivable that since tradition is a continuously flowing and developing process, there must be no fixed answer.

"In the vast field of vernacular dwellings, forms, styles, techniques, customs, and even deeper spiritual beliefs, psychological factors, etc., which are called culture, will be changed sooner or later, more or less." Not only that, but the times are also changing our own thinking methods and values.

However, in this eternal process of change, we may still grasp some relatively stable and valuable things — a temporary answer. And this answer will also have complex levels: in fact, this entire

[Enlightenment from Contemporary Huizhou Vernacular Architectural Creation]

Viewing contemporary Huizhou vernacular architectural creation from the design scheme of Huangshan Artist Center gives us the following enlightenment:

1. Chinese architects have the responsibility not only to protect and utilize the "heritage" of vernacular dwellings well, but also to create new vernacular architecture for the present and future.

2. Exploring new vernacular architecture rooted in the special environment of the local homeland is an important way for Chinese modern architecture to "break through" from mediocrity and imitation.

3. The most touching aspect of vernacular architecture and settlements is the painting-like artistic conception they express. Architecture is born from the environment and also creates a better environment.

4. The essence of traditional Huizhou vernacular architecture includes: integrating with mountains and waters, pleasant scale, composed volumes, elegant and simple colors, and few but refined decorations.

5. Tradition is a continuously flowing and developing process, with no fixed answer. In the eternal process of change, we may still grasp some relatively stable and valuable things.

6. Contemporary Huizhou vernacular architectural creation should draw nourishment from local culture, finding a balance between the rigorous order of Neo-Confucianism and the vivid charm of landscape scrolls.

Contemporary Huizhou vernacular architectural creation is an important topic for the inheritance and innovation of Chinese architectural culture. Through in-depth research on the essence of traditional Huizhou vernacular architecture, combined with modern living needs and artistic functions, we will surely create new vernacular architecture with Huizhou characteristics and era features.

"历史碎片"的现代包容——安徽池州孝肃街"历史风貌"的保护与更新
中文

"历史碎片"的现代包容——安徽池州孝肃街"历史风貌"的保护与更新

单德启 王心邑

（原载于《小城镇建设》，2004.5）

【"孝肃街"——"南湖杏园"历史和现实的碰撞】

孝肃街——"历史的碎片，城市的记忆"：孝肃街是一条位于安徽省池州市的老街，大约有100年的历史，全长200米。老街的格局、尺度尚在，但与城市肌理的联系完全失去。街的南段，有部分民国及解放前的老建筑保存较好，而北段新建、改建建筑较多（图1、图2、图3）。街上几乎每幢房屋都经过不同程度的改建或加建，没有文保单位。据当地居民反映，个别建造于20世纪30至40年代，大部分建造于民国末年和解放初期，历史并不悠久。

客观地讲，孝肃街的历史价值就全国或徽州地区来讲不算突出，而且规模较小，孤零零的点缀在现代楼群之中，有如历史的碎片，只带给人们些许回忆。然而，它是省级文化名城池州市仅存的一条老街，其如何发展对池州市有着十分重要的意义。

南湖杏园——现代化住区：南湖杏园住宅小区II期是城市建设中一个房地产开发项目，用地范围包括孝肃街及其周围一共3公顷的用地，孝肃街贯穿基地南北。开发商曾在该基地南侧和东南侧开发了南湖杏园小区I期工程。

历史与现实的碰撞：一个是待更新的传统街区历史碎片，一个是待开发的房地产项目，两者不

· 规模较小。达不到一定规模，寥寥几幢房子无法构成强烈的历史环境气氛，所以才称其为片段。

这样的历史风貌片段成因是多方面的，就我国的情况而言，既有历史上的原因，也有经济上的原因。有的是既成事实，因为中国特殊的历史发展过程，封建王朝的反复更替，连年战争和自然灾害，很多历史区域和建筑都遭到破坏，只能从历史记载中查考，至今只留下一些残留和局部片断散布在城市中；有的是因为对历史保护工作缺乏重视，在现阶段城市开发建设中，有些历史地段和古迹由于破坏性建设被轻易拆除。这种状态的改变需要全社会的共同关注和监督。这些残片在历史文化遗迹丰富的地区也许不算什么，根本无法引起人们的注意。但在历史文化遗存不那么丰富的城市里，这可能是仅存的历史见证，如再不加以保护很可能也会消失殆尽。

对"孝肃街"这一"历史风貌片段"该如何保护呢？

（1）保护场所的风貌与特色

事实上任何一个文明古国，如埃及、中国，即使是"历史保护区"或者"历史地段"，相对这个国家的整体，也可以说是"国家或民族的历史风貌片段"。这里只是尺度的不同，或者所论及的范围大小不同，它们均可以反映城市的文明。

特别是对于那些历史遗存较少的城市，这样的"片段"更显得难能可贵。然而由于历史风貌片段的尺度较小，即使完整的保护下来也很难形成历史回归的氛围。因此，在对待这些"文明的碎片"时，要着重考虑的是保护其传统的风貌特色，而不是机械地保留建筑物本身，特别是当房屋的结构等已经无法继续维持下去时，更没必要营建大量的仿古建筑以获得协调和连续，因为少量的真实遗存在一堆假古董中提供给人们的只能是虚假的信息。

当然如果经过专家考证和鉴定，"碎片"中确能反映一段历史中真实的功能、独特的材料和结构、无可替代的建筑装修装饰；或有一段历史名人的生活和"业绩"，而被冠以"文物"者，当然要尽可能地修旧如旧的复原。这里指的不是这种情况。

保护历史风貌片段的风貌特色，主要包括以下内容：首先，要保护和延续其原有的空间结构，体现在传统的道路格局上；其次，要保护原有的空间尺度感觉，包括建筑物的体量高度、街道的宽度等，这些显示了建筑物与外部空间的关系，体现了城市肌理；第三，要保护空间的界面特征，包括立面符号、装饰主题、窗洞布局大小、色彩、材料等。

保护的同时也要更新，主要包括两方面：一方面是为改善环境质量所作的更新，包括房屋的结构、构造、基础设施和市民生活设施等；另一方面是为了更好的保护传统风貌所做出的更新，包括拆除破坏景观的建筑物，对年久失修的历史建筑物进行局部改造等。

（2）发挥使用功能，保持活力，促进发展

遵照"原真性、整体性到可持续性"的原则，坚持新就是新，老就是老，尽量不搞假古董，而是以"新"延续"旧"。整条街采取新旧拼贴的办法从秩序中获得统一：北段以新建为主，保留部分状况较好的老建筑，南段则以保留整饬为主。从南到北，逐渐由历史建筑过渡到现代建筑，通过对共有地域特色的挖掘而达到风貌的协调（图7）。

【"历史碎片"的现代包容的启示】

"历史碎片"的现代包容——安徽池州孝肃街"历史风貌"的保护与更新给我们以下启示：

1. 对于历史遗存较少的城市，仅存的历史风貌片段更显得难能可贵，应该加以保护。

2. 历史风貌片段的保护应该着重考虑保护其传统的风貌特色，而不是机械地保留建筑物本身。

3. 保护历史风貌片段的风貌特色，主要包括保护和延续其原有的空间结构、保护原有的空间尺度感觉、保护空间的界面特征。

4. 保护的同时也要更新，包括为改善环境质量所作的更新和为了更好的保护传统风貌所做出的更新。

5. 应该遵照"原真性、整体性到可持续性"的原则，坚持新就是新，老就是老，尽量不搞假古董，而是以"新"延续"旧"。

6. 可以采取新旧拼贴的办法从秩序中获得统一，通过对共有地域特色的挖掘而达到风貌的协调。

"历史碎片"的现代包容是中国历史文化街区保护的重要课题。通过科学的保护和合理的更新，历史风貌片段一定能够在现代社会中焕发新的生机，成为城市记忆的重要载体。

English

Modern Inclusion of "Historical Fragments" — protection and renewal of "Historical Style" in Xiaosu Street, Chizhou, Anhui

By Shan Deqi, Wang Xinyi

(Originally published in small town construction, 2004.5)

["Xiaosu Street" — The Collision of History and Reality in "Nanhu Xingyuan"]

Xiaosu Street — "Fragments of history, memory of the city": Xiaosu Street is an old street located in Chizhou City, Anhui Province, with a history of about 100 years and a total length of 200 meters. The pattern and scale of the old street still exist, but its connection with the urban fabric has been completely lost.

In the southern section of the street, some old buildings from the Republic of China period and before liberation are well preserved, while in the northern section there are more newly built and renovated buildings (Figs. 1, 2, 3).

Almost every house on the street has been renovated or added to varying degrees, and there are no cultural relic protection units. According to local residents, a few were built in the 1930s-1940s, and most were built in the late Republic of China period and early liberation, with not a long history.

Objectively speaking, the historical value of Xiaosu Street is not outstanding nationally or in the Huizhou region, and its scale is relatively small, standing alone among modern building clusters like a fragment of history, bringing people only a few memories.

However, it is the only remaining old street in Chizhou City, a provincial-level cultural famous city, and how it develops is of great significance to Chizhou City.

Nanhu Xingyuan — Modern residential area: Nanhu Xingyuan Residential Community Phase II is a real estate development project in urban construction. The land scope includes Xiaosu Street and its surrounding area, totaling 3 hectares, with Xiaosu Street running north-south through the site.

The developer had previously developed Nanhu Xingyuan Community Phase I on the south and southeast sides of the site.

Collision of history and reality: One is a historical fragment of a traditional district to be renewed, the other is a real estate project to be developed — the two are not

· Small scale. Without reaching a certain scale, a few houses cannot constitute a strong historical environment atmosphere, hence they are called fragments.

The causes of such historical style fragments are multifaceted. In China's case, there are both historical and economic reasons.

Some are established facts — due to China's special historical development process, repeated changes of feudal dynasties, successive wars, and natural disasters, many historical areas and buildings were destroyed, and can only be researched from historical records, leaving only some remnants and local fragments scattered in cities today; some are due to lack of attention to historical protection work.

In current urban development and construction, some historical areas and historic sites were easily demolished due to destructive construction. Changing this situation requires the common attention and supervision of the whole society.

These remnants may not count for much in areas rich in historical and cultural relics, and simply cannot attract people's attention. But in cities with less abundant historical and cultural remains, this may be the only surviving historical witness, and if not protected, it may also disappear completely.

How should the "historical style fragment" of "Xiaosu Street" be protected?

(1) Protecting the Style and Characteristics of the Place

In fact, any ancient civilization, such as Egypt or China, even a "historical protection zone" or "historical area," relative to the whole country, can be said to be a "national or ethnic historical style fragment." Here it is only a difference in scale, or the size of the scope discussed, and they can all reflect the civilization of the city.

Especially for cities with fewer historical remains, such "fragments" are even more valuable. However, due to the small scale of historical style fragments, even if completely preserved, it is difficult to form an atmosphere of historical return.

Therefore, when dealing with these "fragments of civilization," the focus should be on protecting their traditional style characteristics, rather than mechanically preserving the buildings themselves, especially when the structure of the houses can no longer be maintained.

There is even less need to build a large number of antique buildings to achieve coordination and continuity, because a small amount of real relics among a pile of fake antiques can only provide people with false information.

Of course, if through expert research and appraisal, the "fragment" can indeed reflect the real function, unique materials and structure, irreplaceable architectural decoration in a period of history; or has the life and "achievements" of a historical figure, and is crowned as a "cultural relic," of course it should be restored as old as possible.

This is not the case referred to here.

Protecting the style characteristics of historical style fragments mainly includes the following: First, protect and continue its original spatial structure, reflected in the traditional road pattern; second, protect the original sense of spatial scale, including the volume and height of buildings, the width of streets, etc., which show the relationship between buildings and external space and reflect the urban fabric; third, protect the interface characteristics of the space, including facade symbols, decorative themes, window layout size, color, materials, etc.

protection should also be accompanied by renewal, mainly including two aspects: on the one hand, renewal to improve environmental quality, including house structure, construction, infrastructure, and civic living facilities; on the other hand, renewal made to better protect the traditional style, including demolishing buildings that damage the landscape, and partially renovating dilapidated historical buildings.

(2) Exercising Use Functions, Maintaining Vitality, and Promoting Development

Following the principles of "authenticity, integrity, and sustainability," adhere to the principle that new is new and old is old, try not to create fake antiques, but use the "new" to continue the "old." The entire street adopts the method of old-new collage to achieve unity from order: the northern section is mainly new construction, retaining some old buildings in better condition, while the southern section is mainly retention and renovation.

From south to north, it gradually transitions from historical buildings to modern buildings, achieving style coordination through the exploration of shared regional characteristics (Fig. 7).

[Enlightenment from Modern Inclusion of "Historical Fragments"]

Modern inclusion of "historical fragments" — protection and renewal of "historical style" in Xiaosu Street, Chizhou, Anhui gives us the following enlightenment:

1. For cities with fewer historical remains, the only surviving historical style fragments are even more valuable and should be protected.

2. The protection of historical style fragments should focus on protecting their traditional style characteristics, rather than mechanically preserving the buildings themselves.

3. Protecting the style characteristics of historical style fragments mainly includes protecting and continuing their original spatial structure, protecting the original sense of spatial scale, and protecting the interface characteristics of the space.

4. protection should also be accompanied by renewal, including renewal to improve environmental quality and renewal made to better protect the traditional style.

5. We should follow the principles of "authenticity, integrity, and sustainability," adhere to the principle that new is new and old is old, try not to create fake antiques, but use the "new" to continue the "old."

6. The method of old-new collage can be adopted to achieve unity from order, achieving style coordination through the exploration of shared regional characteristics.

Modern inclusion of "historical fragments" is an important topic for the protection of Chinese historical and cultural districts. Through scientific protection and reasonable renewal, historical style fragments will surely焕发 new vitality in modern society, becoming important carriers of urban memory.

本篇术语 Key Terms

历史碎片 / Historical Fragments 现代包容 / Modern Inclusion 孝肃街 / Xiaosu Street 池州 / Chizhou (Anhui) 历史风貌片段 / Historical Style Fragment 南湖杏园 / Nanhu Xingyuan 原真性整体性可持续性 / Authenticity, Integrity, Sustainability 新旧拼贴 / Old-New Collage 以新延续旧 / Using the New to Continue the Old 空间结构空间尺度界面特征 / Spatial Structure, Spatial Scale, Interface Characteristics 城市肌理 / Urban Fabric 假古董 / Fake Antiques

后记
中文

后记

这本论文集，是从近二十多年来发表在期刊和学术会议上的六十余篇文章中编选的。付稿之后，有些话还想说说：

第一，所谓学术研究，我认为要源自实践，又参与和指导实践，尤其是如建筑学这类应用性强、实践性强的学科。我在这二十多年中，无论是承担科研项目、研究生教学和学科建设，都离不开大大小小的实践工程。作为高等学校，参与和承担实际工程项目是要挑选的，其价值取向应该是我们国家城乡规划与建设中的热点、焦点、难点；选项选题就应有学术眼光，不是什么事都要做，尤其不能"见钱眼开"，以报酬为第一出发点。

比如本书所属的领域，实际情况是包括学术界在内的各界就争议不断，传统和现代、保护与更新、全球与地域、经典与时尚，等等，比一般的规划与设计争议更多。研究性项目既有风险又有难度，而这些热点、焦点和难点，我认为正是学术理论能够突破的生长点。

第二，学术研究和学科理论的建设是一个过程，是动态的、发展的，研究者们本身在研究、探索的过程中也在不断重塑自我，调整和优化自己的价值取向和思维模式。二十多年来，我在本领域中也经历了一个这样的心路历程。20世纪70年代末，清华大学应邀为黄山风景区作规划，同时为适应黄山风景区的保护与开发，以及旅游业的发展，在第一轮建设中策划了云谷索道和云谷山庄。

我随我的老师汪国瑜教授承担了云谷景区和云谷山庄设计，首先就将其定位为具有徽派（黄山地属徽州）特色的、有传统风貌的现代旅游建筑；同时，地处景区内它应该也是一座景观园林式的建筑。当时老师、学生、协作的工程师们都先后调研和考察了徽州聚落和徽州民居，从此打开了研究传统民居的大门。所谓"生态、形态和情态的有机统一"，民居建筑的二重性，从表象到结构到哲学观念三层次的提法，"民居传统"和"传统民居"概念的界定等等，都是由云谷山庄设计实践引发的不断探索而形成的理念。

1987年申报国家自然科学基金，系里大力支持《中国民居——人与居住环境》课题，在研究过程中碰到的大量尖锐矛盾是保护与更新改造的问题。课题本身就把民居聚落和人、环境连在一起，诸多社会、经济、人文、生态领域的交叉和综合就成为学术研究深入人无法绕过的话题。在结合贫困山区的苗寨改建和发达地区的绍兴水乡规划中，几个反复下来，诸凡"原封不动不可能，推倒重来不合理"，"少量保护，大量更新改造"，"历史街区是历史的，又是当代的；是街区的，又是城市的"等等理论探索的思想也顺应而生。

村镇建设再深入一些，不可避免地涉及到小城镇问题，城乡一体化问题，这也是基金项目研究从《中国传统民居聚落的保护与更新》到《城市化和农业产业化背景下的城镇结构更新》的必然走向。

这是一个学术研究"时间历程"、"空间范畴"和"内涵拓展"的合乎逻辑的发展。我曾经说过这样的话：如果说我为民居做了一些工作，不如说是民居做了我的工作。民居研究过程改变了建筑师的"自我"，改变了"一切从构图出发"的封闭和单向的思维模式；人们改造世界，这过程也在重塑人，真是千真万确的。

第三，在这二十多年中，我本人并没有想建立什么理论体系、学术构架，更没有奢望成什么"学派"，我只是想把自己融入我们这个时代——一个对往昔学术理论挑战的充满矛盾和生机的时代，做好自己能做的事。记得很多年前，已故的汪坦教授就对我讲过这样的话：一个人在人类文明的历史长河中，能作出一点点贡献，就非常值得、非常不容易、非常了不起了。这句话至今对我影响很深；因此，我常常不敢称道那些"著作等身"、自以为是前无古人后无来者的、时不时创建某某理论、体系的先生们。

我还想到若干年前一位研究生的论文题目是《空》，老师问他什么意思，是不是空间的"空"？他说不是；那是什么意思？讲不清楚。或许是"空故纳万境"吧，"空"到这里，什么也没有了。我对这个年青人敢于冒险稍稍有些好感，但更多的是为他走上一条不食人间烟火的玄之又玄"找不到北"的状态悲哀。当今的时代充满生机，也充满陷阱；我衷心希望年青一代能清醒地看到这个时代的"时代病"——"泡沫"和"旁若无人"、"唯我独尊"。

总之，"学术要走向实践"、"学术研究就是物我互动的过程"、"学术成果仅仅是沧海一粟"，这是我本人执意以这本书为例，想告诉读者最重要的几点体会。如果这能引起读者关注这些学术文字"之外"的"心路"，本人当非常非常欣慰。

感谢中国建材工业出版社的领导、责编、美编各位同志的关心、支持和帮助，许多方面，他们都尊重作者自己近乎固执的意见，非常不容易。郁枫同志和袁牧同志为本书的选编、选图、版面、封面设计和校正付出了辛勤的汗水，志之以表谢意。

单德启

2004年8月于清华园

English

Postscript

This collection of papers is selected from more than sixty articles published in journals and academic conferences over the past twenty-plus years. After submitting the manuscript, there are some things I would still like to say:

First, so-called academic research, in my opinion, should originate from practice, and also participate in and guide practice — especially for disciplines with strong applicability and practicality like architecture.

Over these twenty-plus years, whether undertaking research projects, graduate teaching, or disciplinary construction, I have been inseparable from practical projects large and small.

As institutions of higher learning, participation in and undertaking of practical engineering projects should be selective, and their value orientation should be the hotspots, focal points, and difficulties in China's urban and rural planning and construction; topic selection should have academic vision — not everything should be done, and especially not "seeing money and opening eyes," with remuneration as the first starting point.

For example, in the field this book belongs to, the actual situation is that all sectors, including the academic community, have constant controversies: tradition and modernity, protection and renewal, globalization and regionalism, classics and fashion, etc. — more controversial than general planning and design.

Research projects have both risks and difficulties, and these hotspots, focal points, and difficulties, I believe, are precisely the growth points where academic theory can break through.

Second, academic research and the construction of disciplinary theory are a process, dynamic and developing. Researchers themselves are constantly reshaping themselves, adjusting and optimizing their value orientations and thinking modes in the process of research and exploration.

Over twenty-plus years, I have also experienced such a psychological journey in this field. In the late 1970s, Tsinghua University was invited to plan Huangshan Scenic Area.

At the same time, to adapt to the protection and development of Huangshan Scenic Area and the development of tourism, Yungu Cableway and Yungu Villa were planned in the first round of construction. I followed my teacher Professor Wang Guoyu to undertake the design of Yungu Scenic Area and Yungu Villa.

First, we positioned it as a modern tourism building with Hui-style (Huangshan belongs to Huizhou) characteristics and traditional style; at the same time, located within the scenic area, it should also be a landscape garden-style building.

At that time, teachers, students, and collaborating engineers all successively investigated and examined Huizhou settlements and Huizhou dwellings, thus opening the door to researching traditional dwellings.

The so-called "organic unity of ecology, form, and sentiment," the duality of residential architecture, the three-level formulation from appearance to structure to philosophical concepts, the definition of the concepts of "dwelling tradition" and "traditional dwellings," etc., are all ideas formed through continuous exploration triggered by the design practice of Yungu Villa.

In 1987, when applying for the National Natural Science Foundation, the department strongly supported the project "Chinese dwellings — Human and Living Environment." During the research process, a large number of sharp contradictions encountered were the problems of protection and renewal transformation.

The project itself connected dwelling settlements with people and environment, and the intersection and integration of many social, economic, humanistic, and ecological fields became topics that academic research could not bypass.

In combination with the reconstruction of Miao villages in poor mountainous areas and the water town planning in developed areas like Shaoxing, after several repetitions, ideas such as "leaving everything as is is impossible, tearing down and rebuilding is unreasonable," "a small amount of protection, a large amount of renewal and transformation," "historical districts are historical, yet contemporary; they are districts, yet urban" and other theoretical exploration ideas emerged accordingly.

Going deeper into village and town construction inevitably involves small town issues and urban-rural integration issues — this is also the inevitable direction of fund project research from "protection and renewal of Chinese traditional dwelling Settlements" to "Urban Structural renewal Under the Background of urbanization and agricultural industrialization."

This is a logical development of academic research in terms of "time course," "spatial category," and "connotation expansion." I once said this: if I have done some work for dwellings, it is better to say that dwellings have done work for me.

The process of dwelling research changed the architect's "self," changed the closed and one-way thinking mode of "everything starting from composition"; people transform the world, and this process also reshapes people — this is absolutely true.

Third, over these twenty-plus years, I myself have never wanted to establish any theoretical system or academic framework, let alone aspire to become some "school." I only wanted to integrate myself into our era — an era full of contradictions and vitality that challenges past academic theories — and do what I can do well.

I remember many years ago, the late Professor Wang Tan said this to me: for a person to make even a little contribution in the long river of human civilization history is very worthwhile, very not easy, and very remarkable.

This sentence has deeply influenced me to this day; therefore, I often dare not praise those gentlemen with "works as tall as their bodies," who consider themselves unprecedented and unrepeatable, and who create so-and-so theories and systems from time to time.

I also think of a graduate student years ago whose thesis title was "Emptiness." The teacher asked him what it meant — was it the "emptiness" of space? He said no; then what did it mean? He couldn't explain clearly. Perhaps it was "emptiness thus contains all realms" — "emptiness" reaching this point, nothing remains.

I had a slight liking for this young man's daring to take risks, but more I felt sorrow for his state of being so metaphysical that he "couldn't find north," detached from worldly affairs.

The current era is full of vitality, but also full of traps; I sincerely hope the younger generation can clearly see the "era diseases" of this time — "bubbles," "acting as if no one else is present," and "considering oneself supreme."

In summary, "academia should move toward practice," "academic research is the process of interaction between subject and object," and "academic achievements are merely a drop in the ocean" — these are the most important experiences I insist on using this book as an example to tell readers.

If this can arouse readers' attention to the "inner journey" "beyond" these academic writings, I will be very, very gratified.

Thanks to the leaders, editors-in-charge, and art editors of China Building Materials Industry Press for their care, support, and help. In many aspects, they respected the author's almost stubborn opinions, which was not easy.

Comrades Yu Feng and Yuan Mu paid diligent efforts in the selection, illustration selection, layout, cover design, and proofreading of this book — noted here to express gratitude.

Shan Deqi

August 2004, Tsinghua Garden
